A missing English source, or an existing file other than the export's own `index.html`, fails the build. `LegacyForward` and the `[locale]` layout's forwarding branch (`app/(website)/_layout.web.tsx`) exist only for the development server, where no static file exists and an unprefixed address forwards to English. Never list an unprefixed address in the sitemap or link to one: internal links go through `useWebsiteHref()` (`components/website/useWebsiteHref.ts`, `href('/tools')` -> the public URL in the current language; `https://`, `/app` and `/open` pass through), and `components/website/__tests__/websiteHrefs.test.ts` fails on a hard-coded internal href. A page with no copy in the current language (the blog) is linked in English, so a Spanish page links to `/en/blog/…`.
- **hreflang and canonicals come from one function.** `metadataForPage` (`websiteMetadata.ts`) writes the self-referencing canonical of the page's own locale and `alternates.languages` from `hreflangAlternatePaths` (the same five-locale set plus `x-default` -> the English URL on every page of a cluster, which is what makes them reciprocal); `WebsiteSeo.tsx` emits the same `<link rel="alternate" hreflang>` on the client and `scripts/generate-web-seo-files.js` writes the same set into the sitemap as `xhtml:link`s on one `<url>` per locale. A page's `generateMetadata` is `export const generateMetadata = fixedMetadataFor('<routeKey>')`, which reads the `locale` param (`languageForLocaleParam`). Pinned by `components/website/__tests__/{localeRoutes,websiteMetadata}.test.ts` and `scripts/__tests__/generate-web-seo-files.test.ts`.
- **`app/(website)/[locale]/` has no layout, and every page exports `generateStaticParams = localeStaticParams` (`components/website/localeStaticParams.ts`; `programs/[slug]` returns the language × slug product).** Expo hands a page's `generateMetadata` and loader only the params of the page's own route: with a `[locale]` layout generating the language, `params.locale` never arrived and every Spanish page exported English titles with an English canonical. A new page under `[locale]/` therefore needs that export (and `generateMetadata = fixedMetadataFor('<key>')`); `components/website/__tests__/localeRoutes.test.ts` fails when one is missing.
- **The blog is NOT under `[locale]`: it lives in `app/(website)/en/blog/`** (a layout-less static folder, so its URLs are `/en/blog/…`). Expo runs no loader for the static pages under a generated `[locale]` layout (`blog/index`, `blog/tag/index` read `undefined` from `useLoaderData` and the export crashed), and an English-only tree under `[locale]` would be rendered once per language (about 1,200 tag pages bundled five times: the export ran out of memory). `export-web-wrapper.js` also raises Node's heap to 8 GB because the export now renders every translated page five times. **`<html lang>` is stamped after the export** (`scripts/localize-web-exports.js`; `+html.tsx` cannot see the route and renders `en`); the same script fails the build when a language's home page is missing from `dist/` (the wrapper's exit code alone does not prove the export finished) removes the export's `dist/[locale]` template pages, and removes any English-only page found under another language as a safety net. `build-android-web` runs `export-web-wrapper` -> `localize-web-exports` -> `write-unprefixed-pages` -> `flatten-web-indexes`, in that order (flatten last, so the flat copies carry the language).
- The website is a standard React web app: plain HTML elements and Tailwind/NativeWind classes, **not** React Native primitives.
- **Static SEO** comes from each route's `generateMetadata()` export using `components/website/websiteMetadata.ts` (SDK 58 does not prerender `expo-router/head`). `WebsiteSeo.tsx` updates the same metadata on the client. `WebsiteShell` provides the shared public-page chrome around every `[locale]` page.
- **The link-preview image is drawn at build time, once per language, and never committed.** `scripts/generate-seo-images.js` (`npm run generate-seo-images`, run by `web` and `build-android-web` before the export) composes `public/images/seo/<prefix>.jpg` (git-ignored, 1200×630 JPEG, about 65 KB) in the layout of the old hand-made preview: the Home capture `screenshots/google-play/phone/<language>/01-home.png`, under a drawn status bar coloured like the capture's top row and clipped to the screen's rounded corners, inside the device frame `assets/phone-wrapper.png` (its screen is transparent; the phone runs off the bottom edge with the summary card and the two big actions in view) (a language with no capture of its own uses en-US's, so the screen is English until that language is captured; recapturing with `npm run screenshots:google-play-phone` updates every preview) beside the headline, a line and four items, each with a lucide icon in a tile (`ITEM_ICONS`, read from `lucide-react-native`'s icon data), from `website.seo.image` in that language's `website.json` (the script reads those keys from the JSON, so `check-translations` lists them as unused: do not clean them up; the copy must stay true, so no "free" or "no paywall" claim now that Musclog Iron exists). Text is laid out by pango through sharp with the Manrope TTFs in `assets/fonts/manrope/` (SIL OFL) registered through a throw-away fontconfig file, so the result does not depend on the build machine's fonts; each block is measured, the column shrinks until it fits and the script stops and names the language below 70% size. `seoImageUrl(prefix)` (`websiteMetadata.ts`) is the one URL; `metadataForPage` picks it from the language the page is PUBLISHED in (an English-only page such as a blog post gets the English image), `WebsiteSeo` reads it back from that metadata, and the blog share buttons and the Organization JSON-LD logo use the English one. A new website language needs its `website.seo.image` copy (the script fails before writing anything otherwise). Pinned by `scripts/__tests__/generateSeoImages.test.ts` and `components/website/__tests__/websiteMetadata.test.ts`.
- **`components/website/websiteRoutes.json` is the single registry of fixed website routes**: `path`, optional `robots` (omit for indexable), optional `locales` (see the locale bullet above), optional `llms` blurb. `scripts/generate-web-seo-files.js` regenerates `public/robots.txt`, `public/sitemap.xml` and `public/llms.txt` on every `npm run web` / `npm run build-android-web` — those three files are generated, never hand-edited, and the route list must not be copied into the generator or `llms.txt`. Adding a fixed public route = the registry entry + its `website.seo.routes.<key>` title/description in each `lang/locales` `website.json`. Blog posts, category listings and pagination are the exception: the generator derives them from the Markdown posts through `components/website/blogRoutes.js`. The sitemap lists only public (prefixed) URLs, a translated page once per language with its `xhtml:link` alternates, and `llms.txt` links the English ones.
- **Every exported `X/index.html` also ships as a flat `X.html`** (`scripts/flatten-web-indexes.js`, run by `build-android-web`). GitHub Pages answers a directory URL with a 301 to `http://…/X/` (Cloudflare reaches the origin over http), which contradicted the slash-less https canonical and sitemap for every blog, category, tag, tools and programs index; a flat file next to the directory is served with a 200. Never link to or list a trailing-slash URL. Pinned by `scripts/__tests__/flattenWebIndexes.test.ts`.
- Sitewide `Organization`/`WebSite` JSON-LD is rendered by `WebsiteShell` via `WebsiteStructuredData.tsx`; add page-specific schema there (`FaqPageJsonLd`, `SoftwareApplicationJsonLd`, `BlogPostingJsonLd`) when content maps to a schema.org type. Never fabricate fields like `aggregateRating`.
- **Link to another website page with a plain `<a href>`, not expo-router's `Link`.** On web `Link` renders a react-native-web `Text`, which inherits neither `color` nor typography from HTML ancestors, and `hover:` classes lose to its inline `style`. **A plain anchor is also what makes the page appear at once**: it is a full load, so the exported HTML paints immediately. `Link` navigates on the client, where the route is a lazy chunk plus a loader fetch inside a Suspense boundary whose production fallback is `null` (expo-router hardcodes it in lazy mode), so the content area is blank, header and footer only, until both arrive (`docs/FIXES.md`). The header, footer, mobile menu, cookie banner and blog post links are all anchors, pinned by `components/website/__tests__/WebsiteWrapper.test.ts`. If a website link ever has to use `Link`, put every typography and color declaration on the `Link` itself (inline `color` included) and accept the blank gap.
- **Web icon fonts register during root layout rendering** (`registerWebIconFonts` in `app/_layout.tsx`). Expo SDK 57+ imports routes outside its server font context, so a module-scope call crashes static export; keep it inside the render (an effect would omit it from the server HTML).
- **The website has no decorative layers, and `components/__tests__/deslopGuards.test.ts` ("Website: no decorative layers") holds each at zero.** No dot/grid/noise backgrounds (the old `WebsiteBackgrounds` primitives are deleted, not to be recreated), blurred glow blobs, `backdrop-blur`, gradient surfaces, cast shadows, `uppercase`/`tracking-[…em]` eyebrows, weights above bold or size-named radii (`rounded-control|card|sheet` instead). Sections are separated by a hairline `border-t` and left-aligned headings; capabilities are a plain `<dl>`/`<ol>`, not a grid of matching icon cards. The homepage carries no invented proof (no stats band, no testimonial, no "thousands of users"), and cycle copy never promises phase-based training advice. **The homepage is Hero, `ScreenshotShowcase`, `FeatureGrid` (the one `id="features"` the header links to), `MeetLoggy` (a paragraph beside the mascot, the only Loggy on the page), `Themes` and `CTA`**: a row of title-plus-description columns (the old principles trio and "How it works" steps) read as a template and restated the hero, so they are gone and the privacy claim is made once, in the hero (pinned by the "homepage" test in `deslopGuards.test.ts`). The iOS link is TestFlight, not the App Store; don't write copy that says otherwise.
- **Tools, programs and comparison pages are data plus one pure function each.** `/tools/<slug>` pages (`app/(website)/tools/*.web.tsx`, native `.tsx` redirects) render `ToolLayout` around a form; the maths lives in `components/website/tools/<key>Model.ts`, which must import the app's own function from `utils/` (never re-implement a formula in a page: `components/website/tools/__tests__/toolPages.test.ts` lists the identifiers each model must import, and checks every tool has a route, SEO strings and copy in all ten locales). A tool is a registry entry (`toolsRegistry.ts`), a `websiteRoutes.json` route (`toolXxx` key), `website.seo.routes.<key>` in every `website.json`, and a `lang/locales/<locale>/websiteTool<Key>.json` copy file with the same keys in every locale. Weights are typed in kg or lb (`useToolUnits`) and converted with `displayToKg`/`kgToDisplay`; numbers are shown with `useFormatAppNumber`; inputs are the visitor's own strings parsed by `parseToolNumber`; the shared kg/lb choice and "your gym" form (`gymForm.ts`, `useToolState.ts`) are per-visitor conveniences in `localStorage` (every access try/catch, validated on read). Protein is `bodyweightProtein` (`utils/nutritionCalculator.ts`), the single g/kg rule `calculateMacros` also uses. **Programs**: `components/website/programRoutes.js` (CommonJS, read by `scripts/generate-web-seo-files.js`) maps each bundled template's `__title` to a URL slug and fixes the index order; never derive a slug from array position or a translated title. `programsModel.ts` joins `data/workoutTemplatesData.json`, the locale copy and the exercise catalogue, and `programOpenUrl` builds the same `musclog-workout-plan` document the app's import review reads (a test parses every program's link with `parseWorkoutPlanText`). **Competitor pages** (`components/website/alternatives/competitors.ts`) state only what the linked page says, dated by `checkedOn`; re-check the facts before moving the date, and a fact row needs its source link (counts are tested). **A claim that cannot be fetched and read is removed, not softened**: vendors' help and blog pages sit behind bot challenges (Hevy, MyFitnessPal), so a claim needs a verbatim quote read in a real browser, with the URL; a search summary is not a source, and a challenge page is not to be worked around by an agent (ask the person to have their own browser read it). Each fact row carries its own source, and `checkedOn` is the day the quotes were read (2026-11-14: Strong from its App Store listing, Hevy from its Pro-plan help table, MyFitnessPal from its announcement post and its barcode help page). A competitor with `sources: []` has no fact table (the component skips the table and the "checked on" line). Comparisons stay off the Play listing. **The one-rep max percentage table marks rows whose reps pass `MAX_E1RM_REPS` as extrapolated** (`OneRepMaxRow.extrapolated`, a footnote in every locale) instead of presenting them like the trusted ones. **`/exercises?q=<name>` opens the library already searching** (`exerciseSearchFromQuery`, `components/website/exerciseSearchParam.ts`, read from `window.location.search` in an effect after hydration, never a lazy state initializer, because the static HTML cannot know the query); every exercise name on `/programs/<slug>` links there through `exerciseSearchPath`, and a test pins that every catalogue name finds itself through its own link.
- **`/premium` is an unlisted (noindex, no nav link) page until launch**; at launch flip its registry `robots` off, add an `llms` blurb and link it from the burger menu and footer. Its copy is `lang/locales/*/websitePremium.json`.
- **Navigation below `lg` is the header burger menu** (`MobileMenu` in `WebsiteWrapper.web.tsx`); the footer carries only secondary/legal links at every width. A new primary destination is one edit to `MobileMenu`'s `menuLinks`. The panel is **portalled to `<body>`** (the header's `backdrop-blur-md` would otherwise clip a `fixed` backdrop), carries its **own `lg:hidden`** plus a `matchMedia('(min-width: 1024px)')` listener (otherwise growing past the breakpoint leaves body scroll locked), closes on backdrop and Escape rather than a document-level `pointerdown` (the portalled store/QR popovers count as "outside"), and moves focus in on open and back to the trigger on close.
- **Campaign attribution is first-touch and stays in the browser**: `captureCampaign` (`utils/websiteAnalytics.web.ts`, called from `AnalyticsConsent`) keeps the landing URL's `utm_source|medium|campaign` in `sessionStorage` only. Nothing is sent until the visitor accepts analytics; then `store_button_click` and `demo_start` carry the tags, and Google Play links get them as the install `referrer` at click time (`withPlayReferrer`, set on the anchor in `onClick` so server HTML and hydration agree). Tag shared links like `https://musclog.app/?utm_source=reddit&utm_medium=post&utm_campaign=launch`. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/websiteCampaign.test.ts`. **The gtag stub pushes `arguments`, never a rest-parameter array**: gtag.js silently skips arrays, which is why GA4 received nothing from March to November 2026 (`docs/FIXES.md`, pinned by `utils/__tests__/websiteAnalyticsGtag.test.ts`).
- **Website preferences**: `WebsitePreferences.tsx` owns the single header control for language and theme, with Language/Theme tabs in a portalled, viewport-constrained panel. The language tab is a list of real links (`<a href hreflang>` to the same page in each of the ten languages, Russian included) that remembers the choice in `musclog_lang` and lets the browser navigate; on a page with no translation (the blog, `/open`) it changes the chrome in place instead, closes the panel (a language choice is done, as it is when the page navigates) and returns focus to the trigger. **On the English-only blog that in-place switch also shows `EnglishOnlyNotice`** (`components/website/EnglishOnlyNotice.tsx`): the address stays `/en/blog/…` and the posts stay English, so a polite `role="status"` note reusing `website.blog.englishOnlyNotice` (in the language just chosen, with its own `lang`) appears under the header for `ENGLISH_ONLY_NOTICE_MS` (7 s) or until closed; choosing English again takes it down, and `/open` (whose copy is translated) never shows it. It sits at the TOP because the cookie banner owns the bottom of the screen. The in-place language is deliberately transient: the address wins on the next navigation, and `<html lang>` stays `en` because the posts are English. Pinned by `components/website/__tests__/WebsitePreferences.test.ts`. Theme changes update the browser mirror and app settings. Do not add separate picker pills or duplicate theme options in the burger menu.
- **A web modal root that centres through an inner wrapper takes `useWebModalLayerStyle({ variant: 'layer' })`, not `'centered'`.** `centered` puts `align-items: center` on the root: right when the root IS one dialog's backdrop (`ConfirmationModal`, `SelectModal`, `EndWorkoutModal`), wrong for `CenteredModal`, whose inner wrapper it shrank to its content so the dialog ignored the screen (`docs/FIXES.md`). **`Button.web.tsx` flex buttons keep `min-width: min-content` and auto-width ones never shrink**, so a row of buttons that can overflow takes `flex-wrap` (confirmation dialog, workout card, Add food), and a row of text plus a trailing control takes `min-w-0 flex-1` on the text and `shrink-0` on the control. A block pinned under a `scrollable={false}` area must fit the shortest screen at large text, or move into the scroll.
- **Large text and long translations are handled by wrapping, measuring or dropping decoration, in that order — never by letting a word break.** A row of text plus a control takes `min-w-0 flex-1` on the text and `shrink-0` on the control; a row that can still overflow takes `flex-wrap` with a `basis-[Npx] grow` on its halves (`flex-1` has basis 0 and never wraps). When wrapping is not enough the answer is measured, through `utils/fitText.ts` (pure, `fitLineFontSize` for a number and its unit, `labelsFitEvenly` for "do these labels share this row/grid?"), not `adjustsFontSizeToFit`, which react-native-web ignores: `StepperInput` fits its value to the measured slot, a full-width `SegmentedControl` pill splits evenly only while every label's longest word fits and otherwise sizes to its labels and wraps, `useOptionColumns` (`hooks/useOptionColumns.ts`) gives the onboarding goal cards two columns or one, and `bodyFigureScale` (`utils/bodyFigureScale.ts`, web) shrinks the front/back body figures to their container. Decorative furniture beside a label (a leading icon tile, a chevron, a sheet header icon, mood captions under their icons) gives way when `isCrampedLayout` is true (`utils/crampedLayout.ts`, `useIsCramped`: window width divided by the OS text scale, under 300pt — web and the audit narrow the viewport instead of raising `fontScale`, so a `fontScale` check alone never fires there): the label says what the decoration did, and the control keeps its accessibility label. The web charts' edge x labels shift by `X_AXIS_LABEL_EDGE_MARGIN_WEB` (a margin on a centred item moves it by HALF of itself), and `LineChart` pads its right and top by the last point's radius so the end dot is not cut in half. A footer pinned over a `scrollable={false}` body needs the body's own bottom padding (`CreateExerciseModal`); `FullScreenModal`'s scrolling body already has it. A label-plus-figure row (`WorkoutSummaryStatRow`) wraps with `flex-wrap`: the label group is `grow basis-[140px]` and the figure `ml-auto shrink-0`, so a figure that cannot sit beside its label drops to its own right-aligned line instead of running past a clipped card. A footer button row inside a modal does the same (`grow basis-[140px]` wrappers around `width="full"` buttons), and **`FullScreenModal` pads its scrolling body by the footer's measured height** (`footerClearance`, `BottomButtonWrapper`'s `onHeightChange`; never below the old 120px), because the footer floats over the body and a stacked footer covered the last rows. A `CenteredModal` with a tall body takes `scrollable` (bounded to the window, body scrolls, header and footer stay); so does `ConfirmationModal` (only its message scrolls, with the shared `useScrollFade` cue, because text running under fixed buttons reads as cut off; the Musclog Iron consent uses it, `ConfirmationModal.dom.test.tsx` pins it). **Every `FullScreenModal` that owns its scroller shows the `subtle` "more below" cue by default** (`scrollCue`, a 32pt fade and a 24pt chevron with a 44pt touch area, only while the body overflows and is not yet at its end); `scrollCue="full"` is the 80pt fade and 44pt chevron for a screen whose point is below the fold (the Iron paywall), `"none"` turns it off, and a body with `scrollable={false}` scrolls itself, so it draws its own cue (What's new) or none. The cue is drawn after the footer, so the chevron that reaches into the footer's see-through top padding stays tappable (`components/__tests__/scrollCue.test.ts`). `OptionsSelector` drops its icon-glyph tile when cramped (a photo stays); `MealSection`'s header keeps the meal name at its width and lets the totals wrap, so the menu button never leaves the screen. **Settings rows, navigation rows and table headers share that order, each through one pure function in `utils/fitText.ts`** (`WORD_GLYPH_RATIO` 0.56 is the estimate of a word's width in these rows and `NUMERAL_GLYPH_RATIO` 0.62, the default of `fitFontSize`, of a figure's; every fitting component takes its measured width and the OS text scale from `useMeasuredWidth`, and a padding or gap the fit math counts on is drawn from the same constant, never repeated as a class): a `ToggleInput` row measures itself and calls `layoutToggleRow` — the switch moves under the text when the longest word of the label or description does not fit beside it, the icon gives its room away (always while cramped), and only then does the type shrink, to 12pt; `SettingsCard` calls `layoutIconRow` (icon first, then the title down to 14pt; the chevron stays, it is the affordance); a table whose header words must stay whole gives each column `layoutTableColumns` (a `flexBasis` per column from its own longest word plus `flexGrow: 1`, shared by the header and every row, the header type shrinking to 10pt as the last step) instead of equal columns, and shows a spelled-out header such as "RIR (reps in reserve)" as its abbreviation (`workoutDetail.rirShort`) with the long text as the accessibility label. `layoutToggleRow`, `layoutIconRow` and `layoutActionLabel` are three rung lists over one picker (`pickRung`: the first layout whose room holds the longest word, the last one shrinking the type), and `insetWidth` takes a box's own padding off its measured width. The shared data-log item card (`DataLogModal` `renderItem`, behind Food, Meal, Exercise, Metric and Goal data) drops its icon tile when cramped. A row that pairs a title with a control or a time (`NotificationCard`'s action and time, the Add exercise "Create set" header, Body Metrics' range picker, the Filter workouts footer) is a `flex-row flex-wrap` whose control is `shrink-0`, so the control drops under the text instead of overlapping or clipping it. **Never baseline a word-break or overlap that one of these would fix**: the 2026-11 baseline accepted 40 modal word-breaks as "a single long word has no break point", which was this same layout failing, and they were removed once fixed.
- **Popover vertical placement is `computePopoverTop`** (`components/website/popoverPlacement.ts`), shared by `DownloadModal` and `StoreButtons`' QR popover: it flips above the trigger when there is no room below. Height is measured after the first render (it returns a downward position while `popoverHeight` is 0 and settles on the second pass) — do not measure during render. Horizontal placement stays per-component.
- **SDK 58 web document contract**: `app/+html.tsx` must render all `useServerDocumentContext()` attributes/nodes, including `bodyNodes` for hydration, merged with the existing body classes. JSON-LD uses safe direct script markup, never Helmet. Keep static output for GitHub Pages. Web layout insets come from CSS `env(safe-area-inset-*)` via `useAppSafeAreaInsets`; native keeps window insets.

**Blog:**

- Posts live recursively under `app/(website)/posts`; every file/directory segment must be URL-safe (letters, numbers, hyphens, underscores). Frontmatter requires `title`, `date` (`YYYY-MM-DD`), `category`, `tags`; `description` is optional. `category` is a lowercase kebab-case key whose label must exist at `website.blog.categories.<key>` in every `lang/locales/*/website.json`. Category labels link to `/blog/category/<key>` (later pages `/blog/category/<key>/page/<n>`; the first `/blog` page is the bare path, later ones `/blog/page/<n>`).
- Images live under `public/images/blog/` and are referenced as absolute `/images/blog/...`. Run `npm run compress-blog-images` after adding PNG/JPEG assets (caps at 1792px, writes q85 WebP only when smaller, updates Markdown references, removes the source).
- The blog routes (`blog/index.web.tsx`, `blog/page/[page].web.tsx`, `blog/[...slug].web.tsx`, `blog/category/[category]/`, `blog/tag/[tag]/`) use Expo static data loaders plus `utils/blogPosts.server.ts`; dynamic routes export `generateStaticParams`, and their unsuffixed `.tsx` siblings are native redirects. Expo Router 57+ static manifests keep the unresolved `[...slug]` template, so `loadBlogPostForRoute` must keep handling that literal build-time loader call.
- **`components/website/blogRoutes.js` owns every blog URL shape, the pagination arithmetic, the `SAFE_SLUG_SEGMENT` / `SAFE_CATEGORY_KEY` patterns and the `blogConfig.json` page size.** It is plain CommonJS because `scripts/generate-web-seo-files.js` runs outside the bundle and must read the router's rules. Never rebuild a `/blog/...` path by hand; `utils/__tests__/blogRoutes.test.ts` pins that generator and loaders agree. The page size is `BLOG_POSTS_PER_PAGE` from `blogConfig.json` — change it there only.
- **`/blog/category` is the categories index** (`blogCategories` in `websiteRoutes.json`, `blog/category/index.web.tsx`, `BlogCategoriesPage.tsx`, counts from `loadBlogCategorySummaries`; `BLOG_CATEGORIES_PATH` in `blogRoutes.js` is the path every link uses). A category listing's top-left link goes back to it (`website.blog.backToCategories`), while the all-posts listing links to it (`browseCategories`); a post's "back" link still goes to `/blog`.
- **Tags are free text with a derived URL slug, and `/blog/tag` is the tag cloud.** `blogTagSlug` (`blogRoutes.js`) lowercases, folds diacritics and hyphenates a tag, so "Open Source" and "open source" are one tag and one page; a tag with no letters or digits cannot be linked and `normalizeTags` rejects it (the sitemap generator rejects it too). Pages are `/blog/tag` (`BlogTagsPage.tsx`, `blogTags` in `websiteRoutes.json`, sized by `tagCloudLevel` in `utils/blogTagCloud.ts`: log scale, five steps, the count is also in the markup so size never carries the ranking alone), `/blog/tag/<slug>` and `/blog/tag/<slug>/page/<n>` (`blog/tag/[tag]/`), built from `getBlogTagSummaries`/`loadBlogTagPage` and rendered by `BlogListingPage` with `tag` + `tagLabel`. A tag's label is the first spelling met on the newest post, a post counts once per tag, and every tag chip on a post or card is `BlogTagChip` (never an inline `<span>`). The tag routes' `generateMetadata` is async because the label is free text only the loader knows. Never rebuild a tag URL by hand: `blogTagPath(blogTagSlug(tag))`.
- **One component renders every listing page: `BlogListingPage.tsx`** (SEO + `CollectionPage` JSON-LD + `BlogPostListing`), with an optional `category` or `tag`. `/blog` is a fixed route, so `WebsiteSeo` reads its title/description from `websiteRoutes.json`; a category supplies them from its translated label. Header and metadata copy both come from `useBlogListingCopy`.
- Post Markdown renders server-side with `markdown-it` (`html: false`) and `highlight.js`.
- **Highlighted guides on tag pages are configured in post frontmatter.** Optional `highlightOnTag: ['workout-history']` lists tag URL slugs (lowercase kebab-case). The tag loader selects matching posts from the full catalogue for the first page, in the normal newest-first order; a guide need not carry the tag itself. Highlights reuse the post title and description, do not create tags or alter archive membership, counts or pagination, and render through the generic `BlogPostListing` section. Invalid values fail frontmatter validation.
- **The index is `blog/index.web.tsx`, not `blog.web.tsx`.** Expo writes each loader payload as an extensionless file named after the route's context key, so a `blog` file and a `blog/` directory collide (`EISDIR`). Any `foo.tsx` + `foo/[x].tsx` pair that both export loaders has this problem.
- **`BlogPostShare.tsx` must pass `resetButtonStyle={false}` to every `react-share` button** — otherwise react-share's inline styles beat every Tailwind class and the buttons render as bare glyphs, with no type error. Icons are monochrome (`iconFillColor="currentColor"`) and take their brand color on hover via the `--share-color` custom property read by `.blog-share-button` in `global.css`. The shared URL comes from `absoluteUrl` in `websiteMetadata.ts` (no fourth copy of the site origin). Pinned by `components/website/__tests__/BlogPostShare.test.ts`.
- **Dev-only loader path needs `patches/expo-router+58.0.23.patch`**: `useLoaderData` passes the filesystem context key for grouped routes, so in development the client requested a path containing route groups; the patch strips `(group)` segments in development only (production keeps them). `utils/__tests__/expoRouterLoaderPatch.test.ts` pins it. Revalidate index/post navigation in both environments when upgrading Expo Router.
- **Diagrams are ` ```mermaid ` fences, drawn in the browser.** The server renders `<pre class="mermaid">` with the escaped source (readable without JS) and skips highlight.js; `BlogMermaid.tsx`, mounted by `blog/[...slug].web.tsx`, dynamically imports `mermaid` only when needed and draws with `securityLevel: 'strict'`. Colors come from the live `--c-*` custom properties, and a `MutationObserver` on `<html>`'s `style` redraws on theme change — do not hard-code a Mermaid theme.
- **Images on adjacent lines are a swipeable gallery; a blank line keeps them apart.** `utils/blogMarkdownGallery.ts` is a `markdown-it` core rule (registered in `blogPosts.server.ts`) that rewrites a top-level paragraph made only of two or more images into `.blog-gallery`: a scroll-snap `.blog-gallery-track` (a focusable, labelled `role="region"`) of `<figure>`s plus a `hidden` previous/next row. It therefore swipes, scrolls with the arrow keys and is indexed with no JavaScript (every `<img>` and its alt is in the static HTML, `loading="lazy"`). The Markdown title is the visible caption (not a tooltip); a gallery image with no alt text throws, like bad frontmatter; images in a list, a quote, a link or beside text stay ordinary. `hooks/useBlogGalleries.ts`, called by `blog/[...slug].web.tsx`, unhides the buttons, moves one image per press and disables each at its end (reduced motion jumps instead of gliding). Frames reserve `aspect-ratio: 390 / 844` because every blog screenshot is a 390×844 capture, so nothing shifts as they load. Styles are the `.blog-prose .blog-gallery*` rules in `global.css`. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/blogMarkdownGallery.test.ts` and `hooks/__tests__/useBlogGalleries.test.ts`.
- **`utils/blogPosts.server.ts` (and `mermaid`) are stubbed out of the native bundle in `metro.config.js`** (`blog-posts-server-stub.js`, `mermaid-stub.js`, resolved whenever `platform !== 'web'`, like `sharp`). expo-router's native `require.context` regex matches `.web.tsx` files, and Metro resolves `import()` statically, so the module's `node:fs/promises` import would otherwise fail the native bundle. The stub throws rather than returning empty posts. **Do not "fix" this with a `blogPosts.server.native.ts` sibling** — Jest's `node` project has `native` in `haste.platforms`, so it would hijack the real parser's tests. Any new web-only module importing Node built-ins needs the same treatment. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/blogPostsServerNativeStub.test.ts`.

## Tech Stack

- **Framework**: React Native (Expo SDK 58) with Expo Router
- **Android release optimization**: `app.json` enables both `enableMinifyInReleaseBuilds` and `enableShrinkResourcesInReleaseBuilds` through `expo-build-properties` (shrinking requires minification; keep both). `android/gradle.properties` is the generated mirror for builds that skip Prebuild; resync it through Prebuild whenever the Expo config changes.
- **llama.rn packages only the CPU variants a phone can use** (`plugins/withLlamaRn.js`, mirrored in `android/app/build.gradle`): llama.rn ships llama.cpp once per ARM CPU generation and its loader falls through to the next one when a file is missing, so the plugin excludes `v8_2`, `v8_2_i8mm` and `v8_2_dotprod_i8mm_hexagon_opencl` (plus their JNI wrappers) and disables `syncRNLlamaHtpAssets`, which saved about 13 MB per arm64 download (2.18.0 had grown 31 MB). Every 64-bit ARM phone still runs at least plain `v8`. The Hexagon/OpenCL build is safe to drop only while Android loads models with `n_gpu_layers: 0` (`services/localModels/llamaRuntime.ts`): turning on Android offload means bringing that variant and its assets back. On a `llama.rn` upgrade, `plugins/__tests__/withLlamaRn.test.ts` fails if a file, a CMake target, the task or the loader's fallback changed name. Downloading the libraries with the model is not an option: Play forbids executable code from anywhere but Play (the allowed route is an on-demand Play Feature Delivery module), and Play cannot target bundles by CPU feature (`docs/FIXES.md`).
- **EAS upload archives**: the committed `android/` and `ios/` source projects must stay in EAS uploads. `.easignore` excludes only generated build directories/caches, the exact `.git` entry (`.git/` alone does not remove the shallow clone EAS creates), tests, local tooling, `public/`, website-only posts and runtime-fetched exercise images. Validate with `eas build:inspect --platform android --stage archive`.
- **Node version**: EAS reads neither `.nvmrc` nor `engines`, so `eas.json`'s `base` profile pins an exact Node release and every build profile must `extend` it. Bump the Node major in `engines`, `.nvmrc`, `eas.json`, `.github/workflows/*` and `.github/actions/*/action.yml` together (`utils/__tests__/easNodeVersion.test.ts` enforces it). Keep `cli.version` a major-only floor (`>= N.0.0`): the EAS builder's own `eas-cli` lags npm. See `docs/FIXES.md`.
- **Styling**: NativeWind (Tailwind CSS). Do not downgrade `react-native-css-interop` below 0.2.7 (0.2.6 crashed native builds on a literal `box-shadow` in `global.css`); `utils/__tests__/nativewindBoxShadowPatch.test.ts` pins it.
- **The app imports no `.svg`, and there is deliberately no Metro SVG transformer** (it would replace the Babel transformer for the whole bundle for a feature nothing uses; add one with its first consumer). `svgo` is a direct devDependency because `scripts/generate-loggy-animated.js` runs it. **Loggy's poses are the SVGs in `assets/avatars/loggy-poses/`**, but the app no longer imports them: `LOGGY_POSES` in `components/loggy/animated/loggyPoses.ts` (written by the generator) holds each pose's generated component (`components/loggy/animated/Loggy<Pose>Animated.tsx`) and its proportions come from the component itself (`size`, the `viewBox` width and height the generator writes), and the still is drawn from that same component with no clock; there is no PNG copy. The SVGs stay as generated artifacts (the website's source, the palette test, a designer's reference). **Those SVGs, and the animated ones, are GENERATED from one library of parts — never edit them by hand.** `assets/avatars/loggy-parts/` is the source: one file per part, `<id>/<variant>.svg` (`leg-l/standing`, `log/thinking`, `mug/coffee`; a part is a single `<g id>` and keeps nested groups such as a foot or a held pencil), `faces/<name>.svg` (50 faces; `faces/default.svg` is `<g id="face">`), `palette.css` (the one style sheet: the `.s0`–`.s21` classes every part uses), `animations/<pose>.css` (the keyframes) and `poses.json` (per pose: `viewBox`, `face`, `alternateFaces`, `layers` bottom to top, and an `animated` block). `npm run generate-loggy` (`scripts/generate-loggy-svgs.js`, then `generate-loggy-animated.js`) composes every output, so a change to one face, the torso (`log`/`leaf`/`sweatband`) or a colour reaches every pose that stacks it. A pose lists parts by `"<id>/<variant>"`; the torso is `{ "group": "body", "children": [...] }` with `"@face"` where its face block goes. `alternateFaces: true` keeps all 50 faces in the file, the others `display="none"` (`celebrating`, `coffee`, `default`, `eating`, `lifting`, `nutrition`, `panic`, `pointing`, `reading`, `running`, `salad`, `shrug`, `trophy` and `wave` do today; of the animated twins only `default` does) and `animated.upper` names the contiguous top-level layers the keyframes move as `#upper`. **A part is art only**: just `<g id>` and `<path class d>`, colours by palette class (an inline `fill`, `style`, `display` or unknown class fails the build with the file named), and whether a part is hidden is the pose's decision. Variants are named for the pose that owns them unless shared (`standing` legs, `hip` arm and hand); a part no pose stacks yet (`arm-l/pointing`, `hand-l/pointing`) is a normal library entry. Where the log's edge would show wrongly (its side knot in the gap under an arm), a `log-side/<pose>` patch goes after `log/default` in the body group (`writing`, `drinking`), so the pose keeps following edits to the torso; don't fork a `log/<pose>` for it. Animation-only wrapper groups live inside the canonical part (`steam` in the mug, `pupil-l`/`pupil-r` in the panicked eyes, `tongue` in the focused mouth), so a still carries them as plain unstyled groups. Class numbers are one global table because `generate-loggy-animated.js` reads `s<number>` and `loggyPose.test.ts` requires every animated `<path>` to be byte-identical to its still's. Pinned by `components/__tests__/loggyPartsGenerated.test.ts` (outputs fresh, every part parses, an edit to a face, the torso or the palette reaches every pose that uses it, and the refusals). Adding a pose = its parts, a `poses.json` entry, `animations/<pose>.css`, `npm run generate-loggy`, then the registry gets the pose's line by itself, and `components/__tests__/loggyPose.test.ts` fails if a pose has no component in it. The path data that more than one pose draws is written once to `components/loggy/animated/loggyPaths.ts` and referenced by name (`LOGGY_PATHS.p<hash>`), so the generated components do not each carry the log, legs and arms; a path only one pose draws stays inline. **An SVG that animates through CSS `@keyframes` or SMIL cannot be drawn by importing it**: react-native-svg runs neither, so the file would render as a still. `assets/avatars/loggy-animated/` holds exactly those, one per pose: the still's parts (path for path, no raster; most drop the hidden face variants) with its layers moved by keyframes from `loggy-parts/animations/<pose>.css`. Each keeps its still's proportions, opens on the still's exact frame (so a loop start and reduced motion both show the drawn pose) and stays inside its `viewBox`; a pivot sits on the join it moves, or the ink splits there. A part that hinges on its own must carry its own outline: the wave's `hand-r` holds the hand's ink (the still once kept it in `arm-r`, so the hand cracked off at the wrist), and `arm-r` runs on under the palm, hidden at rest, so a tilted hand only uncovers more arm. **They are wired in through a second generator, never imported**: `npm run generate-loggy-animated` (`scripts/generate-loggy-animated.js`, which `generate-loggy` runs after the SVGs) reads each file's CSS and writes `components/loggy/animated/Loggy<Pose>Animated.tsx` (committed; svgo merges paths, hidden variants are dropped, every group a keyframe rule names becomes a `LoggyLayer`, and the rules become a `LoggyAnimation` data table). `utils/loggyAnimation.ts` (pure, worklet-safe) plays that table back the way CSS does: per-interval cubic-bezier easing, the transform functions composed in the order written, wrapped in the layer's `transform-origin`; `LoggyLayer` puts the result on an `Animated.G` (a `matrix` prop natively, a `transform` string on web, where react-native-svg has no `matrix`), and `useLoggyClock` is the one linear clock per pose (a layer takes the remainder against its own duration, so every layer's duration must divide the longest; the generator refuses otherwise). `LoggyPose` hands the animated twin a clock, or none: **without a clock every `LoggyLayer` is a plain group, which is the drawn pose** (a layer's keyframes open and close on the identity frame, so the still needs no second drawing and no SVG import), and that is what he is drawn as under `useReducedMotion()` and with "Animated Loggy" off. **His three greens follow the theme, and nothing else about him does.** `palette.css` writes the greens (`s1` body, `s3` deep, `s4` body with outline, `s7` shade) as `var(--loggy-<role>, <classic hex>)`, so any renderer that ignores themes draws the original; `generate-loggy-animated.js` turns that value into the prop `{colors.body|deep|shade}` (and refuses any other `var()`), every generated `Art` takes a `colors` prop, and `LoggyPose` supplies it from `useLoggyColors(appearance)` (`components/loggy/useLoggyColors.ts`: the active theme id through `useThemeIdOrNull`, classic outside a `ThemeProvider`, so a test that renders one component needs no provider). **What each role is in each theme is one table, `LOGGY_COLORS_BY_THEME` in `constants/loggyTheme.js`** (plain CommonJS so the generators and the website sync read it: the default dark theme is `LOGGY_CLASSIC`, the artwork Loggy was drawn in; the others were found by moving the greens to the theme's brand hue at Loggy's own lightness, then checked on the theme's backgrounds, and light brands such as Volt and Scope lift all three or he turns to mustard). **A new theme needs an entry**: `constants/__tests__/loggyTheme.test.ts` fails without one and checks his body against his ink outline and the theme's surfaces. **A green that is not Loggy must not use the themed classes**: the salad's lettuce and fork leaf are fixed `s19`–`s21`, or the salad turns pink in a pink theme; a new part that draws food, plants or props that must keep their colour takes a fixed class, and the test pins that no other class is themed. `<LoggyPose appearance="classic">` pins the original green for a surface that takes its colours from him instead of the theme: the year story's posters (`posterKit.tsx`) and `YearShareCard`, whose `POSTER_GROUNDS` would swallow a pink log. The `/app/test/loggy` screen shows wave, lifting and salad in every theme through `ThemeScope`. **Whether he moves is the `animated_loggy` setting** (`SETTINGS.animatedLoggy`, default on, Visual settings → Loggy; a plain `SettingsState` primitive): `LoggyPose`'s `animated` prop is optional and left out it follows the setting, while `true`/`false` force it (the `/app/test/loggy` still-beside-animated comparison, and `YearShareCard`, whose captured image is a still). **Whether he appears at all is the `show_loggy` setting** (`SETTINGS.showLoggy`, default on, same section; the animation toggle is offered only while it is on): with it off `LoggyPose` and `LoggyCameo` render nothing (only `/app/test/loggy` forces `visible`). A wrapper that would leave a gap (margin, positioned box) or that replaces something (`EmptyStateCard` falls back to its icon, `BesideLoggy` takes the poster's full width) reads `useLoggyVisible()` (exported from `LoggyPose.tsx`); a new placement must work with him hidden. The coach avatar (`utils/coachPersonaAvatar.ts`) is not a pose and is unaffected. Pinned by `hooks/__tests__/{loggyVisibility,loggyCameo,useLoggyColors}.test.ts` and `constants/__tests__/loggyTheme.test.ts`. **Edit the part (or its `animations/<pose>.css`), then `npm run generate-loggy`**: `components/__tests__/loggyAnimatedGenerated.test.ts` fails on a stale component and checks every layer opens and closes on the identity frame. The generator rejects what the sampler does not play (a transform function it lacks, keyframes that change function lists, a missing 0%/100%). `/app/test/loggy` shows every pose still beside animated. **`assets/avatars/index.html` is the offline catalogue** (open it from disk: every pose still and animated, any face on any pose, layers to hide, every part zoomed to its drawing, the palette, every theme's greens or your own, pause, speed and scrub): `scripts/generate-loggy-gallery.js` builds it with the pose generator's own `buildStill`/`buildAnimated` plus `scripts/loggy-gallery/gallery.{js,css}` (each SVG in its own shadow root, motion through `getAnimations()`), `npm run generate-loggy` rewrites it last (`generate-loggy-gallery` alone), and it is git-ignored. Verified in a browser on web; the native `matrix` path is not yet exercised on a device. **The public website draws him as an SVG file, never through `LoggyPose`** (which compiles every pose into react-native-svg and reads the app's settings): `components/website/WebsiteLoggy.tsx` renders an element whose CSS background is `public/images/loggy/<palette>/loggy-<pose>.svg`, a copy of the animated SVG that a browser animates by itself. **The copy is per palette because an image cannot read the page's theme**: `scripts/sync-website-loggy.js` (run at the end of `npm run generate-loggy`; `WEBSITE_POSES` there is the one list of website poses: the same run writes `components/website/websiteLoggyPoses.json`, each pose's `viewBox` size, which `websiteLoggyAssets.ts` reads, and the copies are generated, never edited) writes each pose once per distinct set of greens (`applyLoggyColors` swaps the `var(--loggy-…)` fills; `loggyPaletteId` files themes drawn alike under the first of them, so Depth and Light share a directory) together with `loggy-<pose>-still.svg`, the same file plus `* { animation: none !important; }` (not the generated still, which keeps fifty faces and is five times the size). **Which copy a page shows is a stylesheet's decision** (`websiteLoggyCss`, rendered into `<head>` by `app/+html.tsx`; pure, in the assets module because `+html.tsx` runs in Node and must not import the settings stack): the default rule is the classic green and every other palette overrides it under `html[data-theme-id="…"]`, which the theme prepaint script sets from the mirror before the body renders and `ThemeProvider` keeps current. Never swap the file from JS after hydration (it flashes the default green in exactly the theme the visitor picked to escape it), and a new themed rule must be repeated inside the reduced-motion block (a themed rule outside it outranks the block's base rule on specificity). **Reduced motion is the browser's decision**: the same stylesheet selects the still under `(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)`, so those visitors never fetch the animation. `WebsiteLoggy` is a decorative `<span>` (aria-hidden, no `<img>`, no `eager` prop: a background is fetched when its element renders) that reserves its box with an inline `aspect-ratio` from the SVG's `viewBox` (`WEBSITE_LOGGY_VIEWBOX`), so every caller's `h-36 w-auto` or `w-40 h-auto` resolves the other side and placement classes go on it. It honours `show_loggy` (drawn while settings load so hydration matches the static HTML, `useWebsiteLoggyVisible`) and a stand-in falls back to its icon; the "Animated Loggy" setting does not apply. A new website spot is a design decision: add it to `MAY_RENDER_WEBSITE_LOGGY` in `components/__tests__/loggyPlacement.test.ts` with a reason and to docs/DESIGN.md → Website; a new pose is added to both lists and published with `npm run sync-website-loggy`. Pinned by `components/website/__tests__/websiteLoggy.test.ts`.
- **Database**: WatermelonDB (SQLite for Native, LokiJS for Web)
- **Charts**: Victory Native (Skia) for Native, Victory (SVG) for Web
- **AI**: OpenAI & Google Gemini (Generative AI)
- **Localization**: i18next with `expo-localization`
- **Health Data**: Health Connect (Android) via `react-native-health-connect`; HealthKit on iOS via `@kingstinct/react-native-healthkit`. **Do not add `expo-health-connect` back**: both packages register the `expo.modules.healthconnect` namespace and release builds fail `:app:mergeDexRelease` with a duplicate `BuildConfig`. `react-native-health-connect`'s own `app.plugin.js` already writes the manifest entries.
- **Error Tracking**: Sentry (`@sentry/react-native`). Error and event messages are cleaned by `scrubErrorText` in `beforeSend` (keys, emails, query strings, quoted free text). A development build sends nothing (`enabled` in `sentry-init.ts`; set `EXPO_PUBLIC_SENTRY_IN_DEV=1` to send from one), because hot reloads alone filed crashes no person can meet. A person's report of an AI reply is NOT sent through the SDK: `sendAiReplyReport` posts one feedback envelope straight to the DSN after a confirmation, whether or not crash reporting is on (the privacy page says so). **Crash reporting runs from launch while "Anonymous bug report" is on** (default on; off sends nothing, the privacy policy promises that): `app/_layout.tsx` calls `installGracefulFatalHandler()` and then `startCrashReportingAtLaunch()` at module load, in that order, and `SettingsService.getAnonymousBugReport`/`setAnonymousBugReport` apply the stored value through `syncCrashReporting` (`utils/crashReporting.ts`), which starts or stops Sentry and refreshes the device-local copy that the next launch reads before the database. The public website keeps starting Sentry on its first report only. Rules that are easy to break: (1) **never `Sentry.ErrorBoundary`** (or `withErrorBoundary`): its `componentDidCatch` assigns `error.cause`, vision-camera's `CameraError` has a getter-only `cause`, and the boundary threw and closed the app; the root boundary is `AppErrorBoundary`, which never writes to an error and never lets reporting throw (guard in `components/__tests__/AppErrorBoundary.dom.test.tsx`); (2) a fatal JS error in a release build goes to `FatalErrorOverlay` (restart) instead of React Native's handler, through `createGracefulFatalHandler` (`utils/fatalErrorStore.ts`), which Sentry's handler wraps because it is installed first, so Sentry reports and flushes before the screen shows; the overlay is drawn at the root AND inside `components/theme/Modal` (a native modal is its own window), so keep it light; (3) a native module must not send an event once its `OnDestroy` has started (the event runs after the module registry is gone and the throw kills the process): `WitMotionBleModule` sends every event through `emit`; (4) **the WitMotion sensor module sleeps until a screen uses it**: Expo creates every native module object at launch (expo-modules-core has no lazy registration), so the module itself must do nothing until asked. The JS client (`modules/witmotion-ble/src/client.ts`) fetches the native module only on the first subscription or action (`wake`; `stopScan` and `disconnect` never wake it), a screen that is merely mounted passes `useWitMotion({ enabled })` (the Settings sensors modal and the preview while hidden, a live workout with no saved sensor, which reads the saved list from the database instead), and iOS builds its manager on the first scan or connect. Pinned by `hooks/__tests__/useWitMotionLazy.test.ts` and `hooks/__tests__/useSessionBleTracking.test.ts`. Not covered: a native crash in the first second, before the JS bundle has started Sentry. See `docs/FIXES.md`.
- **Food Scanning**: OCR (tesseract.js / rn-mlkit-ocr) and barcode scanning (quagga2 / react-zxing)
- **Camera**: all native camera work goes through `react-native-vision-camera` — the smart camera via the `components/CameraView.tsx` wrapper, video capture on `app/app/test/reps-recording.tsx` via `Camera` directly. **Do not add `expo-camera` (or any second CameraX-based library)**: a newer CameraX wins Gradle resolution and vision-camera crashes at boot with `NoClassDefFoundError`. Two `CameraView.tsx` props are load-bearing for shutter latency and must not be "optimized" away without re-verifying on a **release** build (`npm run build-android-apk-local` + `adb logcat`, filter `CameraView`/`CameraCaptureFlow`; dev builds never reproduced the 25s-shutter incident): `androidPreviewViewType="texture-view"` (the snapshot shutter needs a reliable `getBitmap`; SurfaceView/PixelCopy silently reroutes into the slow takePhoto fallback) and `photoQualityBalance="speed"` (never `"balanced"`, which is CameraX ZSL and stalls for tens of seconds on some devices). Slow or fallback shots surface as Sentry `camera-shutter-*`/`camera-warm-up-*` events.
- **VisionCamera is stubbed on web** in `metro.config.js` (`vision-camera-web-stub.js`): the package throws at module evaluation, and expo-router's `require.context` can reach native-only `.tsx` siblings of `.web.tsx` files. Web camera UI lives in `CameraView.web.tsx` and `.web` route stubs; its ref must keep `takePictureAsync` (`utils/webCameraCapture.ts` grabs the preview frame; without it the shutter did nothing on web); never import vision-camera from a web-reachable module expecting it to work.
- **Every camera surface picks its device through `useUsableCameraDevice(position)` (`components/CameraView.tsx`), never vision-camera's `useCameraDevice`**: `usableCameraDevices` (`utils/cameraAvailability.ts`, pure) keeps the cameras with at least one format, drops auxiliary sensors (largest photo under `MIN_PHOTO_PIXEL_SHARE` of the best one facing that way) and sorts by resolution, then vision-camera's `getCameraDevice` chooses. Its scoring alone opened a 2 MP macro camera with no formats on a Poco X3 (the ROM misreports the main sensor's size), and `useCameraFormat` threw. **A camera failure stays in the camera area**: `CameraView` and `OpticalScannerCamera` render inside `CameraErrorBoundary`, rethrow vision-camera's `onError` into it, report through `reportCameraError` and show `CameraUnavailable` with Try again (`camera.unavailable.*`); no usable camera shows it without Try again. `docs/FIXES.md` has the incident.
- **Camera call-to-actions are shown only while `useHasCamera()` (`components/CameraView.tsx`, beside `useCameraPermissions`, so vision-camera has one wrapper) is true.** The manifest declares the camera `required="false"` (`plugins/withOptionalHardwareFeatures.js`), so Play installs Musclog on devices without one (some tablets, desktops, XR headsets, the iOS simulator). The hook reads vision-camera's device list (`Camera.getAvailableCameraDevices()`, reactive to a USB camera) through `readHasCamera` (`utils/cameraAvailability.ts`; only a camera with a format counts), **fails open** (an unreadable list means "has a camera") and is `true` on web (`CameraView.web.tsx`; the browser only learns of a missing camera when the page asks). It is hardware, not permission. **A camera button renders iff its handler is supplied** (the `SmartCameraShell` shutter rule): the leaves `BarcodeInput` (`onScanPress`), `FoodQuickActions` (`onScanBarcode`), `AddFoodModal` (`onScanBarcodePress`, `onAiCameraPress`) and `FoodSearchModal` (`onBarcodeScanPress`) never read the hook, and the owner that builds the handler passes it only while `useHasCamera()` is true (the one source; `SmartCameraContext` does not re-export it). The form scanner gates itself: `useBarcodeCameraModal` returns `openScanner` as `undefined` without a camera, so its callers pass it straight through. Gated today: the navigation bar's camera button (the bar then has four tabs), the home `scan_barcode`/`ai_photo` actions (`HomeActionContext.hasCamera`), `FoodQuickActions`' scan button, `AddFoodModal`'s barcode and AI camera methods, the barcode icon in `BarcodeInput`, `FoodSearchModal` and `AddFoodItemToMealModal`, the "receive" entries of optical transfer (`OpticalTransferModal`, the landing screen, My Meals' import-from-phone), and the Comeback tips (`comebackRecommendations` takes `hasCamera`). `SmartCameraContext.openCamera` is the backstop and does nothing without a camera (widget taps, old shortcuts). **A new button that opens the camera follows the same rule**; `components/__tests__/cameraCallToActionGuards.test.ts` pins the current ones at source level, on whitespace-collapsed text. Gallery pick is not a camera and stays. Not covered: Bluetooth and location equivalents of the same manifest change.
- **Every capture is cropped; barcode scanning has no shutter.** Both entry points of `useCameraCaptureFlow` (shutter and gallery pick) end in `cropAndProcess`, so `process` only receives a cropped image. There is deliberately no "skip the crop" flag (do not reintroduce `cropOnShutter`). Whether a shutter exists is the owner's call: pass or omit `SmartCameraShell`'s `onShutterPress` (`SmartCameraModal` passes `undefined` while `isBarcodeScanning`; `BarcodeCameraModal` never passes one) — the shell must not decide from `cameraMode`. `patches/@bsky.app+expo-image-crop-tool+0.5.1.patch` moves `openCropperAsync` to `Queues.MAIN`; keep it as defense in depth.
- **SecureStore must not block Expo's single module queue (critical).** Every Expo `AsyncFunction`/`Coroutine` on the default queue shares ONE thread (`expo.modules.AsyncFunctionQueue`). `expo-secure-store`'s Android `getValueWithKeyAsync`/`setValueWithKeyAsync` did their Keystore work directly on it, so a cold first access at boot head-of-line-blocked `openCropperAsync` and `launchImageLibraryAsync` — the first gallery pick after boot stalled ~25s (release builds only; Pixel 10 / Android 17). **Fix**: `patches/expo-secure-store+58.0.2.patch` moves those ops onto a dedicated serial queue (`Dispatchers.IO.limitedParallelism(1)`). SecureStore ships as a prebuilt AAR, so the patched `.kt` is ignored unless `package.json` keeps `expo.autolinking.android.buildFromSource: ["expo-secure-store"]` — both pieces are load-bearing; revalidate on every Expo/SecureStore upgrade. Related: `getEncryptionKey()` (`utils/encryption.ts`) coalesces concurrent reads per key (also fixing a fresh-install race that could persist mismatched keys), and `SettingsService.migrateApiKeysToEncrypted` is a `runOnce` boot migration. Do not reintroduce eager duplicate SecureStore reads at startup. Verify with a release build and the `gallery picker` phase timing in `CameraCaptureFlow` logs.
- **Gallery pick uses the system Photo Picker, no permission prompt — one shared entry point.** `utils/galleryImagePicker.ts` exports `pickImageFromGallery` (`ImagePicker.launchImageLibraryAsync` with no `legacy` flag; Android `ACTION_PICK_IMAGES` / iOS `PHPicker` need **no** media-library permission, so never add `requestMediaLibraryPermissionsAsync` back) and `pickAndCropImageFromGallery` (pick + `openCropperAsync` from `utils/file.ts`; `null` if either is cancelled). `legacy: true` was a failed workaround for the stall above and must not return. There is deliberately no `.web.ts` counterpart (expo-image-picker already handles web). **The pick runs at `quality: 1` and takes no quality argument**: every caller re-encodes through `openCropperAsync`, and compressing twice stacks a second lossy pass; the crop step alone chooses output quality (`pickAndCropImageFromGallery(compressImageQuality)`, `useCameraCaptureFlow`'s `quality`), which matters most for OCR/barcode legibility. `CoachModal`, `CreateMealModal`, `CreateCustomFoodModal` and `CreateExerciseModal` call `pickAndCropImageFromGallery`; `useCameraCaptureFlow` calls the two steps separately for per-step timing logs. Never call `ImagePicker.launchImageLibraryAsync` from a new call site.
- **Export**: xlsx + html-to-image for data export. `expo-print` renders the cycle appointment summary to a PDF (print dialog on web); it is native, so adding or upgrading it needs `npm run prebuild` (and `pod install` on iOS).
- **JSX transform**: SDK 58 uses Expo's resolved Babel JSX transform; the old `@babel/plugin-transform-react-jsx` pin is removed. Keep NativeWind, Reanimated and WatermelonDB decorator transforms in the existing Babel configuration.
- **SDK 58 file I/O**: BLE file serialization uses `writeSync` and `readBytesSync` to preserve ordering and complete JSON before sharing (`write` and `FileHandle.readBytes` now return promises); do not replace synchronous calls with un-awaited asynchronous ones. React Native refs use `ViewInstance`, `ScrollViewInstance`, `TextInputInstance`.
- **SDK 58 native compilation** also requires `patches/@nozbe+watermelondb+0.28.0.patch` (Android BuildConfig generation under AGP 9; an iOS `WMDatabaseJSIBridge` TurboModule installing the SQLite JSI bindings, with the legacy `RCTCxxBridge` installer guarded when its header is absent). The adapter and database format are unchanged; remove only once upstream ships equivalent support.
- **iOS privacy manifest and purpose strings live in `app.json`, never in `ios/`.** `expo.ios.privacyManifests` (no tracking, the required-reason API categories React Native and Expo use, collected data types all unlinked and non-tracking) is what prebuild writes to `ios/MusclogLiftLogRepeat/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy`; a missing app-level manifest fails App Store upload (ITMS-91053). Every `*UsageDescription` says what Musclog does with the permission (never "Allow $(PRODUCT_NAME) to …", never `$(BUNDLE_ID)`, which shows the bundle id), and the microphone is asked for only by hold-to-talk voice input (`expo-speech-recognition`'s `microphonePermission` and `speechRecognitionPermission`; `expo-image-picker`'s `microphonePermission` carries the same string, because `false` there deletes the key and blocks `RECORD_AUDIO`; vision-camera still records without audio). Keep the App Store Connect privacy labels in step with the manifest. The Premium types are Purchase History and User ID (both Analytics + App Functionality, per RevenueCat's App Privacy guide) and Device ID; **the committed `ios/…/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` is generated from `app.json`, so a change to `privacyManifests` is followed by `npm run prebuild:ios` (keep only that file, `git ls-files -d android ios | xargs git checkout --`, discard the rest)**: `iosPrivacyConfig.test.ts` fails when they drift. Regenerate with `npm run prebuild:ios` (`--no-install` works on Linux). Pinned by `utils/__tests__/iosPrivacyConfig.test.ts`.
- **Widget versioning**: regenerate native projects after release bumps so the widget extension's Info.plist and Xcode version/build settings match the app; run `check-version-sync` after prebuild.

## Coding Guidelines

### Architecture

- **Blank lines after blocks/functions**: ESLint's autofixable `padding-line-between-statements` requires an empty line before the next statement after a block-like statement, an `if`, or a function declaration. It does not pad inside `if`/`else` chains or before a closing brace. Run `npm run lint:eslint-fix`. (Core rule in pinned ESLint 9; migrate to ESLint Stylistic before ESLint 11.)
- **No barrel files or re-export-only wrappers**: import implementations from their owning files. No `index.ts` aggregators, no files that only re-export, no platform wrappers that merely alias a shared component — unless an external package or framework requires that exact entry point (document it in the file). Mock services by their own path in tests (`jest.mock('@/database/services', …)` now mocks nothing). Generate ids with `randomUUID` from `expo-crypto`, never a hand-rolled `Math.random()` v4.
- **Keep documentation in sync (required)**: when a change alters behavior, architecture, file layout or an invariant, update the relevant document in the same change. Project Markdown lives in `docs/`; only `AGENTS.md` and `README.md` stay at the repo root, because agent tooling and GitHub look for them there, so never add another document to the root. The set: `AGENTS.md` (implementation rules), `docs/CURRENT_FEATURES.md` / `docs/FUTURE_FEATURES.md` (product status), `docs/DESIGN.md` (UI standards), `docs/FIXES.md` (incidents and workarounds), `docs/ONLINE_PRESENCE.md` (search and store research tooling and its change ledger), `docs/RESEARCH.md` (durable rationale), `docs/THINKING_LEVELS.md` (plain-words map of what each thinking level does to every LLM job and of everything else around an LLM request: provider order, retries, reply parsing and repair, the checks code makes, photos, chat context, streaming, the other jobs that call a model, known gaps; update it whenever `THINKING_PIPELINE`, `AI_TASK_EFFORT`, a job's passes, `withLlmRetry`, a `ProviderPolicy` or the reply-reading ladder change), `README.md` (project-level usage). A stale filename, function, flag or approach is a bug. Do not create another Markdown plan or one-off report. Exception: `doc/redesign/` holds the active de-slop plan (`DESLOP_PLAN.md`) and audit baseline (`design-baseline.json`) — work a phase from there, mark its findings `fixed` in the baseline in the same change, and fold the resulting rules into `docs/DESIGN.md`. `NEW_LEARNINGS_PLAN.md` is the other active plan (features learned from another app, reimplemented, never copied); update its status table as phases ship and delete it when they are done. `docs/AR_PORTION_SCALE_PLAN.md` is a third (measuring portions with ARKit on iOS): same rules, and delete this sentence with it. `docs/WEBSITE_GROWTH_PLAN.md` is a fourth (lifter-facing web tools and blog posts): same rules, and delete this sentence with it. `docs/SHARING_PLAN.md` is a fifth (sharing workouts and meals as serverless links and images): same rules, and delete this sentence with it. `docs/ON_DEVICE_AI_PLAN.md` is a sixth (on-device AI and the AI onboarding step): same rules, and delete this sentence with it. Any user-facing feature added, removed or significantly changed also updates `docs/CURRENT_FEATURES.md` (and `README.md` if the curated overview is affected).
- **Tests are required for every behavior change**: add or update automated tests so they would catch the bug or regression, and run the relevant suite plus type checking and any applicable lint/translation checks before calling the work done. Never weaken, delete or skip a valid test to make a change pass.
- **Ages, calorie safety and what must be said** (legal, not style): Musclog is for 16+ (`MINIMUM_APP_AGE`), **stated, never gated in the app, and the birthday is optional** (`users.date_of_birth` is nullable): onboarding, the AI setup and the profile editors accept any plausible date, tell a person under 16 (`BirthdayNotes`, the AI review's `underMinimumAge` warning) and let them continue. Never invent a birthday: fields start empty, can be cleared, and an unanswered calendar opens on today, which `asBirthday` (`utils/ageGate.ts`, under `MINIMUM_PLAUSIBLE_AGE` 5 is no answer, also the AI contract's `AGE_MIN`) reads as none. Calculations use `ageForCalculations` / `User.getAge()`, which assume `ASSUMED_AGE` (30, named in the birthday helper text) without one, so **check `dateOfBirth` through `asBirthday` before showing or sending an age as the person's own**. Only the 18+ cloud AI rule (`isUnderCloudAiAge`) acts on a stored birthday, and only the website calculator plans nothing below 16. The Terms (`website.terms.sections.eligibility`) and privacy page state both ages. `calculateNutritionPlan` plans no deficit under BMI 18.5; `goalSafetyWarnings` (`utils/nutritionSafety.ts`) warns on the goals screen about a goal below the minimum, a cut while underweight and an underweight target; a generated meal plan never aims below the minimum unless the person turned it off; a starting weight with no history never uses an advanced factor. `HealthDisclaimer` sits wherever calories or loads are worked out. Play descriptions in all ten locales must keep the "not a medical device" paragraph (Play health policy). A restore wipes nothing unless the safety copy was made (`createPreRestoreBackup` throws).
- **Layered structure**: Schema (`database/schema.ts`) → Models (`database/models/`) → Repositories (`database/repositories/`, for complex/non-trivial reads) → Services (`database/services/`). Non-database services (AI, notifications, Health Connect sync) live in top-level `services/`. Models must be registered in `database/database-instance.ts`.
- **Calendar day keys**: fields representing a calendar day (`user_metrics.date`, diary pickers) store **local** midnight ms via `utils/calendarDate.ts` (`localDayStartMs`, `localDayHalfOpenRange`, …); never `Date.UTC` or `setUTCHours(0, …)` for those.
- **`nutrition_logs.date` is a consumed datetime**, not a day key: local day + time-of-day in ms. Calendar-day grouping goes through `utcNormalizedDayKey` / `dayKeyRange` (with `dayRangeClauses` from `database/dayKeyQuery.ts` for the widened DB clauses and `range.filterRecords` to trim the overscan), which re-apply the stored `timezone` offset. When writing it, combine day + time with `combineLocalDateAndTime` (or `instantForDateTimeInTimezone` to anchor to a record's stored timezone); use `withCurrentTimeOnDay` for non-interactive "log now for this day". Never floor it with `localDayStartMs`. `created_at` is strictly record creation and `updated_at` the last update — don't read consumed time from them.
- **Encryption**: sensitive metrics (weight, body fat) and nutrition logs use the helpers in `database/encryptionHelpers.ts`.
- **Every decrypt is expensive — count them on any path that reads a range of logs.** Each value is its own `CryptoJS.AES.decrypt` (an MD5 key derivation dominates), synchronously on the JS thread. Decrypt only the fields a path reads (`NutritionLog.getNutrients()` never decrypts the food name and decrypts each macro once — don't reintroduce a `hasSnapshot()` + `getDecryptedSnapshot()` pair); share concurrent reads of the same window (`getLatestHistoricalNutritionWindow` does, in flight only); and never add a boot task that decrypts every row on each launch — make it `runOnce`, or select rows on a plaintext column.
- **Food quality badges (Nutri-Score / Eco-Score / NOVA / labels)**: never inline a provider's field names. Each source normalizes into `FoodDisplayQuality` (`utils/foodDisplayQuality.ts`) once, at its own mapper (`getOffDisplayQuality` in `utils/openFoodFactsMapper.ts`, `getMusclogDisplayQuality` in `utils/musclogProduct.ts`); UI reads them through `getExternalProductDisplayQuality(source, details)` (`utils/externalFoodProduct.ts`) with a source already resolved by `inferBarcodeNutritionSource`/`resolveExternalFoodSource`. `foodRecordDisplayQuality` reads badges off a stored food; `applyDisplayQualityToFoodRecord` writes them on save. USDA resolves to `undefined`.
- **Unit system (metric / imperial)**: the database **always** stores metric (kg, cm, g, kcal); users choose in settings (`SettingsService.getUnits()`). Use `utils/unitConversion.ts`: `displayToKg`/`displayToCm`/`displayToGrams`/`displayValueToMetric(value, metricType, units)` when saving; `kgToDisplay`/`cmToDisplay`/`gramsToDisplay`/`metricValueToDisplay` when reading; `getWeightUnit`/`getHeightUnit`/`getMassUnitLabel`/`metricDisplayUnit` for labels (never hardcode "kg", "lbs", "g", "oz"); `isWeightMetricType` (weight, muscle_mass, lean_body_mass) and `isLengthMetricType` (height, chest, waist, hips, arms, thighs, calves, neck, shoulders) to classify. Macros, kcal, body fat, BMI and FFMI need no conversion. Food serving weights are grams displayed as g or oz (`ServingSizeSelector` handles it). **Every new feature that shows or accepts weight, height, body measurements or serving sizes must use these helpers and be tested in imperial mode.** The unit comes from the account (`useSettings().units`): never ask the person to pick kg or lb where the setting already says (the only per-item choices are a gym's and an exercise's weight unit, and the unit of an imported file). A food's mass goes through `useFoodMass` (`hooks/useFoodMass.ts`: `formatMass` for display, `toDisplay`/`gramsAfterEdit`/`stepGrams` for inputs, an untouched field keeping its grams); macros are grams in both systems.
- **Energy is stored and computed in kilocalories; the `energy_unit` setting (`SETTINGS.energyUnit`, `kcal` | `kj`, Basic settings) only changes how it is SHOWN and typed.** Conversion lives in `utils/energyUnit.ts` (pure; `KJ_PER_KCAL` 4.184) and reaches components through `hooks/useEnergyUnit.ts` (`formatEnergy`, `formatEnergyWithUnit`, `toDisplay`, `fromDisplay`, `kcalAfterEdit`, `unitLabel`); code with no hook (mappers, services, the widget) takes the unit as an argument or uses `utils/energyLabel.ts`. Never hardcode "kcal" in UI and never convert a value that is stored, sent to a prompt, exported or passed between components. Locale strings that carry an energy amount take the composed value (`{{calories}}` is "2092 kJ", the key holds no unit) or a `{{unit}}` placeholder. A calorie INPUT is typed in the display unit and must go back through `kcalAfterEdit` (an untouched field keeps the exact stored kcal; otherwise opening and saving a goal would drift it by a calorie) — `NutritionGoalsBody`, `targetsDraft`, `useFoodEditForm`, `CreateCustomFoodModal`. Deliberately still kcal: the raw record editor `GenericEditModal`'s calorie fields (it edits stored values, labelled kcal), AI prompts and the Health Connect/HealthKit workout note. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/energyUnit.test.ts`.
- **Settings → Backups and data → Storage is `services/storageUsage.ts` over the pure `utils/storageUsage.ts`.** It measures and clears only what the app may rebuild: the `exercise-images` cache and the cache directory's leftovers older than `TEMPORARY_MIN_AGE_MS`. **Safety backups (and their sidecars) are written to the documents directory, never the cache the system may empty**; older registered ones can still sit in the cache, so cleanup protects every URI in `getStoredBackups()` and sizes recovery points by URI, and only `TEMPORARY_CACHE_DIRECTORIES` plus loose files are touched: library-owned cache directories and the live `ble-workout-tracking` recording never are. The database and the user's photos are measured, never offered. An entry of unknown age is treated as new. Native only (`isStorageUsageSupported`). Pinned by `utils/__tests__/storageUsage.test.ts`, `services/__tests__/storageUsage.test.ts`.
- **Carbs vs fiber convention (critical)** — two conventions exist; do not mix them:
  - **Food items / nutrition logs** (`NutritionLog`, `inferCaloriesFromMacros.ts`, `NutritionCharts.tsx`, food DB mappers): `carbs` = total carbohydrates **including** fiber (US label standard). Digestible carbs = `carbs − fiber`; use `digestibleCarbs(carbs, fiber)` from `utils/carbsConvention.ts` (never re-inline `Math.max(0, carbs - fiber)`) for energy and for comparing consumed carbs against the (net) carbs goal, so the carbs bar doesn't double-count fiber's own bar.
  - **The "total incl. fiber" invariant is established at ingestion.** Every food-source mapper must run `carbs` through `totalCarbsForFoodSource(source, …)` before storing (`FOOD_SOURCE_CARBS_CONVENTION` holds each source's convention; a change is a one-line edit):
    - **USDA** (`Carbohydrate, by difference`, 1005) and **US/Canada FDA** labels already include fiber → `'total'`.
    - **EU (Reg. 1169/2011)** declares _available_ carbohydrate (fiber excluded) → `'net'` (total = carbs + fiber).
    - **OpenFoodFacts** stores one `carbohydrates` field with no recorded convention → `'off-mixed'`: (1) prefer `carbohydrates-total`; (2) else reconcile the **stated label energy** against both interpretations (they differ by `4 × fiber` kcal/100g) and keep `carbs` only when the energy clearly fits the total interpretation; (3) else assume EU/net and add fiber. Step 2 is conservative — pass the raw stated kcal, never an inferred one.
    - **Musclog REST API** scrapes Dutch supermarkets ("Koolhydraten" = available carbs) → `'net'`; update the `musclog` entry if its upstream changes.
    - **AI / LLM estimates** → `'net'` (the macro prompt's `kcal = 4·carbs + 2·fiber` note, `utils/prompts.ts`). Every AI persistence path (`NutritionService.logCustomMeal`/`logCustomMealsBatch`, `nutritionAI.ts`, `MyMealsModal`) must run AI carbs through `totalCarbsForFoodSource('ai', …)`.
    - **Manual entry** (`CreateCustomFoodModal`): the `INCLUDE_FIBER_IN_CARBS_SETTING_TYPE` setting (Advanced → Nutrition; seeded from units in `prod.ts`: imperial = include fiber, metric = exclude) says whether typed carbs already include fiber. Convert with `totalCarbsFromSource(manualEntryCarbsConvention(includeFiberInCarbs), …)`; the field label and hint follow the same setting.
  - **Protein is never a share of calories when a weight is known** (`calculateMacros`, `utils/nutritionCalculator.ts`). With body fat opted in, weight_loss/hypertrophy use g/kg FFM; otherwise protein is `PROTEIN_G_PER_KG_BODYWEIGHT` (weight_loss 2.0, hypertrophy/strength 1.8, general 1.6, endurance 1.4; at least 2.0 in any deficit) × `proteinReferenceWeightKg` — the goal weight when set, else current weight capped at a BMI-30 reference for the user's height. When the anchor doesn't fit the calorie budget, `splitAroundProtein` caps protein at `MAX_PROTEIN_CALORIE_SHARE` (75%) and sets `MacroSplit.proteinLimited`; it reserves `MIN_FAT_CALORIE_SHARE` (15%) for fat first. Every caller passes `heightCm`, `goalWeightKg` and `weightGoal`; only a caller with no weight gets the percentage split. The three shares add up to 100 (fats take the rounding remainder).
  - **User nutrition goals** (`NutritionGoalsBody`, saved goals, onboarding results): `carbs` = **net/digestible carbs only**; fiber is a separate additive macro. Total calories = `protein * 4 + carbs * 4 + fats * 9 + fiber * 2`. Never subtract fiber from carbs here.
- **Workout-set lifecycle is independent from RPE**: `workout_log_sets.completion_status` (`planned` | `performed` | `skipped`) is the sole runtime source of truth; `difficulty_level` is an optional 1–10 effort rating and must never be fabricated to make a set count as performed. Progress counts performed + skipped as resolved; volume, analytics, history summaries, PRs and progression use performed sets only. Every new set writer assigns a status explicitly. The nullable schema column and legacy `is_skipped` exist only for upgrade compatibility (native v26 SQL, the run-once web `WorkoutSetStatusMigration`, and `restoreDatabase` for pre-v26 backups); current-format imports pass the strict schema unchanged, and runtime readers never infer lifecycle from RPE or repair rows on access. Code that needs plain set data uses the single `_raw` boundary in `database/workoutLogSetSnapshot.ts`.
- **`loadMultiplier` is a bodyweight-relative load benchmark, not an intensity knob**: the load a trained lifter typically uses on that exercise as a multiple of bodyweight, measured as the app logs it (per hand for dumbbells).
