Anchors are the frozen entries in `data/legacyExercisesData.json` (Deadlift 1.8, Squat 1.4, Bench Press 1.0, Chest Press Machine 0.9, Lat Pulldown 0.7, Overhead Press 0.6, Barbell Curl 0.42, Leg Press 3.5, Lateral Raise 0.10), mapped by `scripts/exercise-load-multiplier-anchors.js`; family rules are in `scripts/exercise-load-multiplier-policy.js`. Consumers: `utils/workoutEnergyCalculator.ts` (scales mechanical work) and `utils/exerciseGoalProjection.ts` (`estimated1RM / (bodyWeight * loadMultiplier * genderFactor)`, advanced above 1.5).
  - **Derive a new value from the closest movement already in the catalogue, never from the equipment type.** A cable variant takes its anchor's value outright; the only discounts are for real biomechanical differences — unsupported stance (`x0.75`) and unilateral loading (`x0.55`). "It's a cable" and "the stack caps lower" are not reasons (the logged weight is already in every formula). `data/__tests__/exercisesData.test.ts` pins the parity ratios, so a category-wide clamp fails the build.
  - **For a bodyweight exercise the multiplier is the fraction of body mass the movement shifts**, derived from [de Leva (1996)](<https://doi.org/10.1016/0021-9290(95)00178-6>) segment masses (pull-up `1 - 2 x 0.0061 = 0.99`, hanging leg raise `0.41`, quadruped glute kickback `0.20`); the push-up family is `0.70` from [Suprak et al. (2011)](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/20179649/) force-plate data. Don't add per-user anthropometrics (body mass and height cancel; sex moves fractions 0.1–6%, under the model's noise). Store sex-neutral values.
  - **A stored `0` means "no displacement to credit" (isometric holds, cardio, stretching, mobility) and must stay a hard zero.** Don't coerce it to `1` in the calculator (a plank would score as a full-bodyweight rep); fix it in the data. `utils/exerciseGoalProjection.ts` is the exception and guards its divisor, since `0` would yield `Infinity` (`?? 1.0` doesn't catch a stored `0`).
- **`muscle_group` holds two vocabularies and readers must handle both**: the bundled catalogue and `CreateExerciseModal` write coarse names (`legs`, `arms`, `core`, `abdomen`, …), while exercises migrated from the legacy database carry fine-grained ones (`quads`, `biceps`, `abs`, …). The `MuscleGroup` union in `database/models/Exercise.ts` enumerates both. **Never match group names yourself** — use `muscleGroupFamily(group)` / `MUSCLE_GROUP_FAMILY` (`database/models/muscleGroupFamily.ts`, WatermelonDB-free so pure code can import it). A hand-written `Set<MuscleGroup>` naming one vocabulary fails silently (`LEG_GROUPS` in `workoutEnergyCalculator` once missed all 60 bundled leg exercises). `MUSCLE_GROUP_FAMILY` is a `Record<MuscleGroup, …>`, so widening the union fails the build until each name's family is declared.
- **The bundled catalogue is the 873-entry free-exercise-db import** (CC0): `data/exercisesData.json` (structure) + `data/exercisesEnUS.json` (English copy), generated by `scripts/generate-exercises-data.js` using `scripts/exercise-catalogue-mapping.js`, `exercise-load-multiplier-anchors.js` and `exercise-load-multiplier-policy.js`. `scripts/exercise-popularity-policy.js` owns the exactly 100 stable slugs selected from usage rankings; the generator emits `isPopular: true` only for those rows (never `false`). Keep that slug-based — don't infer popularity from the alphabetical `exerciseIndex` or fan one generic ranked movement out across local variants. `isPopular` is structural metadata and not persisted. `data/exercises{EsEs,NlNl,PtBr,RuRu}.json` are generated by `scripts/generate-exercise-locales.js` — edit the generators, not the JSON. After regenerating upstream data, immediately run `npm run generate-exercise-locales` and `npm run generate-lang`. Localized copy joins structural data through stable `exerciseSlug`; `exerciseIndex` is only 1-based display order. Every database id is `fx-<__freeExerciseDbId>` from `appExerciseId`, and bundled workout definitions name `exerciseSlug` directly — never derive either from position. `AppExerciseCatalogueService.sync` owns the database projection (structural fields plus the exact active target-muscle link set) before `LegacyExerciseCatalogueMigration` may retire old rows. The legacy 256-entry catalogue (`data/legacyExercisesData.json`) is only for historical migration v18, multiplier anchors and the Game Boy generator; never point runtime seeding or the Game Boy table at the wrong catalogue.
- **Exercise photos follow the catalogue slug**: `scripts/generate-exercise-images.js` writes 512px q65 WebP frames to `public/images/exercises/<slug>/{0,1}.webp`; `buildExerciseImagePath` and `buildExerciseCloudUrl` (`utils/exerciseImage.ts`) are the path/URL sources of truth. Website renderers combine the relative path with `EXPO_BASE_URL` through `withExpoBaseUrl`. Because every filename is `0.webp`/`1.webp`, `exerciseImageCacheKey` must include the slug (`<slug>__0.webp`). Retired illustrations live under `public/images/exercises/legacy/exercise<N>.webp` only for user-owned clones created at cutover. See `docs/FIXES.md`.
- **A new app language touches more places than `lang/locales/`** (the 2026-11 German, French, Italian, Polish and Turkish work found them all): the folder under `lang/locales/<xx-xx>/` (then `npm run generate-lang`), `data/exercises<Xx><Xx>.json` (add the target to `scripts/generate-exercise-locales.js` and run it: machine translated) and `data/workoutTemplates<Xx><Xx>.json`, `components/website/localeRoutes.js` (`WEBSITE_LOCALES`), the language and metadata tables (`app/+html.tsx` `LANDING_TRANSLATIONS`, `components/website/websiteMetadata.ts` `translations` and `OG_LOCALES`, `WebsitePreferences.tsx` `LANGUAGE_LABELS` — a missing label crashes the header's language picker —, the programs page's `PROGRAMS_COPY`), `app.json` `expo-localization` `supportedLocales.ios` plus the generated `ios/…/Info.plist` `CFBundleLocalizations` (regenerate with `npm run prebuild:ios`), and the tests that list locales (`localeRoutes`, `WebsitePreferences`, `generate-web-seo-files`, `workoutTemplatesData`, `tests/layoutAudit/locales`, `tests/upgrade-compatibility.spec.ts` follows the sitemap). A Haiku (`worker-lite`) translates chunks of about 40 KB well when it is given fixed terminology per language, a checker (placeholders, keys, plural keys, untranslated share) and the rule to write only the plural keys English has; run a value-only terminology pass afterwards (workers disagree on terms such as Workout/Training). OCR (`utils/ocr.ts`) maps only some languages to a Tesseract pack; Italian, Polish and Turkish still fall back to English OCR.

- **Translation**: check `lang/`. Every new feature uses translation keys and adds them to all locales. Plurals are FLAT sibling keys (`setCount_one`, `setCount_other`), never a nested `{ "_one": … }` object, which makes i18next return an object instead of text. Bundled workout definitions are `data/workoutTemplatesData.json`; copy is in `data/workoutTemplates{EnUS,EsEs,NlNl,PtBr,RuRu}.json`. `scripts/generateLangFile.js` generates `WORKOUT_TEMPLATE_COPIES_BY_LOCALE` in `lang/lang.ts`; `data/workoutTemplates.ts` merges the active locale's copy by array index (falling back to `DEFAULT_LANG`), so every copy file must preserve the definition file's order. Template difficulty is structural (`beginner` | `intermediate` | `advanced`), not in locale files; UI translates the identifier. `__title` in the definition file is a maintainer aid, typed on `RawWorkoutTemplateDefinition` only and stripped by `applyWorkoutTemplateCopies` — don't rename it to `title` or read it.
- **User-visible numbers**: format with `Intl` via `hooks/useFormatAppNumber.ts` (components) or `utils/formatAppNumber.ts` (pure code), using locale `i18n.resolvedLanguage ?? i18n.language`. No `toFixed`, raw `{n}` in JSX, or `toLocaleString()` without an explicit locale for display. Keep `roundToDecimalPlaces` / DB math separate from display.

### WatermelonDB Usage

- **Transactions**: all writes go in `database.write(async () => { ... })`. On web, `unsafeResetDatabase()` must also be in a write block.
- **Avoid deadlocks**: never nest `database.write()`. `@writer` model methods must not be called from inside another write block — reach them through `writer.callWriter(() => record.someWriter())` (or `this.callWriter(...)` from a `@writer` model method), which joins the open transaction.
- **Every soft-deletable model overrides `markAsDeleted` with `@writer`** — never inherit WatermelonDB's. This app soft-deletes by stamping `deleted_at`; the base `Model.markAsDeleted` tombstones for Sync (`_status = 'deleted'`, `deleted_at` untouched) and isn't a `@writer`. Without the override it throws through `callWriter`, silently hides the row from WatermelonDB queries while raw SQL and exports still see it, or throws outside a write block (2.10.5 shipped `WorkoutLog` without it: every delete threw after tombstoning, orphaning exercises and sets). `database/models/__tests__/markAsDeleted.test.ts` pins every table with a `deleted_at` column. Cascades belong in the owning service (`WorkoutService.deleteWorkoutLog`, `WorkoutTemplateService.deleteTemplate`), not the override — `WorkoutPlan` is the one exception and cascades only to its own junction rows. Code that must retire rows in the SAME `database.batch` as their replacements uses `prepareSoftDelete(record, now)` (`database/prepareSoftDelete.ts`) instead of hand-stamping.
- **Read-then-write must be atomic (no TOCTOU)**: when a write depends on a prior read ("insert only rows that don't exist", "seed if missing"), do the read **inside the same `database.write()`**. An outside read is fine only as a best-effort early-out, with the authoritative check repeated inside (the old exercise seeder's outside read caused `UNIQUE constraint failed: exercises.id` in builds 270/272). `AppExerciseCatalogueService.sync` reconciles rows and links in one writer (`database/services/__tests__/exerciseCatalogueServices.test.ts`).
- **A migration and `schema.ts` must declare the same columns, and new measurement columns name their metric unit**: `database/__tests__/migrationSchemaParity.test.ts` diffs them (type, optionality, index), requires every column of a migration-created table to come from a migration, and requires every number column added from v27 on whose name mentions a weight or distance to end in `_kg`, `_g`, `_m` or `_cm`. Its `KNOWN_SHIPPED_DRIFT` lists five v8/v10/v16 columns whose shipped migration disagrees with the schema (a shipped migration can't be edited; fixing them needs a raw-SQL migration, which triggers the pre-migration snapshot and is ignored on web) — never add to that list. Derived columns that a JS boot task fills (`exercises.search_text`) must be selected with a clause matching on both adapters: LokiJS's add-columns step sets existing records' column to `null`, and Loki `eq(null)` also matches a missing property (`database/__tests__/exerciseSearchTextWebMigration.test.ts`).
- **Never pass an unbounded id list to `Q.oneOf` — batch through `database/queryByIds.ts`.** SQLite caps bound parameters per statement. `fetchByIds(table, column, ids, ...clauses)` / `fetchMapByIds(...)` chunk at `MAX_QUERY_IDS` (300), dedupe ids and apply extra clauses to every chunk; `MAX_BATCH_OPERATIONS` (500) sizes `database.batch`. Push soft-delete filtering into the query (`Q.where('deleted_at', Q.eq(null))`). `NutritionService.fetchLiveFoodsByIds` is the nutrition-side wrapper — don't inline another "query foods by id, build a Map, skip deleted". Pinned by `database/__tests__/queryByIds.test.ts`.
- **JSON fields**: use `@json` (e.g. `@json('micros_json')`, `@json('week_days_json')`). Keep a plaintext date field beside encrypted fields when the date is needed for queries.
- **Raw SQL — never open expo-sqlite on `musclog.db` (critical, data loss)**: expo-sqlite and WatermelonDB bundle separate SQLite libraries and POSIX locks don't conflict in-process, so a closing expo-sqlite connection unlinks the live `-wal`/`-shm` files; WatermelonDB keeps committing into the unlinked file and everything since silently vanishes on process kill (June 2026: users lost nutrition logs). Raw SQL while the app runs goes through `rawQueryViaWatermelon` (`database/wmdbRaw.ts`, reads/PRAGMAs) or `database.adapter.unsafeExecute` (writes). The only exception is `preparePreMigrationBackupBeforeAdapter()` in `database/preMigrationCapture.ts` (native-only, imported solely by `database/adapter.ts` before `new SQLiteAdapter`): it reads `user_version` and, **only when a data-touching migration is pending** (`pendingMigrationsCanTouchExistingData`, `database/migrationSafety.ts`; additive-only migrations skip it), writes a `.db` snapshot with **`VACUUM INTO`**, closes the raw connection and lets WatermelonDB open the file. `database/preMigrationBackup.ts` registers that snapshot plus an AsyncStorage sidecar; `database/sqliteBackupConvert.ts` converts the snapshot to the portable JSON export **lazily** (on restore/download), opening the _copy_ and injecting the sidecar. A **different** db file (the snapshot, legacy `workoutLoggerDatabase.db`) may be opened with expo-sqlite; the hazard is the _live_ `musclog.db`. Don't open it during migration callbacks, after boot, or from new paths. Read the `docs/FIXES.md` write-up before adding raw SQLite access.
- **Boot-time DB-ready gate**: `seedProductionData()` calls `unsafeResetDatabase()` on a fresh install, temporarily swapping in an `ErrorAdapter` that throws on any query. Startup code that touches the DB (e.g. in `AppBoot.tsx`) must `await waitForDbReady()` (`database/dbReady.ts`); `seedProductionData()` calls `markDbReady()` when done. A provider mounted by `AppDbReadyGate` before onboarding completes must not observe the database at mount (an observable that errors during the reset never emits again): `SettingsContext` and `MenstrualCycleProvider` subscribe after `waitForDbReady()`. Conversely, any `unsafeResetDatabase()` outside seeding (restore, clear data) must first `await waitForBootMigrations()`, because the migration chain starts right after `markDbReady()` (see `docs/FIXES.md`).
- **On web, `AsyncStorage` IS `window.localStorage` — never call `AsyncStorage.clear()`**: it wipes the whole origin, including stored web recovery points (`musclog_backup_data_*`), the backup index and the schema-version marker. Every wipe path uses `database/asyncStorageReset.ts`: `clearAppAsyncStorage(preserved)` and `replaceAppAsyncStorage(pairs, preserved)` skip `isWebBackupStorageKey`. `clearAllAppData()` is the one deliberate exception. **`isWebBackupStorageKey` is also the export filter** (`captureAsyncStorageDump`) — payloads AND bookkeeping — so any new path enumerating AsyncStorage uses that predicate. Pinned by `database/__tests__/asyncStorageBackupExclusions.test.ts`.
- **A restore swaps AsyncStorage over at the end, and writes before it prunes**: clearing it first left `onboardingCompleted`, `seeding_complete` and `currentUserSyncId` missing for the whole restore, so a mid-restore failure got wiped and reseeded on next boot, and `createPreRestoreBackup()` captured an empty `_async_storage_`. Keep the swap after the database is populated and keep `replaceAppAsyncStorage`'s write-then-prune order (`database/__tests__/importDbAsyncStorageOrder.test.ts`).
- **Musclog 1.x has ONE conversion, `MigrationService`, for both the on-device database and a 1.x JSON backup.** A 1.x export is `{ "<old SQLite table>": rows[] }` with no `_exportVersion` (`isLegacyV1Dump`, `utils/legacyV1Backup.ts`), so `restoreDatabase()` hands it to `restoreLegacyV1Database` (`database/legacyV1Import.ts`) before 2.0 validation. That loads the rows into an **in-memory** expo-sqlite database (`:memory:`, never a file) as untyped columns, then resets and seeds via `seedDatabaseContents` (`database/seeders/prod.ts`) with a `MigrationService` built on that source. Keep: load with `withTransactionAsync`, never `withExclusiveTransactionAsync` (a second connection to `:memory:` is an empty database); build the source BEFORE the reset so a bad file fails with current data intact; pass `valuesEncrypted: false` (both 1.x export paths wrote `UserMetrics`/`UserNutrition` plaintext). Every 1.x table `MigrationService` reads is created even when the backup lacks it, with the columns its SQL filters/joins on — a new query naming another column adds it to `REQUIRED_COLUMNS`. Web has no SQLite (`legacyV1Import.web.ts` refuses before anything is wiped). Pinned by `utils/__tests__/legacyV1Backup.test.ts`, `database/__tests__/legacyV1Import.test.ts`, `database/services/__tests__/MigrationServiceLegacyJson.test.ts`.
  - **A 1.x session's sets are in `WorkoutEvent.exerciseData` (JSON `[{ exerciseId, sets }]`), never the `Set` table** (`Set.workoutId` points at the template; 1.x ids start at 1 in both tables, so reading by event id lost ~70% of sets). `scheduled`/`upcoming` events are placeholders and skipped unless they hold sets (`isPerformedWorkoutEvent`); an unmatched nutrition log gets its own food rather than being dropped; plain-text `Chat` rows become `chat_messages`.
  - A converted log's `template_id` is the NEW template id (via `workoutIdToTemplateId`). Its `calories_burned` is filled after the sets exist by `estimateMigratedWorkoutCalories` through `WorkoutService.estimateWorkoutKcal` (1.x stored none), using `loadWorkoutEnergyProfile()` (`database/services/workoutEnergyProfile.ts`) read ONCE per migration and a single `commitPrepared` batch; the arithmetic is the pure `estimateWorkoutKcalFromSets` (`utils/workoutKcalEstimate.ts`). Logs converted before the id fix hold the old numeric id; the run-once `LegacyWorkoutTemplateIdRepair` repoints each orphan (template_id naming no row, deleted included) at the one live template with its workout name, and leaves it alone if several share the name. Hence "sessions of a workout" is a plain `template_id` match everywhere — never add a name-matching fallback to a read.
- **Web recovery points are database dumps, so payloads live in IndexedDB, not localStorage**: `database/webBackupPayloadStore.ts` owns them; only the small metadata index stays in localStorage (an origin's ~5 MB is less than one ordinary export, so `createPreExerciseCatalogueBackup()` failed on every boot and the catalogue cutover could never run on web). Reads still fall back to legacy `musclog_backup_data_*` keys and deletes clear both — keep that fallback.

### Component Design

- **Theme**: use components from `components/theme/` and follow `theme.ts`. Preferences are named palette IDs (`kinetic-depth`, `kinetic-light`, `kinetic-shock`, `kinetic-volt`, `kinetic-blush`) plus `system`; never reduce the stored preference to a boolean. `theme.registry.js` is the canonical catalogue (every ID, mode and primitive palette); `ThemeId`, semantic token trees, CSS/native variables and `THEMES` derive from it. Add a palette there, then run `npm run check-palette`. `DEFAULT_THEME_BY_MODE` is the single answer to "system says light, now what?" — don't spell those IDs elsewhere. `themeMode` is the derived light/dark value for status bars and `dark:` variants. **A new theme is not complete until it is also on the public website (showcase asset/coverage) and in the Game Boy game (`npm run gb:gen-themes`), and has its Loggy greens in `constants/loggyTheme.js` (then `npm run generate-loggy`, which publishes the website copies)**; keep identity and palette intent identical across all three.
  - A theme carries no component-level presentation flags: express anything a component would branch on as palette values (a theme wanting a flat summary card sets flat gradient stops). `ThemeProvider` resolves the preference once and every `useTheme*` hook selects from that context. First paint reads the preference from a mirror — the settings mirror on native, `utils/themeMirror` (`localStorage`, replayed as CSS variables on `:root` by the pre-paint script `utils/themePrepaint.ts`, inlined by `+html.tsx`) on web. **On web nothing rendered may carry the palette before hydration**: `ThemeProvider` puts no `vars()` on its View there (the static export can only render the default palette, and an inline value outranks `:root` for the whole page) and skips its hydration render (`useIsHydrated`, where the mirror reads as empty); `ThemeScope` may still inline its own pinned palette. **On the website the mirror outranks the settings row** (`<ThemeProvider preferMirror>` in `WebsiteProviders`; every theme change in the browser reaches the mirror, while a website write to the row can be lost before the database opens), and `WebsiteThemeSync` writes the mirror back into the row whenever they disagree; inside `/app` the row stays authoritative (`docs/FIXES.md`). A surface that must pin a palette wraps in `ThemeScope` (the camera viewfinder no longer does: it follows the user's theme); don't create another forced-theme context or read `darkTheme`/`lightTheme` from a component.
  - **Theme app icons**: `npm run generate-theme-icon-backgrounds` applies the palette to the alpha silhouette in `assets/icon-bg-color.png`, composites `assets/icon-bg-glass-effect.png` then `assets/icon-bg-text.png`, and writes `assets/theme-icons/icon-bg-<theme-id>.png` plus an opaque copy in `assets/theme-icons/opaque/` flattened onto `surfaceBase` (iOS rejects transparent icons). Expo's configured icon is the default dark theme's output; the generator doesn't edit `app.json`.
  - **Picking a theme also switches the launcher icon.** Every rule lives in `constants/themeAppIcons.js` (CommonJS, read by the config plugin): the default dark theme is the default icon, every other theme an alternate named by its PascalCase id, and `system` keeps the default icon (the OS can flip light/dark while the app isn't running). `plugins/withThemeAppIcons.js` compiles the alternates (iOS `AppIcon<Name>.appiconset`s + `ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_ALTERNATE_APPICON_NAMES`; Android adaptive icons + one `.LauncherIcon<Name>` `activity-alias` each, `.LauncherIconDefault` the only one enabled); `modules/theme-app-icon` switches them. `services/themeAppIcon.ts` `syncAppIconWithTheme` is called ONLY from the manual pick in `VisualSettingsModal` (iOS alerts on every switch — never on boot, restore or no-op). **MainActivity is never disabled and has no MAIN/LAUNCHER filter** (it owns the `com.werules.logger://` filter the widgets use and the Health Connect rationale filter — which is why `expo-alternate-app-icons` wasn't used); only aliases toggle, the new one enabled before the old is disabled. **Android applies the switch when the app is next backgrounded** (launchers close the task or drop the shortcut otherwise). **Adding a theme needs `npm run prebuild`** or `setAppIcon` rejects. Pinned by `constants/__tests__/themeAppIcons.test.ts`, `plugins/__tests__/withThemeAppIcons.test.ts`, `services/__tests__/themeAppIcon.test.ts`.
- **Geometry and type are rules** (`docs/DESIGN.md` → Typography; Spacing, shape, and touch). Radii come from `theme.shape.js` (`tag` 4, `inner` 6, `control` 8, `card` 12, `sheet` 16), read by `tailwind.config.js` and `theme.ts`; write `rounded-tag|inner|control|card|sheet|full`, never Tailwind's rem-based size-named radii (native rem 14 vs web 16). Text stops at bold, small text (`xs`–`base`) at semibold, no negative tracking, `tabular-nums` for changing or aligned numbers. Buttons are flat and sized from `components/theme/buttonMetrics.ts` (shared by `Button.tsx` and `Button.web.tsx`); cards carry no shadow. `components/__tests__/deslopGuards.test.ts` (Phase 10) holds each rule at zero.
- **A screen's header is `ScreenHeader`** (`components/theme/ScreenHeader.tsx`) — never a hand-rolled title/back arrow or a native stack header (`headerShown: true`). Place it inside `MasterLayout`, above the scroll view. Pass `destination` (a `NavItemKey`) when the screen shows the bottom bar: the header is `root` while that destination is one of the user's slots and `pushed` (with back) otherwise (`resolveScreenHeaderForm`) — slots are user-configurable, so never hard-code it. Put it **outside** the screen's `AreaLockGate` with a matching `lockArea`, so back works behind a lock while subtitle and actions are hidden. `deslopGuards.test.ts` (Phase 8) lists the screens on it; add new top-level screens there.
- **Controls come from `components/theme/`**: `Button`, `TextButton`, `IconButton`, `Chip`, `ListRow`, `SegmentedControl` (full width or `pill` — never both on one screen; `pill` is the inline toggle and, with `fullWidth`, the form for a secondary preference in a settings list), `NumericTextField` (a typed number held as the user's own string; empty = no value). `docs/DESIGN.md` → Buttons maps each control kind. Don't hand-roll a pill, round icon button or text link with `Pressable` + classes; add a variant to the theme component. **Never give a centered `TextInput` a native `placeholder`** (on Android the caret sits at the far edge of an empty field) — `NumericTextField` draws the placeholder as an overlay and hides it while focused. Pinned by `components/__tests__/themeControls.test.ts`. Use `SegmentedControl` for mode switching (7D/30D/90D).
- **A plain stored setting is a `SettingDefinition`**, not a get/set pair: `SETTINGS` (`constants/settingDefinitions.ts`) names each setting's key, default, `parse` and `serialize` once; `SettingsService.get(SETTINGS.x)` / `set(…)` / `resetToDefaults([...])` and `SettingsContext` (`readSetting(map, …)`, `DEFAULT_STATE` from `defaultValue`) read through it. Add a simple setting as one definition; keep a dedicated method only for real behavior beyond the stored string (asymmetric shape like `getHomeActions`/`setHomeActions`, merging like `updateNotificationPreferences`, encryption). A setting the context also holds under its own name is one name in `REGISTERED_SETTINGS` (`context/SettingsContext.tsx`): the state type, the default (`defaultSettings`) and the read (`readSettings`) all derive from `SETTINGS`, and `SettingsContextType` derives from `SettingsState`, so there is no second field list to keep in step; only a setting with its own shape (a renamed raw string, a decrypted key) is written out by hand. `serialize` may throw to refuse a never-storable value (`reviewWeekday`). Tests stub settings with `settingsGetMock({ … })` (`database/services/__tests__/settingsGetMock.ts`).
- **Settings the first frame needs come from `utils/settingsMirror.ts`, not defaults.** Settings live in the `settings` table, readable only after the database opens (seconds on a large database). Theme, language, units and the app-lock scopes are mirrored to one AsyncStorage entry (`SETTINGS_MIRROR_STORAGE_KEY`): `hydrateSettingsMirror()` starts at module load in `app/_layout.tsx`, `AppDbReadyGate` awaits it (and applies the mirrored language to i18n) before mounting providers, `SettingsProvider` seeds from it, and every emission writes it back (`recordSettingsMirror`). The table stays the authority. Add to `MirroredSettings` only a setting that changes the first screen and is neither secret (AsyncStorage is unencrypted) nor large; the mirror is in `ASYNC_STORAGE_EXCLUDED_KEYS`, so a restore drops it. `LanguageInitializer` does nothing while `isLoading` (the pre-load language is a default; switching to it flashed English). **Lock scopes are read only in the safe direction**: with no mirror, `appLockScopesPending` is true until the table is read, `isScopeLocked(null, …)` locks every scope, and `AppLockOverlay`/`AreaLockGate` draw a neutral cover rather than `LockScreen` (which prompts for auth on mount; most people have no lock). The exception: a database not ready when `SettingsProvider` mounts — `AppDbReadyGate` only does that for an install that hasn't finished onboarding, which can't have a lock. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/settingsMirror.test.ts`, `hooks/__tests__/useSettings.test.ts`, `components/lock/__tests__/lockPending.test.ts`.
- **Home actions are table-driven, but only half the table is compiler-enforced.** `HOME_ACTION_KEYS` (`constants/homeActions.ts`) is the ordered canonical list and `HomeActionKey` derives from it; the compiler then demands the UI config (`components/home/homeActions.ts`) and the handler (`app/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`), both `Record<HomeActionKey, …>`. It does **not** demand the persistence chain: `HOME_ACTIONS_SETTING_TYPE` (`constants/settings.ts`), `SettingsService.getHomeActions`/`setHomeActions`, and the `homeActionsRaw` field on `SettingsState` plus `homeActions` on `SettingsContextType` (`context/SettingsContext.tsx`) — v2.12.2 shipped without it and the home screen crashed (`docs/FIXES.md`).
  - **Every `SettingsState` field must be a primitive.** The observer commits a new state only when `prev[k] !== next[k]`; a freshly allocated array makes that permanently true and re-renders all ~85 `useSettings()` consumers on every settings write. Store the raw string (`homeActionsRaw`) and parse once in a `useMemo` on the context value. A `NonPrimitiveFields<SettingsState>` assertion makes violations a build error.
  - `HOME_ACTIONS_PER_ROW` is the single grid setting; `MIN_HOME_ACTIONS`/`MAX_HOME_ACTIONS` derive from it. `parseHomeActions` owns validation, de-duplication, both bounds and the default (every default branch returns a fresh array). The grid width is known in one other place: the `flexBasis` `HomeActionsRow` gives each tile's wrapper (`ActionButton` is `w-full`).
  - **Availability is a required `isAvailable` field on each `HOME_ACTIONS` entry**, not an `if` chain; `useAvailableHomeActions` (`hooks/useAvailableHomeActions.ts`) is the single derivation (don't re-inline `HOME_ACTION_KEYS.filter(...)`). **Every catalogue entry must be reachable on some device** (`components/home/__tests__/homeActions.test.ts`); an unshipped action belongs in `docs/FUTURE_FEATURES.md`.
  - **A stored selection outlives its availability, so consumers reconcile rather than filter.** `reconcileHomeActions` drops what this device can't offer and tops up to `MIN_HOME_ACTIONS`, returning the SAME array when nothing changed (the signal `VisualSettingsModal` uses to persist the repair once, on open). `HomeActionsRow` runs it for rendering only and owns the grid. `VisualSettingsModal` reads the available list through a **ref** (this form state is a snapshot, not a subscription — a dep would let a settings change elsewhere re-read disk over the user's in-progress selection; same rule as `usePlanDraft`).
  - **The picker is `components/home/HomeActionsPicker.tsx`, and pick order IS home order.** Pure rules: `toggleHomeAction` (`constants/homeActions.ts`) applies both bounds, appends a new pick at the END (remove and re-pick to reorder) and returns the SAME array reference when a bound refuses (skip the write); `orderHomeActionRows` puts chosen actions first in pick order. Refused rows render disabled. Labels use `home.actions.*` through `homeActionListLabel`. Don't reuse `OptionsMultiSelector` (superset selector; its LINK button wrote `groupId`s onto home actions).
- **Navigation destinations are table-driven — adding one is a data edit.** `NAV_ITEM_KEYS` (`constants/settings.ts`) is the ordered canonical list; `NavItemKey` derives from it.
  - `NAV_DESTINATIONS` (`components/navigation/navDestinations.ts`) holds each destination's icon, bottom-bar `labelKey`, account-menu `menuLabelKey` and `route`. `NAV_SLOTS` (`components/NavigationMenu.tsx`) holds only bottom-bar routing modifiers (`activePath`, `alsoActiveFor`/`prefetch`/`navigateWhenActive`); every slot renders through `NavSlotButton`.
  - **The main destinations are tabs of ONE tab navigator, `app/app/(tabs)/` (Home, Food, Workouts, Profile, Progress, Cycle, Weekly Review, Notes, Settings); every other screen is pushed over it in the outer `Stack`.** The URLs are unchanged (a route group adds no segment). The tab navigator draws no bar of its own (`tabBar` returns null): each screen still renders `NavigationMenu` through `MasterLayout`. **`TAB_ROUTE_NAMES` in `app/app/(tabs)/_layout.tsx` must list every screen in the folder** (a screen not declared there is "hidden" and expo-router redirects focusing it to Home; `destinationNavigation.test.ts` fails otherwise). Before the tabs, every screen was a sibling in one flat `Stack`, whose `navigate`/`push`/`replace` ADD a screen unless the target is the focused one, so each bar tap mounted a new copy and 50 rounds of Food → Workouts → Home left 101 live screens (ten-second switches, then an out-of-memory relaunch; `docs/FIXES.md`). Rules: (1) a main destination is reached with a plain `router.navigate(href)` (`HOME_ROUTE` and `isHomePath` live in `components/navigation/navDestinations.ts`): from a tab it is a tab switch, and from a pushed screen rule (2) turns it into a pop back to the tabs. (2) **`singleTabsRouter`** (`components/navigation/singleTabsRouter.ts`, the outer stack's `UNSTABLE_router`) turns a `NAVIGATE`/`PUSH`/`REPLACE` to `(tabs)` into "pop back to the tab navigator already in the stack" (`pop: true`), so a tap from a pushed screen, a notification or any `router.navigate('/app/nutrition/food')` can't mount a second tab navigator; it must stay a pure function of `original`. (3) **Hidden tabs stay mounted, bounded**: `components/navigation/TabKeepAlive.tsx` (the navigator's `screenLayout`) keeps the most recently focused tabs mounted (`utils/tabKeepAlive.ts`: `KEEP_ALIVE_TABS` 4, `LOW_MEMORY_KEEP_ALIVE_TABS` 2 under `LOW_MEMORY_DEVICE_BYTES` of `Device.totalMemory`, unknown RAM keeps 4) and unmounts the rest; iOS `memoryWarning` drops every hidden tab (Android's React Native never emits it). The limit is a bound on what stays alive, not a feature switch: don't make "keep alive" optional per device (two behaviours double the testing and hide bugs on one class of phone). (4) **A screen that stays mounted must follow what changes while it is hidden**: new route params (the food diary's `date`, Weekly Review's `reviewId`/`reviewDay`/`checkinId` are followed through `useOnChange` (`hooks/useOnChange.ts`), which reacts to a changed value during render, no remount) and midnight (`followTodayAfterRollover`, `utils/calendarDate.ts`: a diary showing "today" moves to the new today). A new tab screen reading `useLocalSearchParams` or a "today" value must do the same. Back from a tab goes to Home (`backBehavior="initialRoute"`, `anchor: 'index'`) and from Home leaves the app. Pinned against expo-router's own stack and tab reducers by `components/navigation/__tests__/destinationNavigation.test.ts` and `utils/__tests__/tabKeepAlive.test.ts`.
  - Availability has **one** definition: `isNavItemAvailable(item, isCycleActive)` in `hooks/useNavigationItems.ts`. Cycle navigation is offered only while tracking is enabled (bleeding or symptoms-only); Settings always exposes `CycleTrackingModeSettings` with the three `CYCLE_TRACKING_CHOICES` as onboarding. Skip pauses without deleting history; mode changes resume the existing cycle and record a dated context via `CycleTrackingService.setTrackingMode` — don't create a replacement cycle.
  - **Don't add a `case` to `renderNavSlot`, a per-destination JSX block to `UserMenuModal`, or a `NavItemKey[]` literal.** Two deliberate explicit branches: `coach` (`route: null`; opens a modal, each surface supplies its handler, renders `CoachUnreadBadgeIcon`) and `cycle`'s `isCycleActive` gate. `Record<NavItemKey, …>` maps are compiler-enforced; plain array literals go stale, so lists are derived or typed `NavItemKeyList`.
- **Charts**: x labels come from `getXAxisLabels`, which caps them by window width (`maxXLabelsForWidth`: six on a large phone, five on the narrowest, fewer when the layout narrows at large text, 44px per gap over a plot that is the window minus 150px), and a legend row wraps. Always provide a `.web.tsx` counterpart for `victory-native` components (avoids Skia errors); use absolute positioning for axis labels; use `ChartTooltipContext` (`context/ChartTooltipContext.tsx`) for coordinated tooltips.
  - **`ActivityHeatmap` (`components/charts/ActivityHeatmap.tsx`) is the GitHub-style day grid from plain views — one file for all platforms, no Skia, deliberately no `.web.tsx`.** Layout and bucketing are the pure `utils/activityHeatmap.ts` (`buildActivityHeatmap`): days are local `yyyy-MM-dd` strings (a ms key drifts an hour across DST), repeated days are summed, any activity is at least level 1. Squares shrink to a readable minimum, then scroll, opening on the latest week; **a grid wider than its card must show that it scrolls** (edge fade into `useSurfaceColor()` plus a paging arrow — a web mouse has no horizontal wheel; wrap in a `SurfaceColorProvider` when not on the screen background). All text arrives translated from the caller (`legend`, `scrollLabels`, `describeDay`); names come from `Intl`. **`compact` is the half-width form** (no weekday column, 8–14pt squares, short date readout; pair with fewer `weeks` and no `legend`). Prefer it over the older `TrainingConsistencyChart` (fixed 12×7, no real dates) for anything calendar-based.
  - **Home shows it through `components/home/HomeHeatmaps.tsx`, which is self-gating**: `home_heatmaps` (`none` default, `workouts`, `nutrition`, `both`) and `home_heatmap_layout` (`full` default, `side_by_side`, `compact`) are ordinary `SETTINGS` definitions mirrored as primitives. `side_by_side` applies only when both are on and uses `ActivityDayGrid.tsx` (last 30 days from `buildActivityStrip`, `HOME_HEATMAP_DAY_GRID_COLUMNS` (10) per row, title only; a tapped day replaces the title in a fixed-height header so height never changes). `compact` draws ONE short card via `ActivityHeatmapStrip.tsx` (one thin row of 30 days per source, same levels and `ACTIVITY_LEVEL_OPACITY`, no legend/labels/scrolling). Each source is an icon with an accessibility label; `useActivityHeatmapDays`'s `enabled: false` keeps an unselected source from subscribing. With the setting off the component renders nothing and subscribes to nothing; the data hook lives in the per-card component below the gate (`components/home/__tests__/homeHeatmaps.test.ts` fails if the route file reads a heatmap setting or calls the hook).
  - Data is live from `ActivityHeatmapRepository` (`observeWorkoutDays`, `observeMealDays`) reading only plaintext columns (nothing decrypted): a workout belongs to the local day it STARTED on; a nutrition day is valued by DISTINCT MEAL TYPES (one AI meal expanded into twelve ingredient rows isn't twelve lunches), bucketed with `utcNormalizedDayKey`, and uses `observeWithColumns` because meal type edits in place. Scales are fixed, not derived from the busiest day (`WORKOUT_HEATMAP_MAX_VALUE` 1.5, `NUTRITION_HEATMAP_MAX_VALUE` 4, `utils/activityHeatmapData.ts`). Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{activityHeatmap,activityHeatmapData}.test.ts`, `database/repositories/__tests__/ActivityHeatmapRepository.integration.test.ts`, `components/workout/session/__tests__/scrollOverflow.test.ts`.
- **Surface color**: components that fade content to "whatever is behind me" (collapsed accordions, scroll shadows) read `useSurfaceColor()` (`context/SurfaceColorContext.tsx`) instead of assuming `background.primary` or taking a color prop. `BottomPopUp` publishes its sheet surface via `SurfaceColorProvider`; any container painting its own background should too (`GenericCard` does for `flat` and `raised`, not the gradient `hero`, so a chip row's edge fade inside a card ends in the card's own fill, not the darker page behind it). A fade starts from the surface at zero alpha (`addOpacityToHex(surface, 0)`), never `'transparent'`, which is transparent black and tints the ramp dark on Android (`FilterTabs` does this; `useScrollFade`, `ActivityHeatmap` and `ExerciseHeader` still use `'transparent'`). Android: content inside such a fade (e.g. `NutritionQualityData` badges) must not use `elevation` (elevated views composite in a separate Z pass and can paint above the non-elevated overlay). The badges draw no shadow at all now.
- **No colored shadow, however it is spelled.** A halo is a shadow tinted with a brand, status or variable color (`shadowColor: theme.colors.accent.primary`, `shadowColor: statusColor`, NativeWind `shadow-accent-primary/50`, a `boxShadow` template with a non-scrim color). The guard in `deslopGuards.test.ts` reads the value, not a token name (the old check only matched `shadows.accentGlow` and missed twelve halos): only `theme.colors.shadow` and `theme.colors.text.onAccent` may set `shadowColor`, which is what chart tooltips and floating buttons use. Selection, focus and "done" are shown with a fill, a border or an icon, never a glow.
- **An icon-only touchable carries an `accessibilityLabel`.** `IconButton` requires one in its types; a hand-rolled `Pressable`/`TouchableOpacity` around a lone icon takes `accessibilityRole="button"` and a label from `common.a11y.*` (`increase`, `decrease`, `previousDay`, `moveUp`, `moreInfo`, `scanBarcode`, …) or an existing `common.close|edit|delete|back`. `components/__tests__/iconOnlyTouchables.test.ts` parses the JSX and fails on a new one. A render callback that is not a component (CoachModal's `renderSend`) cannot call `useTranslation`; it uses the module-level `i18n.t`.
- **Reduced motion.** Reanimated 4 defaults every animation to `ReduceMotion.System`, so `withTiming`/layout animations already honour the setting and need no flag. What it does not cover: a repeating sweep jumps to its end state (the `SmartCameraFrame` scan line draws nothing under `useReducedMotion()` rather than park a glow band at the bottom), and `react-native-fast-confetti` only halves its particles by default (`ConfettiOverlay` passes `reduceMotion={{ mode: 'system', factor: 1 }}` to drop them). Spinners built on RN's own `Animated.loop` are progress indicators and stay; the animated Loggy's clock is the other allowed loop (`deslopGuards.test.ts`) and is skipped outright under `useReducedMotion()`.
- **Loggy appears on a list, and `components/__tests__/loggyPlacement.test.ts` holds it.** A mascot on every screen stops meaning anything, so a new `<LoggyPose>`/`<LoggyCameo>` (or an `EmptyStateCard` `pose`) fails that test until the file is added to its allowlist WITH a reason, and docs/DESIGN.md's Loggy bullet names the moment first. Rules that are easy to break: (1) a recoverable failure uses `LOGGY_FAILURE_POSE` (`components/LoggyPose.tsx`), never `panic`, which is the year story's busiest-month joke; (2) `EmptyStateCard` types `pose` onto the `bare` (whole-screen) form only; (3) once-ever firsts get a `LoggyCameo` through `ConfettiOverlay`'s `withLoggy` (default `true`); a screen that already shows him passes `false` (`workout-summary.tsx`), and a surface the coach persona owns passes `confettiWithLoggy={false}` to `FullScreenModal` (`CoachModal`), since the persona, not Loggy, is the coach there; (4) he is never on cycle, lock, body, settings or coach surfaces or a permanent card (the strength levels card had a flexing one and lost it), and never praises or scolds in copy. (5) a surface built around his own colours (the year story) draws him `appearance="classic"`; everywhere else his greens follow the theme. A new pose is traced from a reference (`trace-image-to-svg`), never generated. **Loggy's artwork is CC0 (public domain)**, unlike the GPL code around it: `assets/avatars/LOGGY_LICENSE.txt` holds the scope and the legal code, `GENERATED_NOTE` (`scripts/generate-loggy-svgs.js`) puts the notice into every generated SVG (so the website copies carry it too) and the animated components' header names it, and `components/__tests__/loggyLicense.test.ts` pins all of it. Keep it so: a reference or part must be art whose rights are ours to dedicate (never third-party or licensed art), and a new Loggy folder or copy goes into that file's scope list. The Musclog name and logo, the coach persona pictures and the generator scripts are outside it.
- **Keyboard avoidance**: any screen/modal with a `TextInput` (or wrapping input component) in a scroll container **must** use `KeyboardAwareScrollView` from `react-native-keyboard-controller` (`KeyboardProvider` is mounted in `app/app/_layout.tsx`).
  - Full-screen layouts / screens (`FullScreenModal` with `scrollable={true}`, plain screens): replace the outermost `ScrollView` with `KeyboardAwareScrollView` and `bottomOffset={16}`.
  - Bottom sheets (`BottomPopUp`): the default `scrollable={true}` sheet lifts itself via `marginBottom` — the lift clears the **whole sheet** (`computeKeyboardSheetLift`, `components/keyboardSheetLift.ts`) so footers stay visible, capped so the header never leaves the screen unless the sheet can't clear the keyboard at all (then the focused input wins); don't narrow it to an input-only lift. With `scrollable={false}` and your own inner scroller, use `KeyboardAwareScrollView bottomOffset={16}` (`EditPastWorkoutDataModal.tsx`).
  - Never use RN's `KeyboardAvoidingView` in new code (edge-to-edge issues on Android).
  - **Never pass `contentContainerClassName` to `KeyboardAwareScrollView`** — NativeWind maps it only for components `react-native-css-interop` registers, so the classes are silently dropped. Put padding/gap on an inner `View`, and keep only layout classes (`flex-1`) in its plain `className` (padding/margin/gap there are dropped too). Enforced by the `local/no-keyboard-aware-content-container-class` ESLint rule.

### AI & LLM Integration

- **AI settings are two modals.** `AISettingsModal` (title "AI providers": Musclog Iron card, free tier, own keys and models, local server, on-device) and `AIBehaviorSettingsModal` (title "AI behavior": daily insights, thinking level, memory and history, OCR and photo quality, coach persona, custom prompts). Settings lists both rows; `AISettingsModal` also opens the second from a row, nested like `PremiumModal`. Each owns its own `useDebouncedSettings`; a new behavior setting goes in the second, a new provider in the first (the local LLM's "turn thinking on" nudge stays with the provider).
- **Every AI option that sends data off the phone asks first, and cloud AI is for adults.** The person's own providers (`gemini`, `openai`, `claude`, `local`) ask through `useAiConsentGate` + `AiProviderConsentModal` when a switch is turned on or a key is saved (the Gemini, OpenAI and Claude switches start on, so saving the key is what activates them) and when AI setup is about to use one; each agreement is recorded with its wording version and time in `SETTINGS.aiConsentRecords` (`services/aiConsentRecord.ts`, a queued read-merge-write; bump `AI_CONSENT_VERSION` when the wording changes). The gate runs the action only AFTER the record is saved, so an option is never on without one. On-device AI asks nothing. The free tier and Musclog Iron are for 18+ (`utils/ageGate.ts`, `services/aiAccess.ts`): refused where they are turned on, hidden in `PremiumModal` (`useCloudAiBlockedForAge`) and switched off at boot before the daily insights. **Daily insights are off by default**: they send data with no tap. Synced Health Connect / Apple Health entries can reach a prompt, so every consent and the privacy page say so; keep them saying it.
- **Turning on the Musclog free tier is an explicit, named consent** (App Store 5.1.2(i)): it forwards requests to Google Gemini, so every path that enables it says so first: the onboarding "Musclog AI" step (`app/app/onboarding/musclog-api.tsx`, a three-way choice of Premium, the free tier or no AI from `availableAiChoices` in `utils/onboardingAiChoice.ts`; Premium only while `usePremium().enabled`, the free tier only while `isMusclogGatewayAvailable()`) shows `onboarding.musclogApi.dataSharingBody` while the free tier is selected, above the button that turns it on (selecting a card enables nothing; choosing Premium opens `PremiumModal` and the step carries on once `useHostedAi` is on), and the AI Settings toggle and the AI-setup provider picker (`app/app/onboarding/ai-provider.tsx`) ask through the shared `AiProviderConsentModal` with `settings.aiSettings.musclogFreeTier.consent*` (`needsFreeTierConsent`, `utils/aiConsent.ts`: only off → on asks; `useAiConsentGate` refuses it to anyone under 18). The setting defaults to off; never enable it without one of these. If the gateway's upstream provider changes, the copy changes with it.
- **The free tier is OpenRouter's free `google/gemma-4-31b-it:free` model, pinned to Google AI Studio (no BYOK key)** (provider kind `gateway`, `constants/gateway.ts`, `services/GatewayService.ts`; Cloudflare AI Gateway is gone). The app calls `https://openrouter.ai/api/v1` directly with `EXPO_PUBLIC_MUSCLOG_GATEWAY_AUTH_TOKEN`, a key that ships in the bundle and so is public: it must be a dedicated OpenRouter key restricted by a guardrail (the free model only, Google AI Studio only, a daily budget; two guardrails, since OpenRouter splits access and budget), never the Premium Worker's key. The model is `google/gemma-4-31b-it:free`. `constants/gateway.ts` ignores a base-URL or model override left over from the retired Cloudflare AI Gateway (2.17.2 shipped with the old EAS variable set and every free-tier request got `401 [{"code":2009,"message":"Unauthorized"}]`); delete those EAS variables too. Every request body is made authoritative in `buildOpenAIClient`'s `fetch` by `withGatewayProviderPin` (`utils/gatewayRouting.ts`): `provider` is exactly `{ only: [MUSCLOG_GATEWAY_PROVIDER], allow_fallbacks: false }` (never `zdr` or `data_collection: 'deny'`, which no free endpoint satisfies), `models` and `plugins` are dropped, and `max_tokens` is set to `MUSCLOG_GATEWAY_MAX_TOKENS` when the request names no limit. The coach chat shows `coach.freeTierNotice` while the provider is `gateway`, and the consent copy says adults only (18+) and not to enter personal or sensitive information. The Premium key is never in the app: only the Worker holds it (`utils/__tests__/premiumKeySeparation.test.ts`), and the Worker's `assertPremiumPrivacy` (`proxy/allowlist.ts`) throws unless every Premium body has `zdr: true`, `data_collection: 'deny'` and `allow_fallbacks: false` (it also sends `order` with `only`). Account-level ZDR at OpenRouter is off, so that per-request object is the only protection. and the guardrail allows only the free model, so a used-up quota fails as a daily-limit error instead of falling back to a paid endpoint billed to the credits that also fund Premium. The account setting "Allow free endpoints that train on request data" must be on or free-tier requests fail; account-wide ZDR is off, so Premium relies on the Worker sending `zdr: true` on every request (`proxy/allowlist.ts`). OpenRouter's 402 and its 403 budget refusals (`limit_source: openrouter_*`) count as credits errors (`isAiCreditsError`). The key is unpaid Google quota, so the consent and the privacy page say Google may keep, review and train on what is sent; if the Google project gets billing (Paid Services), revisit that copy. The free tier stays off on production web (`utils/musclogGatewayAvailability.ts`).
- **Gate every AI-powered affordance on `isAiConfigured` (required).** Any button, menu item, card action, quick action or settings row whose only purpose is to reach an LLM feature must be hidden when no provider is set up. Read `isAiConfigured` from `useSettings()` (reactive; covers the Musclog free-tier gateway, on-device AI, local LLM, Gemini, OpenAI and Claude). Idioms: menu arrays `...(isAiConfigured ? [ { /* item */ } ] : [])` (see `app/app/(tabs)/nutrition/food.tsx` `mealMenuItems`, `CreateWorkoutModal.tsx`, `app/app/(tabs)/notes.tsx` `noteMenuItems`); JSX `{isAiConfigured ? <AiThing /> : null}` (never `&&` — `local/no-jsx-logical-expression` is an error); components taking a prop use `isAiEnabled={isAiConfigured}`.
  - **The one exception is navigation**: `useNavigationItems.isItemAvailable` returns `true` for `'coach'` and `UserMenuModal` always shows Coach, because those are discovery surfaces — tapping opens `AINotConfiguredModal` with an "Open AI settings" CTA. A nav destination may advertise AI; an action inside a menu or sheet must not. Don't "harmonize" the two.
  - Don't use `hooks/useAiEnabled.ts` in new code (Gemini/OpenAI only, non-reactive).
- **AI output is labelled, and every consent for an AI option Musclog runs accepts that it can be wrong.** A surface that shows AI output, or turns an AI option on, renders `AiOutputNotice` (`components/ai/AiOutputNotice.tsx`; `kind` picks one line from `ai.outputNotice.*`, the key tables live in the pure `components/ai/aiOutputNoticeCopy.ts`; tapping opens `AiDisclaimerSheet` with `ai.disclaimer.points.*` and a link to the Terms, rendered inside the component so it is never a sibling modal). Today: the coach chat (it replaced `coach.disclaimer`), the AI meal review (`LogMealModal`), the photo-estimate context sheet, the AI label scan (`FoodMealTrackingDetailsModal`'s optional `aiOutputKind`, set only by that path), the AI workout generator (the notice sits before the generate button and again over the review) and the AI meal generator (saves without a review step, so the notice sits before the generate button), the pasted-workout import, both ChatGPT/Claude hand-off reviews, the AI-setup review, AI settings, AI behavior's insights toggles, `AINotConfiguredModal` and the onboarding AI screens; the chat-message editor in Settings → Data shows `ai.disclaimer.editHint` (`GenericEditModal`'s `hint`), because any message, the coach's included, can be edited or created there. The Iron consent and both free-tier consents end with `ai.disclaimer.consent` through `withAiAccuracyConsent` (`utils/aiConsent.ts`), and the onboarding Musclog AI step shows it under the free tier's data-sharing notice. That paragraph is the express acceptance the Terms' "AI Features and AI Output" section relies on (EU digital-content rules bind a consumer to "this may be wrong" only when they were told specifically and accepted it expressly), so never drop it from a consent, and bump `PREMIUM_CONSENT_VERSION` when it changes. The Terms section (`website.terms.sections.aiOutput`: `content1`, `content2`, ten `items`), the liability carve-out (`liability.content3`: nothing limits liability the law does not allow us to limit; keep it, since an exclusion that claims too much can be struck out whole), `disclaimer.content3` and the privacy pointer (`optionalAi.accuracy`) carry the same clauses in all ten locales. Copy never promises that an AI result is accurate. Pinned by `components/__tests__/aiOutputNoticeCoverage.test.ts` (a new AI surface joins its list), `components/website/__tests__/aiOutputTerms.test.ts`, `components/ai/__tests__/AiOutputNotice.dom.test.tsx`, `utils/__tests__/aiConsent.test.ts`.
- **The supported-model lists are the only place a model is retired — never write a model migration.** `GEMINI_MODELS` and `OPENAI_MODELS` (`constants/ai.ts`) are both the picker contents and the allowlist. `resolveGeminiModel`/`resolveOpenAiModel` (`utils/aiModels.ts`) map any stored value not in the list to `DEFAULT_GEMINI_MODEL`/`DEFAULT_OPENAI_MODEL`; `AiService.getAiConfig` applies them every call and saves the replacement, and `SettingsContext`/`aiKeyTest` resolve through the same functions. Removing an entry retires it for everyone on their next AI call. Gemini thinking config is per family in `GEMINI_MODEL_CAPABILITIES` (`utils/gemini.ts`): 3.5/3.6 Flash accept `minimal`; 3.8 Flash, 3.1 Pro and Gemini 4 list only low/medium/high; an unlisted level is an API error — re-check when adding a model. Refresh monthly with the `update-ai-models` skill. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{aiModels,geminiGenerationConfig}.test.ts`, `services/__tests__/AiService.test.ts`.
- **Every LLM request goes through one funnel, `complete()` → `runPrepared()` in `utils/coachAI.ts`, and what differs per provider lives in `ProviderPolicy`.** The free-chat reply, a plain-text insight and a structured job are one `CompletionRequest` (`operation`, `system`, `turns`, `userMessage`, `images`, `task`, optional `schema` and `sink`, `chat`) prepared per provider (`prepareGeminiCall`/`prepareClaudeCall`/`prepareOpenAiCall`/`prepareOnDeviceCall`, each returning the debug payload, a `once` attempt and a `stream` attempt) and run by `runPrepared`, so the retry loop, the stream's reset, the debug dump and the token usage (`noteUsage`) exist in exactly one place; an adapter never calls `withLlmRetry` or writes a dump itself. The downloaded model reports its own token counts (`LocalGenerateParams.onUsage`, `extractLocalUsage`); Apple's system model is `unmanaged` (no retry, timeout or abort race) and answers a structured request with a parsed `value`. The downloaded model's native tool call (`requestNativeToolCall`) goes through `runPrepared` too. `redactDump` keeps what a request carried out of the debug dump (voice transcripts). Provider differences are policy fields, never a `provider === 'x'` branch in a feature: `structuredReplies` (`'enforced'`: the server enforces the schema and a token-limit stop is a failure; `'repaired'`: the reply is shape-checked and repaired; absent: used as it came), `keepsPartialStructuredReply` (custom local server), `skipsMealFirstLook`, `estimatesFoodsFromLabels` and `structuredFailureIsNoAnswer` (both on-device engines), next to the older `chatSampling`, `maxChatHistoryTurns`, `runsSamplesSequentially`, `reasoningEffort`, `prepareImage`. `PROVIDER_THINKING` (`utils/aiThinkingLevel.ts`) is keyed by `CoachAIProvider`, so a misspelt provider is a type error. `generateStructured` sends its `userMessage` AS GIVEN: text a person or a label wrote goes through `wrapUserContent`/`prepareChatText` at the caller (`generateMealStructured` in `utils/coachMealSteps.ts` does it for every meal request, the one place that forks between a photo and words), and text written by code (an instruction, `conversationTranscript` of a plan request) is never escaped or capped as a whole (a plan request once lost the newest message to the 2,000-character cap). **An AI-estimated meal is logged by one function, `logTrackedMeal` (`services/trackedMealLogging.ts`), for the coach chat and the camera**: food by food, scaled to the portion, with each matched `foodId` and fiber; its review totals are `trackedMealTotals` (`utils/trackedMealTotals.ts`, carbs through `storedMealCarbs`), so the camera's review says what the diary will say.
- **Claude (Anthropic) is a third own-key provider, with its own small module.** `utils/claude.ts` holds only what has no dependency on the retry layer (client, `buildClaudeMessages`, `extractClaudeText`, `claudeStreamDelta`, `claudeEffort`); the request itself is `buildClaudeParams` + `prepareClaudeCall` in `utils/coachAI.ts`, sent through `complete()` (the ONE funnel of every request: chat, plain text, history, structured, image; `runPrepared` writes the debug dump and records token usage once), so retry/timeout/abort/streaming/debug-dump behave like the other providers. Rules that are easy to break: (1) **send only `max_tokens`, `system`, `messages` and `output_config`** — the 5.5 models reject `temperature`/`top_p`/`top_k`, `thinking: disabled`, `budget_tokens`, prefill and forced `tool_choice`; structured output is `output_config.format` (a strict schema via `makeSchemaStrict`), never a forced tool; (2) thinking is always on, so the only lever is `output_config.effort`: `claudeEffort(effort)` maps the job's `AiEffort` (`utils/aiEffort.ts`) to `low`/`medium`/`high`; (3) a conversation must start with a user turn, so `buildClaudeMessages` drops a leading coach message (the seeded welcome) and merges same-role turns; (4) a refusal is an empty reply (`malformed` for structured calls, `{}` for chat) — **no server-side `fallbacks` are configured**, add them in `prepareClaudeCall` if refusals show up; (5) `StructuredDialect` has a `claude` member: schema builders must return the Claude shape (optional fields rather than OpenAI's required-nullable, `additionalProperties: false` added by `makeSchemaStrict`). The key is the encrypted `claude_api_key` (`SettingsService.getClaudeApiKey`/`setClaudeApiKey`, excluded from backups like the others); the model and switch are `SETTINGS.claudeModel`/`SETTINGS.enableClaude`, with `resolveClaudeModel` against `CLAUDE_MODELS`. Priority is last (`hosted → gateway → on-device → local → gemini → openai → claude`, `AI_PROVIDER_KINDS`), mirrored in `SettingsContext`. "Test key" lists models (`models.list({ limit: 1 })`: free, no model needed). `@anthropic-ai/sdk` builds a `TextEncoder` at import, which the jsdom Jest project lacks (`jest.setup.jsdom.js` fills it). Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{claude,coachAIClaude,aiModels,aiKeyTest}.test.ts`, `services/__tests__/AiService.test.ts`.
- **How hard the AI thinks is one setting with four levels, read in one place: Off, Medium, High, Tokenmaxxing** (`SETTINGS.thinkingLevel`, `utils/aiThinkingLevel.ts`, pure). It keeps the historical key `use_thinking_mode`, whose old `'true'` reads as `medium` (what the switch did), so installs and backups need no migration. A request never reads it raw: `thinkingLevelFor(config)` (`utils/coachAI.ts`) goes through `effectiveThinkingLevel`, which makes the **Musclog free tier always `off`** (one daily quota shared by every free user; the picker shows the row disabled with `thinkingLevel.freeTierNote` and keeps the stored level; the one exception to the steps, not the effort, is a spoken meal, which always takes the Medium steps through `trackMealFromVoice`) and caps a `config.background` request (the daily insights, `configureDailyTasks`) at `medium` (`BACKGROUND_THINKING_CEILING`: nobody watches it, and on Iron High would be two requests every day). A level decides two things, each in one table: the reasoning effort per job (`AI_TASK_EFFORT`, next bullet) and how many requests a job makes (`THINKING_PIPELINE`: `verifiedMeal` from Medium, `reviewRounds` 1 at High and up to 2 at Tokenmaxxing, `samples` 3 at Tokenmaxxing (what labels, imports and the no-look meal fallback use), `mealLooks` up to 2 looks × 2 samples at Tokenmaxxing (what a meal uses instead), `chatReview` at Tokenmaxxing, `earlyExit` (true only at Tokenmaxxing, so every Tokenmaxxing count is an upper bound: `EXTRACTION_FIRST_READINGS` is 2 readings, a third only when they disagree; `mealStageIsSettled` (`utils/mealConsensus.ts`) stops a meal after its first look and its two samples when they agree (`MEAL_AGREEMENT_KCAL_TOLERANCE`, 15% of the larger kcal), nothing in the look is uncertain and no sample needed the recheck, else a second look is taken; `reviewAndRevise`'s `isUnchanged` (`sameValue`) ends review rounds whose revision changes nothing; Off, Medium and High never stop early); `extractionSamples` reads an extraction twice at High). The extra requests have two shapes, both in `utils/aiPasses.ts`: `runSamples` (independent answers, one after another on `on-device`/`local`, together elsewhere, each its own logical request so Iron gives each an idempotency key; a sample that fails while others answer is dropped and handed to `onDroppedFailure`, which every caller wires to `reportAiError`; a cancel stops everything, and with no answer the first failure is rethrown and nothing is passed to the callback) and `reviewAndRevise` (a revision replaces the answer only when code says it is no worse; a failed round keeps the answer, only a cancel propagates). What each job does with them: **meals** (`trackMealThinking`) first get two independent first looks at the photo or description (`observeMeal`, `utils/mealObservation.ts`, `THINKING_PIPELINE.tokenmaxxing.mealLooks`: the scene in a sentence or two, the setting, the container and its size, what shows the scale, each food with how much of the container it fills, added fats, sauces and drinks, what is uncertain; no calories and no weight of any food, because grams are only as good as the sense of scale behind them; cloud and own-key providers only, never the on-device model; a failed look is dropped, and with none left the meal falls back to 3 plain samples). Each look feeds two verified samples, and `consensusMeals(samples, groups)` (`utils/mealConsensus.ts`) keeps an ingredient when at least half of the answered samples found it, whichever look they came from (with four samples, two: one look's two samples are enough, a single sample of four is not; with no looks it is the plain vote of strictly more than half; median grams and per-gram macros), then review rounds (`reviewTrackedMeal`: missing ingredients and wrong grams, `applyMealReview` in `utils/mealReview.ts`, added ones estimated and rechecked, kept while `ingredientMacroProblems` does not grow); **the on-device photo** is read `extractionSamples` times (later readings warmer) and voted with `voteIdentifiedFoods` (`utils/localFoodPhoto.ts`), its per-100 g labels combined with `medianLabel` (`utils/onDeviceMacros.ts`); **labels and imports** (`extractWithConsensus`) are combined field by field by `combineExtractions` (`utils/extractionConsensus.ts`), and only disputed fields trigger ONE arbiter request (the same request plus the readings), whose answer goes back through `combineExtractions(readings, { arbiter })`: the arbiter is aligned to the readings the way they were aligned to each other (by key, by identity for lists of named entries such as workouts, by index otherwise) and settles only the nodes the readings disputed (a positional merge once wrote one workout's reps into another); **plans** (`generatePlan`) get critique rounds told the problems code found (`utils/planFindings.ts`), a revision kept only with no more findings; **insights** (`generateInsight`) are drafted unseen and fact-checked with the same system prompt against `ungroundedFigures` (`utils/numberGrounding.ts`), then the final text reaches the stream once; **chat** at Tokenmaxxing is drafted unseen and reviewed, except on both on-device engines, which always answer in one request (`ProviderPolicy.skipsChatReview`: the review turned a 450M model's good drafts into "I'm Loggy.", a copy of an earlier reply or the checklist itself) (`draftAndReviewCoachReply`; only the review is streamed, a failed or empty review keeps the draft, a failed draft gets the apology one request would; the four `sendVia*` are transport only and throw, and `sendCoachMessage` builds the system prompt and the trimmed turns (`turnsFor`, `latestChatTurns` for every provider) once and turns a failure into the apology or a typed error once, `utils/__tests__/coachAIChatContract.test.ts`), with `CoachStream.onPhase('reviewing')` → `CoachReplySnapshot.phase` → `useChatMessages().replyPhase` showing `coach.reviewingReply` until text arrives. **The chat review is the one second pass no code judges, so `getChatReviewInstruction` (`utils/aiReviewPrompts.ts`) is a four-point checklist, not an open "improve this"** (a fact or number that contradicts the app data or the conversation is fixed; a claim about the person nothing supports is removed; pain or injury, an eating-disorder signal, an extreme deficit, pregnancy or medication narrow the advice, add no optimisation that could harm and suggest a qualified professional; it answers what was asked in the persona's voice, length rules and the person's language) and a draft that passes all four comes back unchanged; `draftAndReviewCoachReply`'s logic did not change (a failed or empty review keeps the draft). Off and Medium make exactly the requests they made before the levels. **A review round, the arbiter and a meal's first-look notes are follow-ups of the original request** (`FollowUp`, `followUpOf` in `utils/coachAI.ts`, the `followUp` option of `generateStructured`/`generateWithImage(s)Structured`): the same system prompt, history, photos and already-sanitized user message, then the answer under review as the model's own turn and the instruction as the next user turn, or, with no answer, the instruction appended to the same user turn (Gemini needs alternating roles). Only the person's own words go through `wrapUserContent` (cut at 2,000 characters, quotes and newlines escaped), once; text a pass adds never does (it used to, so a meal-plan critique arrived cut before the plan and a fact-check saw literal `\n`; `utils/__tests__/coachAIFollowUp.test.ts` pins it). The arbiter's readings are quoted inside its instruction between `--- READING n ---` markers. The picker is `components/ai/ThinkingLevelPicker.tsx` (Settings → AI behavior, `AIBehaviorSettingsModal`, and the coach quick settings) over `useThinkingLevelChange`, which asks first what `thinkingLevelChangeGate` says: on Musclog Iron moving up to High shows a note, and Tokenmaxxing a note and then `authenticateForDangerousAction` (fingerprint, face or device PIN); nothing is written until both pass. "Tokenmaxxing" and "Musclog Iron" are never translated. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{aiThinkingLevel,aiPasses,mealConsensus,mealReview,extractionConsensus,numberGrounding,planFindings,coachAIExtractionConsensus,coachAIReviewPasses,coachAIStreaming,coachAITrackMealThinking,coachAIMealLooks,mealObservation,coachAIMatchOwnFoods,localFoodPhoto,chatReviewInstruction,llmRetry,llmRetryOwner}.test.ts`, `components/ai/__tests__/ThinkingLevelPicker.dom.test.tsx`.
- **How hard a request reasons is decided per kind of job and level, in one table.** `utils/aiEffort.ts` (pure) names four tasks (`chat`, `extraction`, `insight`, `planning`) and `AI_TASK_EFFORT` gives each an effort per thinking level (`off`: extraction `minimal`, chat `low`, insight and planning `medium`; `medium` and `high`: `high` for chat, insight and planning but `medium` for extraction, so they differ in requests, not effort; `tokenmaxxing`: `max` for chat, insight and planning but only `high` for extraction, because a reading task is a lookup and never gets the maximum). `coachAI.ts` asks `resolveEffort(config, task)` once per request and translates it per provider: `claudeEffort` (`utils/claude.ts`, `output_config.effort`; `max` is sent as `max`), `buildGeminiGenerationConfig(model, { effort })` (`utils/gemini.ts`; `high` sends no thinking field, the model's own default; `max` sends `thinkingLevel: HIGH`, or the dynamic budget `-1` on 2.5; `minimal` is the family's lowest allowed level) and `openAiReasoning` (`reasoning_effort` `low`/`medium`/`high` through `reasoningEffortValue`, which sends `max` as `high`, since not every OpenAI model takes more). A new request kind names its task (`requestStructured`'s `task`, default `extraction`; `generateText`/`generateTextWithHistory`'s trailing `task`, default `insight`; `sendStructuredChatTurn` is `chat`; plan and volume generators are `planning`); never read the setting directly in a request builder. Which providers send `reasoning_effort` is `ProviderPolicy.reasoningEffort` (`utils/providerPolicy.ts`): OpenAI itself always, **Musclog Iron only once `/v1/config` says `reasoning_effort: true`** (`HostedConfig.reasoningEffort`; an older Worker answers an unknown body field with a 400, so a new app never sends it blind), a local server, the free tier and Claude/Gemini (their own parameters) never through this path. The Worker clamps the ask to `features.<feature>.reasoning_max` (`effectiveReasoning`, `docs/PREMIUM_WORKER.md`) and gives a higher effort a larger `max_tokens` (`max_tokens_by_effort`), so the table holds only as far as the deployed config allows. On Musclog Iron each level's description says how it uses the monthly allowance (`settings.aiSettings.thinkingLevel.ironNote.*`). The values are product choices, not measurements (`docs/RESEARCH.md` → Reasoning effort per job).
- **An AI meal estimate is refined by code before anyone sees it.** `trackMeal` and `estimateNutritionFromPhoto` are two entry points of ONE `estimateMeal` (`coachMealEstimate.ts`: it throws a typed, user-fixable failure for the caller to describe, reports anything else once and returns null, and toasts nothing; the person's custom prompts are appended on every path, Off included, by `withCustomPrompts`), whose one-request path runs the model's answer through `refineTrackedMeals` (`utils/coachMealSteps.ts`; the meal code is `coachMealEstimate.ts` entry points, `coachMealSampling.ts` thinking-level composition, `coachMealSteps.ts` building blocks, `coachMealPhotoOnDevice.ts` the downloaded model's photo reading, `coachMealFoods.ts` the name match; they import the request layer from `coachAI.ts` and `coachAI.ts` imports none of them, so there is no factory or dependency bag): an ingredient that named a food id the database does not have AND came with no numbers (the model invents ids; a foundation-food match arrives with zero macros) is estimated in one follow-up request, so it is never logged at 0 kcal; then every ingredient that is not the person's own food goes through `ingredientMacroProblems` (`utils/mealPlausibility.ts`: energy against `4·protein + 4·carbs(net) + 9·fat + 2·fiber`, macro mass against grams, kcal per gram) and the ones that fail are re-estimated once, the new numbers replacing the old only when they have strictly fewer problems. With nothing to fix it makes no request; a failing refinement keeps the original (only a cancel propagates). From the Medium thinking level up they switch to `trackMealVerified` (recipe extraction, id check, **name match**, estimation of the rest), which ends in the same plausibility recheck (High and Tokenmaxxing add samples and review rounds, previous bullet). **The name match** (`matchIngredientsToOwnFoods`, `utils/coachMealFoods.ts`, then `pickFoodMatch`, for every provider including the on-device photo path, which now ends in the same `completeMealIngredients` + `finalizeMealIngredients` steps as the verified cloud path: the model is offered only the foundation foods, so its claimed ids miss what the person eats) looks every ingredient still without a confirmed food id up by name in the foods on the phone: a match keeps the model's name and grams, takes the food's own numbers through `normalizeAiMealIngredients` and is never sent to `estimateMissingIngredients`; a tie, no match, a per-serving food (numbers per serving cannot be scaled by grams), a name with no a-z0-9 word (Russian) or a failed lookup leaves the ingredient to the estimate (a failed lookup only costs the request it would have saved); the lookup asks `FoodService.searchFoodsByTerms` for ONE word per ingredient, the longest (`searchTermsFor` sorts longest first, and a match must carry every word of the name, so that word's matches are a superset of every possible match) and takes every food that has it, with no cut (a limit in name order could hide the right food, or one of two tied foods, and turn "a tie is no match" into a confident match on the survivor); `pickFoodMatch` is not to be loosened; the ingredients a review round adds (`reviewTrackedMeal`) go through the same match, and the `estimating` progress step's `matched` counts claimed ids and name matches together; foundation foods are compared by their English text (see **Foundation foods are localized**), so every meal schema also asks for an `englishName` beside the displayed `name` (see **Foundation foods in every prediction**) and the match compares that, on the name or the description of a stored food, while the person keeps reading their own wording; without one it matches only coinciding words or foods saved in the app language (`docs/THINKING_LEVELS.md` → known gaps). Both report each real step through the optional last argument `onProgress` (`utils/mealEstimateProgress.ts`: `reading`, `observing {photo}`, `estimating {count, matched}`, `rechecking {count}`, `comparing {count}`, `reviewing`; copy `food.aiProgress.*`; only the first of several samples reports), shown by the camera overlay, My Meals' generator and the coach's track-meal status bar. Progress lines state only what the code is doing with counts it has: never a paraphrase of model reasoning, a percentage or a countdown. Each ingredient is paired with the estimate that answers it by `alignEstimates` (`utils/foodName.ts`: by normalized name, an estimate answers at most one ingredient, then what is left over by position only when the leftovers are as many on both sides; `normalizeFoodName` is the one name key, shared by the readings' vote, the review and the consensus), and a step that may improve an answer but must never fail it runs through `bestEffort` (`coachMealSteps.ts`: reports, falls back, only a cancel propagates). `completeMealIngredients` and `recheckImplausibleIngredients` hand back whether a second look was needed, so the verified path returns `{ response, rechecked }` and nothing is filled in through a shared object. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{aiEffort,mealPlausibility,mealEstimateProgress,foodName,coachAITrackMealRefine,coachAITrackMealThinking,coachAIEffort}.test.ts`.
- **On-device AI can hold two downloaded models, and the runtime chooses per request.** `constants/localModels.ts` has `DEFAULT_LOCAL_MODEL` (LFM2.5-VL 450M, onboarding and AI settings, reads photos) and `LOCAL_TEXT_MODEL` (LFM2.5 1.2B, text only, offered ONLY in AI settings (`OnDeviceAiSection`) once the 450M is ready and the phone reports `LOCAL_TEXT_MODEL_MIN_RAM_BYTES`, 7.0e9; never in onboarding). `services/localModels/modelStore.ts` is a factory with two instances, `localModelStore` (today's paths `<root>/models`, `<root>/.partial`, key `LOCAL_MODEL_DOWNLOAD_STATE`) and `localTextModelStore` (everything under `<root>/text`, key `LOCAL_TEXT_MODEL_DOWNLOAD_STATE`, in `ASYNC_STORAGE_EXCLUDED_KEYS`); neither store may touch the other's folders, there is one runtime unloader for both (`registerLocalModelUnloader`), and `deleteLocalModel()` queues both removals at once and unloads once (the storage screen uses it). `modelStore.ts` holds only state and the order of operations; the file work is `modelDownload.ts` (download loop, resume record, space), `modelInstall.ts` (hash, stage, move, write the record, prune), `modelDiskScan.ts` (what a store's folders hold) and `modelFolders.ts` (the layout). `parseInstalledRecord` drops a file path that could leave the model's folder. `llamaRuntime.ts` holds ONE context: a request with a photo goes to the 450M, every other request to the 1.2B when installed, else the 450M; a switch unloads completely before loading. `ProviderPolicy` is per engine, not per model file, except what `providerPolicyFor` reads from `config.textModel` for the gguf engine (the on-device probe copies `capabilities.textModel` from the status store): `answersPainWithFixedReply` and the 450M's `maxVoiceTools` of 3 while it is false, and `nativeVoiceModel` `'text'` (the 1.2B's own voice payload, see **Voice to text**) while it is true. A new model is a manifest entry plus a decision about which requests it takes; never load two at once. Pinned by `services/localModels/__tests__/{modelStore,localTextModelStore,llamaRuntime}.test.ts`, `components/settings/__tests__/OnDeviceAiSection.dom.test.tsx`.
- **The on-device coach has a pain safeguard.** `mentionsPainOrInjury` (`utils/painMention.ts`, pure: a word and phrase list per app language, whole words of the lowercased message, accents NOT folded because Polish "ból" folded is Turkish "bol"; ordinary soreness is not on it) runs on the person's latest chat message for on-device configs only. With `answersPainWithFixedReply` (the 450M, no text model) `sendCoachMessage` returns `coach.onDevicePainReply` in the config's language without a request; otherwise `getChatMessagePromptContent(..., { painMentioned })` puts the measured safety sentence into `getOnDeviceIdentityReminder` (do not reword it without re-measuring: `docs/RESEARCH.md` → Pain and injury safeguard). Cloud providers are untouched. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{painMention,coachAIPainSafeguard,providerPolicy,onDeviceChatPrompt}.test.ts`.
