- **The AI meal camera takes up to five photos of ONE meal, and the setting `ai_meal_camera_flow` picks the flow.** `detailed` (the default; `SETTINGS.aiMealCameraFlow`, AI behavior → Image processing, also a choice block of the 2.18.0 What's new tour) makes `SmartCameraModal` collect photos in a reducer (held, with the estimate, the review and the one `quick`/`detailed` decision, by `components/camera/useMealCameraSession.ts`; the modal itself never reads the flow; `utils/mealPhotoDraft.ts`: `mealPhotoDraftReducer`, `MAX_MEAL_PHOTOS` 5, a refused add returns the same object, origin / added-fat chips clear when tapped again) and open `components/camera/MealPhotoDetailsModal.tsx` (a `FullScreenModal` child of the camera, its discard `ConfirmationModal` inside it): a strip of photos with a + tile, then optional name, note, origin, added fat and tags; the + tile closes the sheet back to the viewfinder, where `MealDraftButton` takes the context button's slot while the draft has photos. While the draft holds anything (`mealCameraControls`, `utils/mealPhotoDraft.ts`: `addingToDraft`), the camera is only there to add to it: the mode picker is hidden and the camera's close (X and Android back) reopens the sheet instead of closing the camera, except while an estimate runs. A gallery pick in this flow takes up to the photo slots left (`gallerySelectionLimit`, through `pickImagesFromGallery`), and `useCameraCaptureFlow` crops each picked image in turn before processing any (a cancelled crop drops only that image). The estimate (`estimateDraft`, over `useMealPhotoEstimate`) reads every photo and calls `estimateNutritionFromPhoto(config, photos, context, onProgress)`, which accepts one data URL or a list; a failure or cancel reopens the sheet with the draft intact and the reason. `quick` keeps shutter → estimate and the pre-photo `AINutritionTrackingContextModal`. Rules that are easy to break: (1) **a photo is a `MealImage` (`utils/mealImages.ts`, `toMealImage` is the one data-URL parser) and `generateWithImagesStructured` is the one funnel**: Gemini gets one `inlineData` part per image, Claude N image blocks before the text, OpenAI-compatible providers N `image_url` parts; a label photo is the same call with one image. Both funnels take a named job (`StructuredJob`), not positional arguments. (2) **Photos are prepared once per estimate** (`prepareMealImages`, then `prepared: true` for every look, sample and review), never per request, and the provider's step takes the photo `count` so the photos share one byte budget (`limitsForImageCount`, `hostedDecodedBytesLimit`: Musclog Iron's body cap is shared by all of them). Everything that is not a photo must fit `HOSTED_BODY_OVERHEAD_BYTES` (42 KB: `utils/premium/__tests__/hostedBodyOverhead.test.ts` measures the largest built-in request, about 30 KB with the full foundation list, five photos' framing, the longest meal context and first look, plus `HOSTED_CUSTOM_PROMPT_ALLOWANCE_BYTES` for custom prompts); a change that grows the meal prompts must keep that test green. (3) **The user's extras become prompt text in ONE place, `formatMealPhotoContext`** (`utils/mealPhotoContext.ts`); several photos add `getMultiPhotoMealNote` to the user message (one meal, count each food once; the system prompts keep saying "the photo"). (4) **How many photos fit is the provider's:** `useMealPhotoLimit` reads Musclog Iron's `image.maxImages`/`bodyMaxBytes` from `/v1/config` (`loadHostedMaxImages`, one photo and the old 512 KB cap when the Worker does not send them, so deploy the Worker before the app) and everything else takes five; `services/premium-worker` allows five images and a 1 MB body. (5) **On-device, one photo per request**: `estimateMealFromPhotoOnDevice` identifies each photo alone (progress step `identifying`), merges the lists with `mergeFoodsAcrossPhotos` (a food in several photos is one food at its LARGEST portion, never summed), a failing photo is left out unless none read; the cloud pipeline needs no extra pass, because every request already sees all the photos. (6) Only the FIRST photo is saved with the logged meal (`food.imageUrl`, no schema change), and its kept copy is deleted again when the meal cannot be written. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{mealPhotoDraft,mealPhotoContext,mealImages,coachAIMultiImage,multiPhotoMealNote}.test.ts`, `components/camera/__tests__/MealPhotoDetailsModal.dom.test.tsx`.
- **A local model's reply is read by one ladder, and repaired by the model as the last step.** Every reply goes through `parseStructuredJson` (`utils/localReplyShape.ts`: fences and chat-template tokens, the JSON inside surrounding text, `jsonrepair`); free chat (`parseCoachResponse`) uses it too and falls back to reading `msg4User` out of a cut-off object, so a person is never shown raw JSON. For a provider whose policy sets `structuredReplies: 'repaired'` (a custom local server and the downloaded on-device model), `requestStructured` (which the photo funnel goes through) then calls `reviveLocalStructured`, and the photo flow `repairStructuredWithModel`: a reply that is malformed or fails `checkStructuredShape` gets ONE text-only repair request (prompt from `jsonRepairPrompt`/`jsonRepairMessage` in `utils/localReplyShape.ts`), sent through `requestStructuredOnce` like any structured request (never a hand-built second dispatch) and shape-checked again; a cancel rethrows, any other failure keeps the original. A reply that parsed but missed part of the schema stays as it was when the repair does not improve it (OpenAI-compatible callers always tolerated partial answers); cloud providers are never repaired by a second call (their server enforces the schema). `requestStructured` builds the schema once, in `structuredDialect(config)`, and hands it to `requestStructuredOnce` and the repair. Plain-text replies are cleaned too: `generateInsight` is the one plain-text path and its `send` the one place a plain reply goes through `stripTemplateTokens` (`requestPlainText` takes a ready user message and returns each provider's text as it came), the on-device chat strips its reply in `sendViaOnDevice`, and a streamed plain reply shows `stripStreamingTemplateTokens` (also hides a half-written `<|…` token, trims only the start). Both also remove reasoning blocks (`REASONING_TAGS`: `<think>`, `<thinking>`, `<reasoning>`; an unclosed one drops the rest, a closing tag with no opener drops what came before) and the markers that are not `<|…|>`-shaped (Gemma's `<start_of_turn>`, `<end_of_turn>`, `<bos>`, `<eos>`, `<s>`, `</s>`, `[INST]`), so `parseStructuredJson` never mistakes a brace in the reasoning for the answer. An empty on-device chat reply stays empty (`sendViaOnDevice`): the reply service fails the send and gives the text back, never saving the apology as a coach turn a small model would copy. The Gemini image branch reads through `parseStructuredJson` like every other parser. `reviveLocalStructured` is the ONE place a local model's reply is shape-checked and repaired (a reply longer than `MAX_JSON_REPAIR_INPUT_CHARS` is not repaired at all, since a cut copy would come back as a repaired list that lost its tail; a repair call that itself fails is reported through `reportAiError('coachAI.repairStructured')`) (the downloaded model is held to its schema; a custom server's partial answer is kept when the repair does not do better), and a `malformed` result carries the FULL reply in `raw` (the repair step refuses one over `MAX_JSON_REPAIR_INPUT_CHARS` instead of cutting it, since a shortened copy would make the repair drop every item past the cut). `CoachAIConfig` is a union: an `on-device` config always carries its `engine` and `vision` (`OnDeviceConfig`, `isOnDeviceConfig`) and every other provider has neither, so an engine is never guessed. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/coachAILocalChat.test.ts`.
  A cut-off reply from a provider that enforces its schema is not repaired at all: where `ProviderPolicy.structuredReplies` is `'enforced'` (Musclog Iron, your own OpenAI, Gemini and Claude keys), `isTruncatedStop` (`utils/replyTruncation.ts`, reading `finish_reason` `length`, `stop_reason` `max_tokens` or `finishReason` `MAX_TOKENS`) turns a token-limit stop into `malformed` with reason `truncated` in `readStructuredReply` (`utils/coachAI.ts`) before the ladder runs. It is reported once to Sentry (`ai-structured-reply-truncated`, tags only, never the reply) and is not retried. The free tier, a local server and the on-device model keep the full ladder. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{coachAITruncatedReplies,replyTruncation}.test.ts`.
- **Quality signals and silent failures reach Sentry; every one is tags only, never a reply, prompt, food name, path or key.** An unexpected failure anywhere in the AI pipeline goes through `reportAiError(error, context)` (the camera's `useCameraAiProcessing` with its timer name, `useChatMessages.sendMessage`, every `bestEffort` step), which keeps out what the person fixes themselves (a rejected key, a timeout, a cancel, a spent allowance, a dropped connection) and lets in the hosted kinds that are faults: `unavailable`, `alreadyProcessed` and `consentRequired` (the app and the Worker disagree on the consent version). What is not an exception but a quality problem goes through `reportAiEvent` (`utils/aiTelemetry.ts`, a grouped message per event, provider and operation, consent checked inside `captureMessage`): `ai-structured-reply-unusable` (a local reply stayed unusable after the shape check and the repair, also the on-device photo reply), `ai-structured-reply-repaired` (how often the repair call is needed), `ai-meal-look-unusable` (a first look at a meal that held nothing usable), `ai-meal-estimate-empty` (an estimate that ended with no ingredient: no reply, or a reply with none), `ai-chat-reply-copied` (a downloaded-model chat reply that copied an earlier coach turn and was written again), `voice-command-unusable` (a voice command's reply could not be read as a command), `local-model-checksum-mismatch` and `local-model-projector-failed`; `ai-structured-reply-truncated` stays in `coachAI.ts`. A new silent fallback in the AI code says so one of these two ways. Outside the request path the on-device setup reports its own failures and never rejects, because every caller is a tap or a `void` call: `services/onDeviceAiSetup.ts` (`onDeviceAi.enable|disable|engine`, resolves `false`), the status probe (`onDeviceStatus.refresh`, keeps the last snapshot) and the model store (`localModels.<op>`, network failures and no-space excepted). The Worker forwards its own `high` log events, and the `error` ones in `REPORTED_ERROR_CODES` (`otari_provider_unverified`, `settle_failed`), as exceptions whose TYPE is the event code (`sentryLogger`, `services/premium-worker/src/logReport.ts`; Sentry gets the type and the allowed tags only, and each route and code is sent at most once a minute per isolate), because Cloudflare's invocation log is off and a console line pages nobody. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{aiTelemetry,hostedErrors,coachAILocalChat}.test.ts`, `services/localModels/__tests__/{modelStore,llamaRuntime}.test.ts`, `services/premium-worker/test/logReport.test.ts`. **Every user-facing function of `utils/coachAI.ts` (insights, critique, labels, past-data parsers, plans, volume) runs inside `userFacing(name, run)`**: the person is told what they can act on (`notifyUserFacingAiError`), every failure goes through `reportAiError(error, 'coachAI.<name>')` and the function answers null; a new one uses it, so no failure is only `console.error`ed (pinned by `utils/__tests__/coachAIFunctionReporting.test.ts`). Silent fallbacks outside the request path report through `handleError(error, '<context>', { showSnackbar: false })`: `useMealPhotoLimit`, the local model's saved-download state, free-space and space checks (`localModels.savedDownload|freeSpace|spaceCheck`), the runtime's unload and projector release (`localModels.unload|releaseProjector`), Apple's availability reads (`onDeviceAi.appleAvailability|appleLocale`), and the Worker logs `upstream_toggle_unreadable` (console only, never paging) when the D1 toggle cannot be read.
- **Structured output**: use `makeSchemaStrict` (`utils/coachAI.ts`) for OpenAI function calling to ensure `additionalProperties: false`. It does **not** populate `required`; add every declared property to `required` explicitly or strict providers reject the schema.
- **Prompts** are managed in `utils/prompts.ts`; append custom user prompts from the `ai_custom_prompts` table (users create context-specific prompts — general, nutrition, exercise — and toggle which are active).
- **User-fixable AI failures have one classification point, and Gemini text one extractor.** `toUserFacingAiError(error, provider)` (`utils/aiErrors.ts`) maps a raw SDK failure to `AiInvalidKeyError` / `AiTimeoutError` / `null`; `AiCancelledError` is the caller's own cancel. **Gemini answers a wrong key with HTTP 400** (`API_KEY_INVALID` in the payload), so `isInvalidApiKeyError` reads the message/details and a bare 400 stays ordinary — never reduce it to a status check, and never map a gateway, on-device or local failure to a key error. The mapping happens ONCE in `withLlmRetry` (`utils/llmRetry.ts`), which also owns the timeout (`LLM_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`, per-call `CoachAIConfig.timeoutMs`; cancel with `CoachAIConfig.signal`), never retries auth errors, and retries a timeout exactly once. Most `coachAI` functions return `null` on failure, so their catch blocks call `notifyUserFacingAiError` (`utils/aiErrorNotice.ts`, deduplicated snackbar); the coach path (`sendViaGemini`/`sendViaOpenAI`) throws the typed error and `useChatMessages` renders it with `describeAiError`. Typed errors never reach Sentry (`reportAiError`). **Gemini text goes through `extractGeminiText`** (`utils/gemini.ts`), never `response.text ?? parts[0].text` (when every part is a thought the old fallback parsed the thought as the answer). **Gemini generation config goes through `buildGeminiGenerationConfig`** + `GEMINI_MODEL_CAPABILITIES` (Gemini 3 takes `thinkingLevel`, 2.5 takes `thinkingBudget`, exactly one per family; how much a call thinks is decided per kind of job by `utils/aiEffort.ts` (see the effort bullet below)). With no key `configureBasicGenAI` throws `AiInvalidKeyError`. `AiService.getAiConfig` reads every setting in parallel. "Test key" (`utils/aiKeyTest.ts`) reports `network`, not `rejected`, for anything that isn't a key rejection. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{aiErrors,llmRetry,geminiText,geminiGenerationConfig,aiKeyTest,aiErrorNotice}.test.ts`.
- **Logging an AI meal takes a date AND a time, and the modal awaits the save.** `LogMealModal`'s `onLogMeal` receives one `LogMealData` (`date`, `time`, `mealType`, `portionGrams`) and is awaited: the modal closes only on resolve and stays open with its draft on throw. The time reaches the database as `consumedTime` in `options` of `NutritionService.logCustomMeal`/`logCustomMealsBatch` via `consumedDateTimeOnDay(date, consumedTime)`; omit it for "that day, right now". The coach path threads it through `useChatMessages.markMealAsTracked`. Never floor with `localDayStartMs`.
- **Meal photos open full screen through `ZoomableThumbnail`** (`components/ZoomableThumbnail.tsx`, which renders `ZoomableImageModal` inside its own tree so the viewer is a child of whatever modal holds the thumbnail). Gesture geometry (scale clamp 1–5×, pan bounds, focal-point zoom, double-tap target, swipe-to-close) is pure `'worklet'` code in `components/zoomableImageMath.ts` (`components/__tests__/zoomableImageMath.test.ts`); photo gestures use `fitContain` with real image dimensions. Sharing is `utils/shareImage.ts` (system share sheet; a download on web), deliberately NOT `expo-media-library` (needs a permission and prebuild review). Auto-save-to-gallery is in `docs/FUTURE_FEATURES.md`.
- **The publisher is a legal fact in three places and must change in all ten locales together**: Jubinganga Supply (a Dutch sole proprietorship, KVK 72255145, PO Box 69307, 1060 CJ Amsterdam, `support@musclog.app`) is named in `website.contact.operator` (shown on the contact, privacy and terms pages), `website.privacy.sections.controller` and the `definitions.company` entries of privacy and terms; the Terms' governing law is the Netherlands. The privacy policy carries the GDPR basics (controller, legal bases, the person's rights and the Autoriteit Persoonsgegevens, transfer safeguards) and its `lastUpdated`/`terms.lastUpdated` are bumped with every change to either page. Store listings, the App Store review notes and the Play trader details must agree with it.
- **Project links and feedback have one source**: the repo URL, support address and (nullable) community invite live in `constants/projectLinks.json` (JSON because `scripts/generate-web-seo-files.js` runs outside the bundle), surfaced as `GITHUB_REPO_URL`/`SUPPORT_EMAIL`/`DISCORD_INVITE_URL` in `constants/misc.ts`. Never hard-code `github.com/blopa/musclog-app` or the support address. While `DISCORD_INVITE_URL` is null, nothing links to a community server. Settings → Help & feedback (`HelpAndFeedbackCard`) uses `utils/feedbackLinks.ts`; the `version`/`platform`/`locale` query parameters are the field ids of `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/*.yml` (`utils/__tests__/feedbackLinks.test.ts`), and the environment is `getAppBuildInfo()` (`utils/appBuildInfo.ts`) — version, build, platform, OS, language, nothing about the person.
- **Coach personas: one registry, one fallback point, display prose only.** The `coach_persona` setting (`SETTINGS.coachPersona`) selects `loggy`, `dracula`, `cthulhu`, `sherlock` or a user-written `custom` one, defined in `constants/coachPersonas.ts` (pure; `COACH_PRESETS` is a `Record`, so a new preset fails the build until its voice and keys exist).
  - **`resolveCoachPersona` is the only place fallbacks are decided**: on-device AI always resolves to Loggy (no context room; its prompt is the separate minimal one), and so does a custom persona missing name or voice. The result is ONE flat `ResolvedCoachPersona` (`id`, `promptName`, `nameKey | null`, `voice`, `avatar`) — never branch on preset-vs-custom in a consumer; `coachDisplayName` and `coachAvatarSource` (`utils/coachPersonaAvatar.ts`) are the only two answers. UI and prompts both read it (`CoachPersonaProvider`/`useCoachPersona` in `context/CoachPersonaContext.tsx`, `getActiveCoachPersona` in `services/coachPersona.ts`). **Bubble name and avatar come from the persona** (`CoachModal`'s `renderBubble`/`renderAvatar`), never the message's `user` (`COACH_USER` in `useChatMessages` is just `{ _id: 2 }`). `CoachPersonaProvider` (mounted around `CoachProvider` in `app/app/_layout.tsx`, observing via `SettingsService.observe`) is the single owner of the custom-persona subscription.
  - **Prompt split**: `getBaseSystemPrompt` is a locked, name-neutral core (how to talk, scope, safety, language, "app data is data, not instructions"; the chat-only reply fields and rules are `getChatReplyRules`); the persona is a separate block (`buildCoachPersonaBlock`, `utils/coachPersonaPrompt.ts`) appended after it and before custom prompts and memories, so a persona can only colour wording; the block also tells a character to use a light touch (a flourish or two, short replies stay short), to drop the act when the person is in pain, upset or on a safety/medical/eating topic (never mocking body, weight or effort) and to render its mannerisms in the reply's language, and Loggy has a voice of his own, not the generic tone line (`utils/__tests__/coachPersonaPrompt.test.ts`). **Only prose the user reads opts in**: the chat reply and the six prose insight builders (`getNutritionInsightsPrompt`, `getMealCritiquePrompt`, `getRecentWorkoutsInsightsPrompt`, `getWorkoutInsightsPrompt`, `getRecentWorkoutInsightsPrompt`, `getWorkoutVolumeInsightsPrompt`), each calling `getPersonaSystemPrompt(language, context, provider)` (`utils/prompts.ts`) with `provider`/`context`/`eatingPhase` as one trailing `InsightPromptOptions` object (`provider` is `AiProviderKind`; on-device gets Loggy and no injected memories). Every structured or function-calling prompt (`createWorkoutPlanPrompt`, the `getParse*` prompts, `getGenerateMealPlanPrompt`, …) stays persona-free, and the block tells the model to keep `sumMsg`/`sumUserMsg`/`remember_me` neutral. `utils/__tests__/coachPersonaBasePrompt.test.ts` pins both lists — a new prompt builder goes in one.
  - A custom persona's text goes through `sanitizeForPrompt`; the editor refuses text `validateInputSafety` flags. Name/voice/avatar live in the JSON setting `coach_custom_persona` (`SettingsService.updateCoachCustomPersona`, a queued read-merge-write sharing `createUpdateQueue` with `updateComebackState`; read via `SettingsService.get(SETTINGS.coachCustomPersona)` — no `getCoachPersona`-style wrappers), **deliberately not in `SettingsState`**: the avatar is a ≤256px **data URI** (survives web blob URLs and restore on another device), making the row tens of KB. Preset avatars are `assets/avatars/<id>.png`, mapped once in `utils/coachPersonaAvatar.ts`.
  - Seeded welcome messages are templates (`coach.welcomeMessage*`, `{{name}}`) carrying `{"type":"welcome"}`. `applyPersonaWelcome` (`services/coachPersonaWelcome.ts`) **reads the stored persona itself and takes no persona argument** (greeting from a screen's last render greeted a first-time custom coach as Loggy) and rewrites them via `ChatService.rewriteUntouchedWelcomeMessages` only for a conversation whose ONLY live message is that welcome (unmarked legacy ones are recognised by text in any bundled language); each conversation is counted first and read only when it holds one message; count, read and the batched write share one writer. The picker (`CoachPersonaPicker`, used by AI behavior and the personalized-onboarding `coach-persona` step — which follows the AI choice and makes that setup 11 steps) writes through `useCoachPersonaPicker` (saving the custom persona also selects it and greets once; step numbers come from `PERSONALIZED_STEP` in `utils/onboardingRoutes.ts`); the step carries no `nextRoute` (a nested query string in the Musclog AI step's `nextRoute` is decoded once too often).
- **Foundation foods are localized, once, like exercises**: first-run seeding (`database/seeders/prod.ts`, USDA rows only: `data/common_foundation_foods.json` is not seeded and reaches a phone only through a Game Boy day share) writes `name` and `description` in the app language of that boot, and nothing re-localizes them on its own (see **Translate foods and exercises on demand**), so existing installs keep English. English is the text of the two seed files; `data/foundationFoods<Xx><Xx>.json` holds the other nine languages for both files, keyed by `external_id` (first row wins). They are generated by `npm run generate-foundation-food-locales` (never hand-edit: correct a machine translation in `scripts/foundation-food-translation-overrides.json` and run it with `-- --overrides-only`), then `npm run generate-lang`. `data/foundationFoodCatalogue.ts` is the one reader. **Never assume a foundation food's stored words are English**: the AI food lists (in-app and the ChatGPT/Claude hand-off) and the meal name match go through `getEnglishFoundationFoodText` (a field the person rewrote stays theirs), and `FoodService.searchFoodsByTerms` also matches the English and app-language copy in memory (`findFoundationFoodExternalIds`; SQLite `LIKE` only ignores case for ASCII). `utils/optical/` stays free of i18n, so the Game Boy receiver is handed `localizeFoundationFood`. Pinned by `data/__tests__/foundationFoodCatalogue.test.ts`.
- **Translate foods and exercises on demand**: Settings → Profile and units → Localization has two rows, Translate foods and Translate exercises (each behind a confirmation), so either can be run without the other: `CatalogueTranslationService.translateFoods(language)` renames `source: 'foundation'` foods and `translateExercises(language)` renames `source: 'app'` exercises and rebuilds their `search_text`, each in one batch. **It is never automatic**: do not call it from a language change, boot or restore. `swapBundledText` (`utils/bundledText.ts`) is the one rule for what may be replaced: a touched row's field only when it matches some bundled language's copy (a person's own wording stays), an untouched row (`createdAt === updatedAt`) always, and an untouched row keeps `updatedAt` so it stays recognisable. Diary entries keep their `logged_food_name` snapshot. Pinned by `database/services/__tests__/CatalogueTranslationService.test.ts`.
- **Foundation foods in every prediction**: all LLM functions predicting food or meal macros (`trackMeal`, `generateMealPlan`, `estimateNutritionFromPhoto`, …, and any new one) must read `SettingsService.getSendFoundationFoodsToLlm()` and pass it to the system prompt and the function schema. **The list is every foundation food (223 are seeded; a Game Boy import can add common ones), compact and stable, under English names, and one call builds both the prompt text and the way back**: `foundationFoodsFor(config, style)` (`utils/coachAI.ts`) returns null when `ProviderPolicy.omitsFoundationFoods` (the on-device engines only) or the setting is off, else `getFoundationFoodsPrompt(style)` (`utils/prompts.ts`) returns `{ text, idByRef, count }` from `FoodService.getFoundationFoodsSorted` through the pure `buildFoundationFoodList` (`utils/foundationFoodList.ts`): one `reference|name` line per food (references 1..N, no UUID, no macros: a match's numbers come from the database, scaled to the grams), sorted by name then id in code-unit order so the prompt is identical request to request (prompt caches), capped at `FOUNDATION_FOODS_PROMPT_CAP` (1000). The model returns the reference as `foodId`; `resolveFoundationRefs`/`resolveFoundationRefsInMeals` turn it into the real id BEFORE `normalizeAiMealIngredients`, against the list that request sent, and a reference that names nothing is dropped like an invented id (`refineTrackedMeals` reads who claimed an id from the raw answer first, so an invented one with no numbers is still estimated). The meal-plan references stay as written while a critique round re-reads the plan and are resolved once at the end. Schemas take a `MealSchemaDialect` (type in `utils/prompts.ts`, chosen by `mealSchemaDialect(config)` in `utils/coachAI.ts`): OpenAI strict mode lists every property as required (`foodId` a required nullable, `englishName` a required string); Gemini and Claude keep `foodId` optional and require `englishName`; the on-device engines and a custom local server (`optional`) require neither, because a required field a server does not enforce would cost a repair request on every estimate. `englishName` (the plain English food name, matching only, never shown) is in the recipe, one-request trackMeal, review (added ingredients) and on-device identify schemas (optional there); the one-request path only carries it, nothing there name-matches. `services/externalFoodLog.ts` keeps its own 200-food list with real ids because its answer travels through the person's clipboard, where a per-request map cannot work.
- **Foundation food matching**: the LLM may return an existing DB food's `foodId` with **zero macros**, expecting the caller to look up real values. Always call `NutritionService.normalizeAiMealIngredients(ingredients)` before saving or displaying; for ingredients with a `foodId`, reuse the existing food rather than calling `FoodService.createCustomFood`.
- **AI-assisted onboarding is one contract and one apply pipeline.** The guided forms, the in-app setup chat and a ChatGPT/Claude import all converge on `utils/aiOnboarding/contract.ts` (versioned `musclog-onboarding`) and `services/onboardingAnswersApply.ts` (`applyOnboardingAnswers`, `applyProfileUpdate`, `commitOnboardingProfile`, `stageAnswersForGuided`).
  - **Contract**: `normalizeAnswers` judges each answer with the zod schemas `contractDocumentSchema` is assembled from (`answerSchemas`), so the document an assistant is told to write and the one we accept are one definition; a crossed bound is `outOfRange`, any other mismatch `invalid`; unknown keys are dropped and listed via `KNOWN_DOCUMENT_KEYS`; a newer major is refused.
  - **One flat vocabulary, one adapter.** `OnboardingAnswersDraft` is the only answer list: the guided screens' drafts (`PersonalizedSetupData`, `BodyStatsDraft` in `utils/onboardingDraft.ts`) are `Pick`s of it, read back through `sanitizeDraft` and written with `pickAnswers`/`pickBodyStats`. The only translation between flat answers and the contract's nested document is `flatToDocument` — don't add a fifth mapper. Offered choices (`FIELD_CHOICES`) and `FieldEdit`'s choice fields derive from `CONTRACT_ENUMS`; `activityLevel` is typed `ActivityLevel` (1–5). `isRecord` lives in `utils/isRecord.ts`. The envelope every document that comes back from outside the app shares (the `schema`/`version` check and its refusals, `trimmedText`) is `utils/externalDocument.ts`: the setup file, the workout plan and the food log all read it through `readEnvelope`, and a fractional version is a minor revision of its integer part. The leave-for-ChatGPT/Claude step is `useExternalAssistantImport` with `ExternalAssistantHandoff`; each caller supplies its own `ExternalHandoffCopy` table of whole keys and its own `accept`.
  - **The AI only collects answers.** Nutrition targets and starter plans come from `calculateNutritionPlan` and the existing results/first-workout screens; model output is untrusted, validated (`parseAiTurn`, `parseContractText`), and the chat has no tools.
  - **Isolation from the coach**: the transcript lives in one encrypted entry (`AI_ONBOARDING_DRAFT`, `utils/aiOnboarding/draftStorage.ts`), never `chat_messages`; both drafts (`AI_ONBOARDING_DRAFT`, `PERSONALIZED_BODY_DRAFT`) are in `ASYNC_STORAGE_EXCLUDED_KEYS` and cleared by `setOnboardingCompleted`. The setup prompt (`utils/aiOnboarding/prompts.ts`) appends no custom prompts or memories and takes no profile data. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/onboardingAiFlowWiring.test.ts`.
  - **Credentials**: a key is saved only by `services/aiProviderSetup.ts` `testAndSaveApiKey`, after `testApiKey` returns `ok`, through the normal encrypted setters; it never appears in prompts, links, the contract, drafts or logs. Choosing free AI or saving a key sets `skipMusclogApi`, so the later Musclog AI step is skipped (the free tier outranks a key in `getAiConfig`). The chat calls its chosen provider via `AiService.getAiConfigForProvider`. Both go through `PROVIDER_PROBES` (`services/AiService.ts`): each provider says whether it's usable and how to build its config, so a provider that loses the priority race builds nothing (building a Gemini/OpenAI/Claude config can save a replaced model).
  - **Applying is idempotent** (per-draft in-flight map plus `applyState`; every step upserts, notably `persistFitnessProfile`, the single profile writer that `persistFitnessDetails` delegates to). Profile mode shows current-vs-new per field, never completes onboarding, starts a comeback, or creates a gym when one exists.
  - **External hand-off is copy-first**: no prompt-link format is documented for ChatGPT, and Claude's `q` only for desktop `claude://`, so `buildExternalChatUrl` adds `?q=` only under 6000 characters. Only the instructions are ever copied or put in a URL.
  - **`isAiConfigured` exception**: the landing and Profile entry points show regardless (the external/import path needs no in-app provider).
  - **Screens share a session, shell and copy table.** Every AI-setup screen starts from `useAiSetupSession` (mode, saved draft, way out to the forms, `go(screen, params)` — the one place the route string is cast for expo-router) and gates on `isDraftLoaded`; mode-dependent wording is `AI_SETUP_COPY` (`utils/aiOnboarding/modeCopy.ts`), never `isProfile ? a : b`. The review is `ReviewScaffold` with bodies `OnboardingReview` / `ProfileReview` in `app/app/onboarding/ai-review.tsx`. One chat turn (ask, one quiet repair retry, validate, merge) is `runOnboardingTurn` (`services/aiOnboardingTurn.ts`); `useAiOnboardingChat` owns transcript, in-flight request and saving. `chatState.ts` stays pure (type-only imports); the error classifier is `classifyError.ts`.
  - `requestStructured` (`utils/coachAI.ts`) is the one place a schema-bound reply is requested (on-device, Gemini, OpenAI-compatible): `generateStructured` and `sendStructuredChatTurn` (`utils/structuredChat.ts`) are thin callers; don't add a third copy of that dispatch. `weight_loss` is offered on the guided goal screens via `components/onboarding/onboardingOptions.ts`.
- **Onboarding has a shared training branch, and answers apply only when the nutrition plan is accepted.** `app/app/onboarding/training-setup.tsx` serves the quick (`mode=quick`, step 2 of 7) and personalized (`mode=personalized`, step 2 of 11) setups, where Health Connect used to be. It only writes `TRAINING_SETUP_DATA` (`utils/onboardingTraining.ts`); `applyTrainingSetup` (`utils/applyTrainingSetup.ts`) creates the first active gym and the `workout_autonomy` setting from `nutrition-goals-results.tsx` after acceptance, skipping the gym when one exists. `first-workout.tsx` ends the flow for anyone with training answers; `pickStarterTemplateIndex` recommends a long catalogue plan only for `gym`, returns the matching **Short Full Body** plan (`SHORT_PLAN_TITLES`, `shortPlanTemplateIndex`) for `home`/`minimal`, and resolves by the template's `__title`, never array position. An optional "Coming back after a break?" toggle stores `comingBack: { breakLength }` in `TRAINING_SETUP_DATA` (`parseTrainingSetup` stays backward compatible; length validated against a literal list because `comebackState` imports this module's values); `planWeightGoal` makes the first nutrition plan maintenance for such a person, `applyTrainingSetup` starts comeback mode on acceptance (track `both`, `baseBefore` null, a running comeback untouched), and `first-workout.tsx` recommends the short plan for any location. `HEALTH_CONNECT_PROMPT_PENDING` is set by the first-workout screen and consumed once by `workout-summary.tsx`; setups without training answers get the Health screen right after the results. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{onboardingTraining,applyTrainingSetup,onboardingTrainingFlow}.test.ts`.

### Platform Specifics

- **Android**: use namespace imports for image manipulation: `import * as ImageManipulator from 'expo-image-manipulator'`.
- **Web**: target a mobile viewport (390x844); web-specific overrides use `.web.tsx`.
- **A platform sibling may export more than its base module, never less.** Metro picks `.ts` / `.web.ts` / `.ios.ts` / `.android.ts` per platform, but TypeScript checks only the base — a function missing from one variant is a runtime `TypeError` on exactly one platform (v2.12.2: `syncDailySteps` only in `services/healthConnectFitness.ts`; `sendOnDeviceStructured` missing from `utils/onDeviceAi.web.ts`). `utils/__tests__/platformModuleParity.test.ts` enforces it over `constants/`, `database/`, `hooks/`, `services/`, `utils/` (named **value** exports only). A deliberate omission goes in its `ALLOWED_OMISSIONS` table **with its reason**. See `docs/FIXES.md`.
- **`reloadApp()` on web is a URL decision, not a navigation**: `window.location.reload()` re-requests the current URL, so a restore started from onboarding reloads into onboarding over an onboarded database. `restoreReloadTarget(pathname)` (`utils/app.web.ts`) owns the choice — onboarding paths are `location.replace`d to `/app`, everything else reloads in place (`app/(website)/progress.web.tsx` relies on it). Don't "fix" it with `router.replace` before the reload (expo-router commits `history.replaceState` on a later tick). See `docs/FIXES.md`.
- **Open links: `https://musclog.app/open#<workout|food>=<percent-encoded payload>` opens the app, and ONLY that exact path does.** `utils/openLink.ts` (pure) is the one parser (`parseOpenLink`, `parseOpenFragment`) and `utils/openLinkInbox.ts` holds the pending slot (`setPendingOpenLink`/`takePendingOpenLink`/`subscribePendingOpenLink`) for the screen that will own the import; `app/+native-intent.tsx` checks it BEFORE the widget `action=` branch (payload text may contain "action="), stays inert until onboarding completes, stashes the payload and routes `workout` to `/app/workout/workouts`, `food` to `/app/nutrition/food` (no/unknown payload: `/app`). **`hooks/useOpenLinkHandler.ts` is the one consumer** (`useOpenLinkHandler(kind, onPayload)`, and `useIncomingOpenLink` for a screen that owns an import modal): on focus it drains `takePendingOpenLink(kind)` and while focused it listens through `subscribePendingOpenLink`, so a cold start, a link that arrives on another screen and one that arrives on the target screen itself all land. **Only the focused screen instance takes the payload** (a second copy lower in the stack would open a modal nobody sees), and the other kind stays pending. `workouts.tsx` and `food.tsx` hold `incoming: { text, key }` ABOVE their `AreaLockGate` and pass it to `ExternalWorkoutPlanModal` / `ExternalFoodLogModal`, which run their existing `accept(text)` once per `key`: success opens the review directly (nothing is saved before the person confirms), failure shows `linkError` on the first step, and the newest link replaces an open review. The gate does not mount its children while locked, so behind a lock the review waits for the unlock (`utils/__tests__/openLinkWiring.test.ts`). `encodeOpenPayload` percent-encodes everything but RFC 3986 unreserved characters (plain `encodeURIComponent` leaves `( )`, which end a markdown link), `buildOpenHttpsUrl` is the shape the assistant writes and `findOpenLinkInText` finds a link in any text (markdown, `<url>`, bare, a whole chat message). **Sharing a saved workout produces such a link**: `WorkoutTemplateService.getShareMessage` keeps its name, description and exercise list and appends `workouts.shareOpenLink` plus the `https://musclog.app/open#workout=…` link from `buildTemplateShare` (`utils/aiWorkoutPlan/templateShare.ts`, pure: one `musclog-workout-plan` document with a single workout; custom exercises have no catalogue slug and are left out of the LINK only; warm-up sets and loads are not part of it; null, so plain text, when nothing can travel or the link would pass `OPEN_LINK_SUGGESTED_MAX_CHARS`). The payload is in the FRAGMENT so no server (GitHub Pages, analytics) sees it; never move it to a query string. The claim is declared in `app.json` only (`android.intentFilters` with an exact `path`, `autoVerify`; `ios.associatedDomains`): **the native manifest and entitlements are generated, never hand-edited**, regenerate with `npm run prebuild:android` / `prebuild:ios`. Ownership is proved by `public/.well-known/assetlinks.json` and `apple-app-site-association` (copied into `dist` by the Pages workflow; both must be served with 200, JSON and no redirect). **`assetlinks.json` holds two SHA-256 fingerprints and `apple-app-site-association` the real Team ID**; no placeholder is left, but nothing in the repo proves the fingerprints are the Play App Signing key and the EAS upload key, so compare them with Play Console → App signing and `eas credentials` before relying on App Links. If they are wrong Android never verifies the link, and a tap there always lands on the bridge page below instead of the app. `app/(website)/open.web.tsx` is the bridge for when the OS does not open the app (no app, an in-app browser such as an assistant's, a tap from another musclog.app page; the page cannot make an in-app browser hand an https link to the app, and chat apps generally only linkify `http(s)`). Once it has read the payload it counts down `OPEN_FORWARD_SECONDS` (5, `hooks/useCountdown.ts`, a deadline not a tick count so a paused tab forwards promptly) and then sends the visitor to the custom-scheme twin (`com.werules.logger://open#…`, `buildOpenSchemeUrl`) with `window.location.assign`. A browser may refuse a launch no tap asked for, so the button, the same URL behind a tap, stays on the page and the text after the countdown points to it. The page removes the fragment from the address bar after reading it so the analytics script never sees it. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{openLink,openDeepLink,openLinkConfig}.test.ts`, `hooks/__tests__/useCountdown.test.ts`, `components/website/__tests__/openPage.test.ts`.
- **Widgets**: Android/iOS home screen widgets live in `widgets/`; see the Home Screen Widgets entry below.

### Feature Highlights

- **Store-rating requests go through `recordPositiveMoment(moment)` (`services/reviewPrompt.ts`) and nowhere else.** The policy is pure (`utils/reviewPrompt.ts`): a request needs 3 positive moments since the last one, 120 days between requests and at most 3 ever, and the state is written BEFORE `requestReview()` (the OS throttles and never says whether the dialog showed). It does nothing where `isAvailableAsync()` is false (web). Today's moments: a workout finished within the last 15 minutes on a summary visit that didn't show the Health Connect prompt (`workout-summary.tsx`), and the first successful Weekly Review decision of a visit (`weekly-review.tsx`). A new moment must be a finished, positive action shown with the UI idle, never an app launch or an error path. `REVIEW_PROMPT_STATE` is device-local and stays in `ASYNC_STORAGE_EXCLUDED_KEYS`. The manual "Rate Musclog" row (`HelpAndFeedbackCard`) opens `GOOGLE_PLAY_URL` and never calls `requestReview()`. It is hidden on iOS until there is an App Store listing. `expo-store-review` pulls in Play Core's review library, which an F-Droid build would have to stub out. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/reviewPrompt.test.ts`, `services/__tests__/reviewPrompt.test.ts`.
- **What's new announcements are data**: `data/whatsNew/<x.y.z>.json` holds one release's ordered pages and `info`, `choice`, `featureList`, `action` blocks; `releases.ts` explicitly imports, validates and sorts them. The registry test requires every JSON file to be registered, valid and translated in all ten locales. `whatsNewSeenThroughVersion` (AsyncStorage) means **seen through this app version**, never the last announcement version; it only moves forward (including on empty upgrades and downgrades) and is included in backups. Fresh onboarding writes it in the same `multiSet` as completion; landing-screen imports opt out so restored records survive and legacy backups get the tour; restoring a backup without the record allows the tour after reload.
  - Home checks once per JS session after DB readiness/onboarding, presents only while focused, and suppresses widget camera launches. `WhatsNewIntro` is centred, leads with a large icon and lists each page's headline via `pageHeadlineKey` with supporting copy via `pageHeadlineDetailKeys`; its footer is always Back/Skip + Next side by side (Skip takes Back's slot on the first page). A page may carry `requires` (a `WHATS_NEW_CONDITIONS` entry: `cycleTracking`, and `premium` = `isPremiumUiEnabled()`, which gates the single Premium page of **2.17.5.json**, whose `openPremium` modal action renders `PremiumModal` inside the tour). **A release may bring its own first page** (`intro` in its JSON: `titleKey`, optional `descriptionKey`, `pose`, `requires`, up to a few `items` drawn as large filled rows), shown by `WhatsNewIntro` in place of the generic one; `selectWhatsNewFlow` takes it only from the NEWEST release with pages in the tour and only when its `requires` holds (`flow.intro`, else `null`), never borrowing an older release's, and the "what's in this tour" list is kept only when the tour also carries older releases. 2.17.5's is the one-time Premium banner (`iron` Loggy), with the old Premium page after it: `loadWhatsNewContext` (`utils/whatsNew/context.ts`) supplies it for the auto flow, Settings replay uses `MenstrualCycleContext`, the debug preview assumes all hold, and omitting `context` hides gated pages.
  - Missing releases merge oldest-first into one intro plus at most `WHATS_NEW_MAX_PAGES` (12) content pages, reserving space for the newest releases first. `supersededIn` removes a page/block at that version; empty pages disappear. Done, Skip, X/back and route actions save the record (killing the app mid-tour doesn't). Settings replays the newest live release without changing the record. Choices read stored settings and write immediately on tap, optimistic with failure recovery; untouched choices never write. Workout settings and cardio are nested child modals controlled by `useSubModalState`; route actions save and close before navigating.
  - **To add a release:** add its version-named JSON; register its import in `releases.ts`; add `whatsNew.*` keys in all ten `lang/locales/*/whatsNew.json`; run `generate-lang`, translation/locale checks and the What's new tests. Extend `utils/whatsNew/catalog.ts` only for a new icon, pose, setting or action. An `info` block may name a `pose` (`WHATS_NEW_POSES`: `celebrating`, `iron`, `running`, `writing`, `trophy`, `flex`, `magnify`, `reading`): `WhatsNewPicture` draws that Loggy in place of the icon badge (height is a share of the window, 120–190pt), and falls back to the icon while `show_loggy` is off, so a page that must not read as empty also carries a `featureList` of what the feature does. Every 2.16.0 page uses a pose, and so do the three pages of 2.18.0 (on-device AI: the AI on the phone, meal photos, and "small, so check its work"; no `intro`, since 2.17.5's Iron banner promised to be the last big banner). An `action` block may set `primary: true` for the page's one filled button; `openWorkoutImport` is a modal action (`WorkoutImportModal` rendered inside the tour). `scripts/check-translations.js` scans every `*Key` string in release JSON, so cleanup preserves these keys. **2.15.2.json** holds the pages of 2.13.1 and 2.14.2, which never reached production (last was 2.12.3); **2.16.0.json** introduces Loggy (with `showLoggy`/`animatedLoggy` toggles) and announces import, Your year in Musclog, strength levels and training load. Release versions stay unchanged until release preparation; `check-version-sync` warns non-fatally if a file is ahead of the app. Debug → What's new shows/clears/sets the record and previews releases (its explicit edits reset the session gate).

- **Workout plans and schedule ownership**: `workout_plan_templates` is the only plan-membership store; templates deliberately have no `plan_id` (one workout may belong to several plans). `workout_plans.cycle_type` selects which membership field matters: weekly plans use `week_days_json`; rotating plans use `position` and must clear weekdays.
  - **`WorkoutPlanService.savePlan` is the ONLY way to change an existing plan** (fields + full membership replacement as one aggregate write; no `updatePlan`/`setPlanMemberships` pair, since committing a cycle-type change without clearing weekdays leaves them on a rotating plan). JSON and AI program generation goes through `WorkoutTemplateService.createPlanWithTemplates`, which prepares every child template AND the parent plan and commits them in one `database.batch` (WatermelonDB never rolls back a batch that already landed, so a template-by-template loop that failed on the plan orphaned workouts). `saveTemplate` reads first, then prepares, then commits one batch.
  - `SaveTemplateData.planIds`: `undefined` leaves memberships alone, `[]` unfiles the template — never default it to `[]`. Membership diffs from `saveTemplate` use `WorkoutPlanService.prepareSyncTemplateMemberships`, which returns `{ activePlanIds, records }` (prepared records because `saveTemplate` already owns the writer; plus the membership set after commit). Plan liveness is checked for BOTH halves of the diff (an id being added and one an existing junction row points at), so memberships left by a vanished plan are retired.
  - **Calendar ownership is contextual and never mirrored**: active weekly memberships supply planned weekdays; `schedules` applies only to templates with zero active plan memberships; a rotating or unscheduled planned workout must not fall back to a dormant standalone schedule. **Enforced on write on EVERY membership path**: `saveTemplate` resolves membership _before_ writing schedules (via `prepareSyncTemplateMemberships`, or `getActivePlanIdsForTemplate` when `planIds` is omitted and days would be written) and skips standalone `schedules` if the template will belong to a plan; `createPlan`/`savePlan`/`setTemplatePlans` soft-delete the standalone `schedules` of every workout they file, in the same batch, via `prepareStandaloneScheduleCleanup`. Don't move the schedule block above the membership block (dormant rows would resurrect when the workout leaves the plan).
  - `workout_templates.week_days_json` is deprecated: normal saves clear it, and editing a legacy row materializes its effective days into standalone `schedules`. `workout_logs.plan_id` is stamped at session start but write-only for analytics in this version. Bundled programs create rotating plans and name workouts by day only.
  - The workout library screen stays an orchestrator; filtering, grouped plan sections, empty/loading states and card rendering belong in `components/workout/WorkoutLibraryContent.tsx`, which runs `groupTemplatesByPlan` once and renders flat layouts by passing `plans: []` (no parallel `filteredTemplates` pass). "Which plans is this workout in?" is one flow, `components/workout/usePlanAssignment.tsx`, shared by the library screen and `CreateWorkoutModal`; a plan created from its picker is seeded via `CreateEditPlanModal`'s `initialTemplateIds`, so plan and first membership are one `createPlan` call. That hook's `selectedPlanIds`/`onChange` are OPTIONAL and `onConfirm` reports failure by returning `false`, never by throwing (it's reached from a `Pressable`'s `onPress`, which doesn't catch).
  - **The plan editor's form state is a snapshot, not a subscription.** `hooks/usePlanDraft.ts` seeds it once per `planId` via `WorkoutPlanService.getPlanSnapshot`; `CreateEditPlanModal` holds only presentation. Don't seed from `useWorkoutPlans()` (it re-emits on every membership write and would replace the half-typed form). `initialTemplateIds` is read through a ref at mount (callers build it inline). The load effect is keyed on `planId` alone with a `cancelled` flag.
  - **"Which weekday is this workout due on?" is one read: `WorkoutPlanRepository.getResolvedSchedules()`** (fetches all three calendar stores into `resolveWorkoutSchedules`). `NotificationService.scheduleWorkoutReminders` and `WorkoutService.getUpcomingScheduledWorkouts` must not issue those queries themselves.

- **The session re-targets planned sets, and it must say so.** Between sets, `WorkoutSessionService.saveSet` rewrites the next planned set and PERSISTS it (`useWorkoutSessionState` only reads it back). `selectRetargets` (`utils/intraSessionRetarget.ts`) decides which sets move: `workout_autonomy = manual` re-targets nothing unless the exercise has an explicit progression rule, a set the user typed over (`override`) is left alone, and a skipped set re-targets the next planned set from the last performed working set. The rewrite carries the previous set's actual load over, then recomputes weight or reps (per `progressionMode`) to hold the target reps in reserve. All arithmetic is one pure function, `computeIntraSessionAdjustment` (`utils/setAdjustment.ts`), returning the new values **and** a `SetAdjustment` — a number the app changed on the user's behalf is never presented as their choice. `EnrichedWorkoutLogSet.adjustment` carries it to the UI, where `useAdjustmentMessage` (`components/workout/useAdjustmentMessage.ts`) states it in one shared sentence (live session, preview, Next time, Weekly Review). The "adjusted" marker (accent underline in Inline, calculator icon in Guided, cleared once the user types over) goes on **the fields `adjustedFields(adjustment)` returns** — never inline a cause check. A `target_rir` change is marked on `adjustment.field`, a start adjustment on whatever plan and set now disagree about, and a `carry_over` is deliberately not badged (that load is the user's own).

- **Cycle tracking: encrypted records, one pure model, no phase prescriptions.** Everything personal lives in an encrypted JSON `payload` on `cycle_periods`, `cycle_day_logs`, `cycle_contexts` (v36) plus `menstrual_cycles.config`; only ids and timestamps are plaintext. **Only `database/cyclePayload.ts` and `database/repositories/CycleRepository.ts` touch ciphertext**; everything else sees DTOs (`utils/cycle/types.ts`). The retired plaintext `period_logs` table and `period_flow`/`period_symptoms` metrics are read only by `CycleDataEncryptionMigration` (boot migration, idempotent by data, mandatory recovery backup first, originals **destroyed** in the same batch) and `normalizeCycleDataForImport` for pre-v36 backups; legacy plaintext `menstrual_cycles` columns are blanked and unread. Writes go through `CyclePeriodService`, `CycleDayLogService`, `CycleContextService`, `CycleTrackingService`, `CycleTransferService`; each reads and validates INSIDE its writer (no overlapping periods, one day log per day) and resolves the encryption key before the writer opens. **Encrypt before `prepareCreate`** (assigning after throws).
  - **`utils/cycle/` is pure and is the one model.** `cycleHistory` keeps every cycle and tags those that shouldn't feed predictions (`too_short`, `possible_missed_period`, `user_excluded`, `hormonal_context`, `postpartum_context`, `life_context`) rather than dropping them. `cycleStats` derives confidence from usable cycles AND spread (never raw log count) and owns the prediction window (`window`) and its centre (`predictedLength`, recency-weighted); `medianLength`/`rangeLow`/`rangeHigh` stay the plain facts the UI and appointment summary quote — never centre a prediction on them. Changes must keep `utils/cycle/__tests__/predictionBenchmark.test.ts` passing (hit rate, error AND window width per profile). `cycleTimeline` is the single phase/prediction layout; `cycleSnapshot.buildCycleSnapshot` assembles it for context, reminders and charts.
  - **Phases other than `menstrual` are an ovulation estimate**, so without the fertility opt-in (`showFertility`, default false in `computeCycleTimeline`) the layout is only `menstrual` + `between`. **"Is this period still going?" is one predicate, `isOngoingPeriod`** (`utils/cycle/periodRules.ts`), used by the timeline's phase and the snapshot's `activePeriod`. **The day counter never wraps**: with no new period logged it keeps counting and the prediction becomes `late` (no segments, no phase). **Never compute a date as `start + N * 24h`** — use `addCalendarDays` (`utils/cycle/dates.ts`); across DST the 24h form lands an hour off midnight.
  - **An end date is a fact only when the person gave it.** `endKind` is `open`, `logged`, `estimated` (derived from usual length, only when chosen) or `unknown` (occupies only its start day for overlap). Only `logged` durations feed average period length. `resolveEnd` (`utils/cycle/periodEnd.ts`) is the only place an end is derived.
  - **Context decides which predictions exist** (`contextGuidance`, `utils/cycle/contraception.ts`): a method that usually stops ovulation or no-bleeding tracking suppresses the period prediction, postpartum waits for two periods, the fertile window needs a natural cycle. `CONTRACEPTION_TRAITS` is a `Record` (a new method fails the build until decided). **The fertile-window estimate is opt-in (`cycle_show_fertility`, default off) and always shown with `cycle.fertilityDisclaimer`.**
  - **No phase-based advice anywhere.** No energy labels, hormone cards or intensity multipliers; observations come only from the person's own records (`cycleInsights`: at least three cycles, "X of your last Y", with the days and workout ids behind them, never causal wording). Workout code must not import cycle phase, and the live workout session shows no cycle card (an observation at session start reads as "today will go worse"). The workout observation compares each session only with sessions of the same workout (`WorkoutPoint.workoutKey`) away from period starts, skips Weekly Review lighter-week windows, and needs `MIN_WORKOUT_INSIGHT_CYCLES` (4) comparable cycles.
  - **Pause is not delete.** `MenstrualCycleContext` observes every cycle and splits it into `cycle` (active) and `pausedCycle`; pausing keeps history, `resumeTracking` re-activates, and only a user with no cycle goes through `cycle-setup`. "Delete all cycle data" **destroys** rows (`prepareDestroyPermanently`) because soft-deleted ciphertext is still decryptable.
  - **Privacy surfaces / app lock** (`utils/appLockState.ts` reducer, `AppLockContext`, `AreaLockGate`, `AppLockOverlay`) refuses to enable without a device credential. **What's locked is a set of `AppLockScope`s** (`APP_LOCK_SCOPES`: `app`, `cycle`, `workout`, `nutrition`, `progress`; `AppLockArea` is every scope but `app`), stored comma-separated under the historical key `app_lock_mode` (old `off`/`cycle`/`app` values remain valid serializations). `parseAppLockScopes` is the one reader; `SettingsState` holds only `appLockScopesRaw` and `useSettings().appLockScopes` is parsed in a `useMemo` (primitive-state rule). `scopeRequiresLock(scopes, scope)`: `app` covers every area. **Every screen that shows an area's records is wrapped in `<AreaLockGate area="…">`** (cycle: `cycle.tsx` and its modals; workout: every screen in `app/app/workout/`; nutrition: `nutrition/food.tsx`; progress: `progress.tsx`; Weekly Review holds `workout` and `nutrition`), pinned by `components/__tests__/appLockCoverage.test.ts`. The gate wraps content inside `MasterLayout` so navigation stays reachable; copy per scope is `LOCK_AREA_COPY` (`components/lock/lockAreas.ts`, a `Record`). **Turning any lock on/off requires authenticating first** (`changeLockScope`, `utils/appLockScopeChange.ts`) and the list is read after the prompt. Controls: `AppLockSettings` in Settings → App lock (`AppLockSettingsModal`, hidden on web) and the cycle settings' Privacy section. Not gated by design: Home summary cards, Profile and the AI coach. **`AppLockOverlay` is drawn at the root AND inside `components/theme/Modal`** (a native Modal is its own window) and, with any lock on, draws a blank privacy cover while backgrounded (`isObscured`). **Until lock settings are known, every scope is locked** (`appLockScopesPending`). Every surface rendering cycle records sits behind the cycle lock (`CycleLockGate`; `MenstrualPerformanceChart` returns null while `isLocked('cycle')`). Exports (CSV, PDF summary) are behind the cycle lock and defuse spreadsheet formulas.
  - **Cycle reminders are a contract, planned in one pure pass.** `planCycleReminders` (`utils/cycle/cycleReminders.ts`) plans every kind in `CYCLE_REMINDER_KINDS` (period heads-up, opt-in due day, opt-in neutral "longer than usual" the day after the latest plausible start — never "late", never pregnancy — fertile window, pill, daily check-in, ongoing-period check, backup). Predictions exist only for an estimate the screen would show, so a context change (pregnancy, loss, hormonal method) cancels them on the next reschedule. **All wording comes from `CYCLE_REMINDER_COPY` / `cycleReminderContent`** (`utils/cycle/cycleReminderCopy.ts`) for scheduling AND the settings preview, at three privacy levels (`CycleNotificationStyle`, parsed only via `parseCycleNotificationStyle`): `discreet` (stored name kept, shown as "Private", never says what it's about), `subtle` (names the task, never periods/fertility/pills), `detailed` (states a range, calls it an estimate). The daily check-in (and a continuous pill) is ONE repeating `DAILY` trigger, not 28 one-shots (iOS keeps 64 pending per app, shared with other reminders). A pause (`reminders.pause`) silences everything except `PAUSE_EXEMPT` (the pill); a repeating reminder waits for the reschedule after the pause ends. Cycle reminders use their own Android channel (`cycle`, lock-screen visibility PRIVATE). Permission is asked when a reminder is turned on (`CycleNotificationService.enableCycleReminders`), never at launch. `getCycleDeliveryStatus` feeds the delivery line and test send through ONE `CycleDeliveryState` (`cycleDeliveryState`, `utils/cycle/cycleReminderStatus.ts`: `web`/`unknown`/`noPermission`/`switchedOff`/`ok`); `reminderStatusKind` and `cycleReminderPreviews` are pure there too. `resetCycleSettings` resets every cycle preference in one write (`SettingsService.resetToDefaults`). A tapped reminder opens `/app/cycle?reminder=<kind>` → `ReminderExplainerSheet` ("why did I get this?"). Recording a pregnancy loss offers a 4-week pause (`CycleContextModal`), never applies one silently. Pinned by `utils/cycle/__tests__/cycleReminders.test.ts`, `services/__tests__/NotificationService.test.ts`.
  - **Completion flows.** Optional encrypted-JSON fields include `cycleLengthKnown`, `confirmedLongCycle`, bounded rating definitions, impact/test observations, `reminders`, `backupReminderDays` (no SQL migration needed). A confirmed long cycle bypasses only `possible_missed_period`, never manual, short-interval or physiological-context exclusions. Unknown fallback length suppresses dates only until usable history exists; `cycleTimeline` widens sparse ranges and `historyAnalysis.evaluatePredictions` uses only earlier starts. Confidence is heuristic, not calibrated. Setup context begins today; never infer contraception or pregnancy history from past period dates.
  - **Health provenance and sharing.** `cycleHealth` implementations request cycle permissions only on an explicit read/export action. `CycleHealthImportService` commits a batch of source-ID keyed `observations.health` values; never overwrite self-report or infer period starts from imported flow. `selectedSharing` is an allowlist; date/field selection can't include context, sex, medication, imported health records or unselected notes. Static sharing can't be revoked. `historyAnalysis`/`CycleTrends` work without period anchors and omit missing values rather than treating them as zero.
  - **Manual recovery boundary.** `CycleTransferService.import(null, ...)` creates the cycle and records atomically; never create a placeholder cycle before validating a file. External CSV mapping is explicit and rejects ambiguous dates/flow labels. Backups remain manual: encrypted file creation/sharing requires a user action; reminders never create files or upload. Don't add automatic cloud upload, cloud credentials or a portable cloud-enable flag without a new user request. "Prepared" backup status isn't proof the file was saved off-device.
  - **Education and reminders.** `careGuidance` links are NHS/RCOG education, not diagnosis or triage; symptom/impact presets aren't a validated PMDD scale. Never infer ovulation from an LH entry or basal/skin temperature alone. Daily reminders stay optional even with hidden/suppressed predictions; the pill reminder renews its 28-day horizon of one-shots (skipping pill-free days) on every foreground, and the last one planned says to open the app (`renewalDue`), so the series never ends in silence; period/fertility reminders still need a meaningful visible estimate. Keep app.json's native health permissions and committed manifests/usage descriptions synchronized.
  - **Deleted cycle text doesn't linger; recovery points stay encrypted.** A cleared day log and a removed context are destroyed (`prepareDestroyPermanently`, no undo). A removed period stays soft-deleted for the in-session "Undo"; the boot task `CycleRecordPurge.purgeDeleted` destroys it next launch. Exports drop soft-deleted cycle rows. Automatic on-device recovery points (`createLiveBackupJson`, web `executeLiveBackup`, web pre-migration dump) pass `keepCycleCiphertext`; downloading one goes through `exportPortableRecoveryPoint` / `decryptCyclePayloadsInDump` so it restores anywhere. `CycleDataEncryptionMigration` deletes its own pre-conversion backup (a copy of the plaintext it removes) once every converted record reads back. Pinned in `database/services/__tests__/cycleRecords.integration.test.ts`.
  - **`MenstrualCycleProvider` is the one owner of cycle data, mood included.** It exposes the snapshot's own fields (`timeline`, `predictionStats`, `activePeriod`, …) with no aliases; `today` is local midnight from `hooks/useLocalToday.ts` (a `useSyncExternalStore` that changes only when the calendar day does). Shared mood is observed once there (`moods`, `moodsLoading`, `moodsError`) — don't call `useCycleMoods` from a component. Payloads are schema-checked on decrypt (`CycleRepository`); shapes (`PeriodPayload`, `DayLogPayload`, `ContextPayload`) are declared there once.
  - **Write and observer boundaries.** Existing models are prepared inside `commitPrepared`, with all reads and encryption done before preparation, so adapter rejection restores cached state. The legacy encryption conversion and permanent cycle deletion each use ONE adapter batch/transaction even above `MAX_BATCH_OPERATIONS` (chunking would leave partial state); the migration reads its snapshot inside the writer after the recovery backup and key priming. `MenstrualCycleProvider` captures config ciphertext in immutable observer state (WatermelonDB reuses model objects). `updateConfig` accepts a `CycleConfigPatch` or a synchronous updater returning one; the service reads and merges the latest encrypted config inside the writer, including partial nested reminder edits. UI sends only changed fields, and list add/remove/toggle uses the updater's current config, not a render snapshot.
  - **Journal preferences and portability.** Goals, custom symptom names, quick-log favorites/dismissed suggestions, enabled daily fields and custom field definitions live in encrypted `menstrual_cycles.config` (optional fields; no schema migration); observations (including archived custom labels) stay in each encrypted day payload. Legacy bundled favorites may still be read from `cycle_quick_log`; new choices persist in encrypted config, never plaintext settings. Quick actions use `CycleDayLogService.patch` to merge against the latest day inside its writer; patches preserve observations; zero and false are observations, not emptiness. Mood remains the shared `user_metrics` mood: `CycleDayLogService.save(input, mood)` commits both atomically (`undefined` leaves mood alone, `null` clears that day's moods). Full cycle deletion keeps shared moods and says so. `useCycleMoods` observes shared edits for comparisons/exports.
  - **Cycle-only files.** `cycleTransfer` owns the versioned JSON/CSV parser and strict date/field validation; `cycleMerge` owns preview/commit conflict and overlap rules; `CycleTransferService` re-reads inside its writer and commits once without resetting other tables. Dates travel as YYYY-MM-DD. CSV has supplementary JSON columns for symptom keys and observations, plus mood; readable exports have no password protection. `backupCrypto` authenticates encrypted cycle files before decryption (PBKDF2-SHA256, independent AES/HMAC keys, random salt/IV) — don't use the device-value `encrypt` shortcut. Last-backup preparation time is device-local, excluded from full-app backups.
  - **Comparisons and no bleeding.** `cycleComparison` uses logged starts only (cycle-day alignment and up to 14 days before the next actual start; no predicted phase alignment). Missing values differ from explicit none and unrated presence. No-bleeding mode hides period actions and predictions but retains unexpected bleeding/spotting inputs. Custom symptom labels resolve through `cycleSymptomLabel` everywhere.
  - Pinned by `utils/cycle/__tests__/`, `utils/__tests__/appLockState.test.ts`, `database/services/__tests__/cycleRecords.integration.test.ts` (real in-memory database and encryption). See `docs/FIXES.md`, `docs/RESEARCH.md`.

- **Offline notifications are planned, not repeated.** `utils/notificationPolicy.ts` (pure) owns every rule about whether and when a local notification may exist (quiet hours, pause, reminder lead time, accountability style, the 14-day workout-reminder window, the nightly overview window, the weekly-goal-at-risk test). `refreshAllNotifications()` (`services/notificationRefresh.ts`) re-plans all of it on boot, every foreground and after a workout. Planners are split by domain — `NotificationService` (workout, goal-at-risk, live session), `NutritionNotificationService` (overview, check-ins, weekly review), `CycleNotificationService` (cycle reminders, own queue) — sharing `cancelScheduledByType`/`loadNotificationPreferences` from `services/notificationScheduling.ts`; don't grow `NotificationService` back past 1k lines or re-inline the cancel-by-`data.type` loop. Reminders are one-off `DATE` triggers, never repeating `WEEKLY`/`DAILY` (a repeating trigger can't skip a day already trained, honour a pause or leave quiet hours; the cycle pill reminder is the precedent). Preferences are ONE JSON setting (`SettingsService.get/updateNotificationPreferences`, merged in the service so quick taps can't clobber each other), not in `SettingsState`. The live session — rest alert, active-workout banner, unfinished-workout nudge — is exempt from quiet hours and pause. Android channels and action categories are named once in `constants/notificationActions.ts`; the rest alert has its own channel and is the only `timeSensitive` notification. Action buttons (`+30s`, `Snooze`) are handled in `utils/notifications.ts`; the unfinished-workout reminder is deliberately NOT cancelled on screen unmount and is cleared by `clearActiveWorkoutLogId`. Permission is requested in context (`RestAlertsPrompt`, after the first completed set), once. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/notificationPolicy.test.ts`, `services/__tests__/NotificationService.test.ts`.
- **Notification buttons, snoozes and explainers** — three rules:
  1. **Cycle buttons are neutral on purpose**: the pill category is _Done / Later / Skip_ (`NOTIFICATION_CATEGORIES.cyclePill`), every other cycle reminder has _Snooze_ (`cycleReminder`) — an action row reading "Taken" would give away a discreet reminder. Done/Skip go through `CycleNotificationService.recordPillAnswer` (waits for the database; merges into today's page inside `CycleDayLogService.patch`; writes `observations.pill` `'taken' | 'skipped'` — never write the answer elsewhere). Later/Snooze re-send the content as a one-shot of type `menstrual-cycle-snooze` (`CYCLE_SNOOZE_NOTIFICATION_TYPE`), **not** `menstrual-cycle`, so the reschedule that replaces ordinary reminders doesn't erase it; that same pass re-checks each snooze with `isSnoozeStillValid` (`utils/cycle/cycleReminders.ts`; a pause silences every kind but the pill, and an estimate is dropped once the prediction is `hidden`/`suppressed`) and reuses the single `getAllScheduledNotificationsAsync` read. Both types route to the cycle screen.
  2. **A workout reminder carries its own explanation**: `scheduleWorkoutReminders` puts `dayIndex`, `reminderTime`, `leadMinutes` and `fromPlan` in `data`; the tap handler turns them into route params with `workoutReminderRoute`, and `parseWorkoutReminderParams` (both in `utils/workoutReminderExplainer.ts`) reads them back for `WorkoutReminderExplainerSheet` (tolerating older reminders that carry only the template). The day/time are the _plan's_, not the lead-adjusted fire time. Its exits go through `SettingsService.updateNotificationPreferences` / `setNotificationsWorkoutReminders` then `refreshAllNotifications`; its `NotificationsSettingsModal` is rendered inside the sheet (`local/no-sibling-modals`). A plan membership has no reminder time (08:00 default), so there's deliberately no "change time" button.
  3. **Rest alerts have three independent settings** (`restAlertSound`, `restHapticStrength`, `restHeadsUpSeconds`; `SETTINGS` definitions mirrored in `SettingsState`): the chime is a channel choice on Android (`rest-timer` vs `rest-timer-quiet`; a channel's sound is fixed at creation) and `content.sound` on iOS; the heads-up is `restPhase(remaining, total, headsUp)` + a `'headsUp'` `hapticBeat` + (backgrounded) a quiet notification (`REST_TIMER_HEADS_UP_NOTIFICATION_ID`) cancelled with the alert and skipped when the rest isn't longer than `headsUp + REST_HEADS_UP_MIN_GAP_SECONDS`. Pass the heads-up to `hapticBeat` only for a rest longer than the window, as `restPhase` gates it. **The work timer** (`hooks/useWorkTimer.ts`, `utils/workTimer.ts`, `WorkTimerBar`) counts down a timed set separately from rest; reaching zero calls `onFinish`, and if that started a rest the hook remembers it so the next set completion passes `autoRest: false` (`consumeRestHandoff`). Its time's-up notification (`WORK_TIMER_NOTIFICATION_ID`) rides the rest-alert switch and sound choice.
  - Pinned by `services/__tests__/NotificationService.test.ts`, `utils/__tests__/{notificationResponse,workTimer,workoutReminderExplainer,restTimer}.test.ts`, `hooks/__tests__/useWorkTimer.test.ts`, and the snooze cases in `utils/cycle/__tests__/cycleReminders.test.ts`. Deferred work is in `docs/FUTURE_FEATURES.md` → _Notification follow-ups_.
- **Weekly Progress Check-ins**: managed via `NutritionCheckinService`. Periodic targets are generated upon goal setting. Trends use 7-day rolling data (weight, calories, activity).
- **Gym profiles, plates and exercise restrictions** — three things that look like settings and aren't:
  - `gyms` holds a named equipment set (kilograms like every weight; bar, increment and plate weights cross persistence through `toStoredGymWeightKg`, seven decimals to preserve quarter-pound inputs; `plates_json`/`equipment_json` are raw strings parsed in exactly one place, `snapshotFromGymRow` in `utils/gym.ts`, so a hand-edited or newer row degrades to defaults instead of throwing mid-set). The active gym is the primitive setting `ACTIVE_GYM_ID_SETTING_TYPE` (`activeGymId` on `SettingsState`); `hooks/useActiveGym.ts` joins it with observed gyms, so a deleted gym reads as "none".
  - **Plate math is not greedy**: `platesPerSide` (`utils/plateCalculator.ts`) is a bounded knapsack (one pair each of 20, 15, 10 kg for 25 kg a side: greedy takes 20 and is stuck 5 short; the answer is 15 + 10). Plates load in PAIRS (a stored count of 3 is one pair). `roundToLoadable` is the ONE rounding rule for loads, with or without a gym (takes the resolved `units`): without a gym a bar-loaded lift moves in pairs of the smallest standard plate (2.5 kg / 5 lb); anything else is only tidied to input precision (0.5 kg / 1 lb) — snapping dumbbells to the 2.5 kg grid invented equipment (12 kg → 15, +25%). `loadStepFor`'s `roundLoad` delegates to it, warm-ups load from `defaultGymSnapshot(units)`, and `weightInputStep` keeps the legacy 0.5 step. **Use `StepperInput`, not `NewNumericalInput`, for plate and bar weights** (`NewNumericalInput` is whole numbers only; plates come in 1.25).
  - **Restrictions** (`exercise_restrictions`; kinds `never` > `injury` > `dislike`) have one live row per exercise (`ExerciseRestrictionService.set` replaces); an injury's `expires_at` makes it inactive without deleting (`isRestrictionActive`; the manager shows "healed"). Precedence is in `exerciseExclusionReason` (`utils/exerciseSubstitution.ts`): never/injury, then equipment the gym lacks, exclude; a dislike only demotes and flags. **`ExerciseSuggestionService` is the one answer to "what may the app suggest right now?"** — swap list, AI plan prompt and AI plan response all use it. The prompt is filtered AND the response re-checked (a model can ignore its list): a restricted exercise in a generated plan is swapped for the best same-region substitute the plan doesn't already contain, or dropped. The swap list filters in SQL (`ExerciseListFilters.muscleGroups/equipmentTypes/excludeIds`) so pages stay full, which is why `MAX_EXERCISE_RESTRICTIONS` equals `MAX_QUERY_IDS`.
  - **A swap changes only the session**: `WorkoutLogExerciseService.replaceLogExercise` rewrites a wholly planned log block, or splits pending sets into a new block once any set is performed/skipped (`replaced_exercise_id` keeps the ORIGINAL exercise across repeated swaps, `substitution_reason` records why), and resets only still-planned sets to the new exercise's load (a planned 80 kg bench isn't an 80 kg fly); performed and skipped sets are history. "Just today" and "machine occupied" persist nothing; pain and dislike merely PRE-SELECT an opt-in toggle to remember the choice — never apply it silently. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{gym,plateCalculator,loadIncrement,exerciseSubstitution}.test.ts` and service tests beside them.
- **Warm-ups, start-of-workout progression and the rest timer.** A set has two independent axes: lifecycle (`completion_status`) and kind (`set_type`). `isWorkingSet` (`utils/workoutSetCompletion.ts`: performed AND not `'warmup'`) is the predicate for "how hard did they train" — volume (`WorkoutLog.calculateVolume`, `calculateSessionVolumeFromSets`), PRs, e1RM, the six `WorkoutAnalytics` queries (which also filter `set_type != 'warmup'` in SQL), progression's `lastSets`, muscle-group set counts, LLM summaries — while `isPerformedWorkoutSet` stays for "what did they do" (history, calories, Health Connect deliberately KEEP warm-ups). In the live session a warm-up is never the "previous set" a working set is re-targeted from, and a planned warm-up is never re-targeted. `set_order` is ONE ordering key for the whole workout, so `WorkoutLogExerciseService.addWarmupSets` shifts every later set (other exercises included) in the same batch; WatermelonDB's `prepareUpdate` mutates immediately, so read the anchor's order into a local before building operations.
  - **Start-of-workout progression** (`WorkoutProgressionService.applyStartProgression`, called by `WorkoutService.startWorkoutFromTemplate` after the template is copied) is gated by `workout_autonomy` (`manual` copies the plan verbatim; `suggest`, the default, progresses). It reads previous sessions in parallel BEFORE its writer (holding the write lock across per-exercise queries blocks every other write), reads and writes the sets it rewrites in ONE writer, skips a workout that has begun or was already progressed (safe twice), and is **best effort — a failure must never stop a workout from starting**. The history/projection boundary is `WorkoutProgressionService` (`projectAdHocExercise` for Add/Swap, `projectTemplate` for preview), both using `projectExerciseProgression` and `computeStartTarget`; `progressesLoad` is the ONE check that keeps cardio, mobility and stretching unprogressed.
  - Coaching rules living in that pair only: a deload needs **two** sessions in a row at the same load that failed — average rated RIR 0 for the automatic path (`failedBefore`), below `repMin` for double-progression and top-set rules (`missedAtSameLoad`) — the first miss holds. `reps_first` climbs from the PLANNED reps (`repsFirstRange`: plan … plan + 2), falling back to generic 8–12 / 12–16 only when `plannedFromHistory` says the "plan" is a copy of last session (Add/Swap). The automatic path progresses when last session's average rated RIR met the live target (`DEFAULT_TARGET_REPS_IN_RESERVE`, 2) AND planned reps were hit. A session whose effort was never rated falls back to double progression on reps alone (`computeUnratedTarget`; holds when there's no plan to judge against) and attaches a `range` rule detail so the explanation never claims an unrecorded RIR. "Last session" means the latest COMPLETED WORKOUT: history orders by the parent log's `completed_at` (a log exercise added later has a fresh `created_at`).
