The Jest suites mock llama.rn and a newer desktop llama-server accepts the template as it is, so check a real model file on the llama.cpp build llama.rn bundles whenever either changes): given the schema prompt it copied the prompt's first example for nearly every sentence ("Hello my name is Pablo" became "copy yesterday's breakfast"), and in its own format it does far better (`docs/RESEARCH.md` → Voice commands on the downloaded model). Its tools, example turns and reply conversion are `utils/voiceCommand/nativeTools.ts` (`NATIVE_VOICE_TOOL_ORDER` puts `search_food` first, since the model leans to the first tool; `buildNativeVoiceTools(offered, chosen?, model?)` builds the tools, `nativeVoiceMessages(offered, chosen?, model?)` what comes before the sentence and `nativeVoiceExamples(offered)` the preamble plus example turns of the offered tools only, the preamble's refusal saying "I can only log food." only while every offered tool is about food; both are derived from `VOICE_TOOLS`, where each tool's `native` entry holds its position, example turns, description, slots and, where the 1.2B measured better with its own, `textModel` wording); **these were measured as sets, so change them only with a re-measurement** (`nativeTools.test.ts` freezes the 450M's two-tool payload, the default three-tool order, whose weigh-in example comes first, and the 1.2B's instruction and `copy_meal`). **The two downloaded models get different payloads** (`ProviderPolicy.nativeVoiceModel`, `'text'` once the 1.2B is installed): the 450M is shown example turns; the 1.2B is given one system line (`TEXT_MODEL_INSTRUCTION`) and its own `copy_meal` wording on a first try, and the example turns only for a tool the person picked. **The 1.2B's chat template renders only `content` and ignores `tool_calls`**, so its example turns reached it as empty replies until `toEngineMessages` began writing the calls into `content` in the model's own `<|tool_call_start|>[name(key="value")]<|tool_call_end|>` form for such a template (`templateDropsToolCalls`: it lists tools and never reads `tool_calls`; the 450M's reads them and its request is unchanged). Read a model's rendered prompt (llama-server `/apply-template`) before benchmarking few-shot turns on it. The engine mapping (`toEngineMessages`, `toEngineTools`, `readToolCalls`) is `services/localModels/localToolMessages.ts`, shared by the native runtime and its web bridge (which only serves the 450M and passes no template); a local request takes tools or a schema, never both. The pure `utils/voiceCommand/` does the rest for both shapes: `types.ts` (the type model, `MAX_VOICE_COMMANDS` 1), `tools.ts` (the `VOICE_TOOLS` registry: `copy_meal`, `search_food`, `log_weight`, `log_meal`, `add_note`, `go_to`, plus the non-actions `none` and `cant_determine_tool`; each action carries an `effect` — `navigate`, `prefill` or `confirm` — and none writes anything), `schema.ts` (per dialect, flat slots, never a union, only the offered tools' slots; each slot is a `choice`, `text` or `number` and the parser reads it from that definition), `parse.ts` (a tool that was not offered is `cant_determine_tool`), `prompt.ts` (at most 2,000 characters for any five tools, for Apple's on-device model; each tool brings its own `promptLine` and `promptExamples`), `resolve.ts` and `toolSelection.ts`. **Which tools a request offers is the person's choice cut to the model**: `SETTINGS.voiceCommandTools` (a comma list in pick order, AI behavior → Voice tools, `VoiceToolsSheet`; unset means `DEFAULT_VOICE_TOOL_SELECTION`, so new tools reach people who never chose; backed up and never cut on write) and `ProviderPolicy.maxVoiceTools` (the 450M 3; the 1.2B, Apple's model and a custom local server 5; cloud all: measured, the 450M falls apart at five tools while the 1.2B with its own payload holds up; Apple and local servers are not measured; `activeVoiceTools` takes the first picks and offers them in registry order). **Two checks hold for every provider, because a small model fills slots from its examples**: a slot whose value is outside its choices drops the whole command (`parse.ts`; the downloaded model writes `from_day: "tomorrow"` as filler, and reading it as "not said" turned it into an action), and a `search_food` whose food the person never said is `cantDetermine` (`isFoodSaid`, `utils/voiceCommand/grounding.ts`: accents folded, one food word matching a spoken word, plural and singular both ways, no Unicode property escapes). A "food" that is only a meal's name (`mealOfWord`, `utils/voiceCommand/mealWords.ts`: the app's `food.meals.*` labels in every bundled language plus a few spoken-only synonyms) becomes a copy review with no source day. **A weigh-in is read from the transcript, never from the model** (`resolveLogWeight` with `utils/voiceCommand/spokenWeight.ts`): the sentence holds one number, the model's must agree with it, it is 20–400 kg, a unit counts only when said next to it (else the account's); the action carries kilograms and `VoiceWeightSheet` shows and takes the account's unit only (never a unit picker), and the sentence must name a unit or a word for weighing ("I'm Maria and I'm 30" opened a weigh-in without that). A spoken meal's description is the whole transcript; a note keeps the model's words only when at least 80% of them were said (`isMostlySaid`), else the transcript; `go_to` always offers `cycle` (leaving it out would tell a cloud provider whether the person tracks a cycle) and the provider checks availability afterwards. When the downloaded model writes a key twice, `nativeVoiceReply` keeps the last valid value (never "today" over a real source day) and drops a meal of "other" from a tool whose `native.dropsOtherMeal` is set. Do not strip punctuation from the transcript: it was measured and routed worse (`docs/RESEARCH.md`). After the sheet a voice command opened closes (`useVoiceTask`, `hooks/useVoiceActions.ts`; for `go_to` at once, `useWrongTaskNotice`), a "wrong task?" snackbar links to AI settings (`aiSettingsHref`, `constants/aiSettingsRoute.ts`; Settings opens the sheet from the `openAi` param), never while the sheet is up; a saved weigh-in or logged meal shows its own message instead. The model answers with relative day words and code turns them into calendar days (a weekday is the latest one before the target day; a copy whose source is not earlier than its target is refused); the model never writes anything. **Every rejection** (`none`, `cant_determine_tool`, an unreadable reply, a missing or unsaid food, a bad source day) opens `VoiceCommandModal`'s picker: one row per OFFERED tool (`titleKey`, `labelKey`; the outcome carries `offered`) plus "Ask the coach". Picking one calls `interpretVoiceCommand(transcript, { tool })`, which offers the model only that tool (native: `buildNativeVoiceTools(offered, tool)` with the same examples; other providers: a one-tool schema enum and a prompt line) and **always ends in that tool's action**: `parseChosenCommand` ignores the tool name and unusable slots, a source day that is not earlier is dropped (the review asks), and a food that was not said opens an empty search. That leniency is only for a tool the person picked, never for the first request. Provider failures stay error snackbars; a cancel is silent, and a generation token ignores a late answer (Apple's on-device engine cannot be aborted). **Where an action goes** (`VoiceCommandProvider`): `copyMeal`, `searchFood` and `addNote` go into `voiceActionInbox.ts` (deliberately not an open-link kind, so no outside link can trigger one) and the app navigates to the food diary or Notes, where `useVoiceActions(kinds, …)` on the focused screen takes **only its own kinds** (`takePendingVoiceAction(kinds)`; a focused diary once could take another screen's command) and a new `submit` clears the inbox. The diary (`useDiaryVoiceActions`, called above the nutrition lock) shows the day and opens the copy-day review scoped to the meal (`CopyDayFromHistoryModal`'s `initialSourceDate`, `sourceMealType`, `targetMealType`) or the food search with `initialQuery`; Notes opens its editor with the body filled in. `goTo` navigates directly. `logWeight` and `logMeal` are confirmed **in the provider**, as modals nested inside `VoiceCommandModal` (never siblings): `VoiceWeightSheet` writes exactly one weight metric on Save (`UserMetricService.createMetric`; not `AddUserMetricEntryModal`, which also writes defaults for body fat, height and mood), and the spoken meal runs `trackMealFromVoice` (`useMealTextEstimate` over `useCameraAiProcessing`; the Medium steps on every provider and level, the free tier included: recipe, name match against the person's own and foundation foods, estimate of the rest; each request keeps the person's effort; `config.redactDump` keeps the transcript out of the debug dump) and opens `LogMealModal`, which logs through `logTrackedMeal` and shows that day in the diary. Nothing is written without a tap. **Adding a tool**: a name in `VOICE_ACTION_TOOLS`, an entry in `VOICE_TOOLS` (`effect`, `titleKey`, `labelKey`, `promptLine`, `promptExamples`, `native` position, example turns, description and slots), a `VoiceAction` kind handled by the provider (and a screen's `useVoiceActions` if it goes through the inbox), a new slot in `SLOTS` only if the existing ones do not fit, a place in `DEFAULT_VOICE_TOOL_SELECTION`, translations, and a re-measurement of the downloaded models' routing with the new tool offered (`docs/RESEARCH.md`). `/app/test/debug` has a typed "Voice command" field that takes the same path without a microphone. An unreadable reply is `voice-command-unusable` in Sentry; the transcript is never reported, and both request shapes pass `redactDump`, so it stays out of the opt-in debug dump too. `RECORD_AUDIO` is declared and the privacy policy lists the microphone; Data safety and the App Store labels are unchanged because the audio is neither stored nor sent. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/voiceInput.test.ts`, `hooks/__tests__/{useHoldToTranscribe,useLockedHoldGuards,useVoiceActions}.test.ts`, `components/modals/__tests__/VoiceWeightSheet.dom.test.tsx`, `components/settings/__tests__/VoiceToolsSheet.dom.test.tsx`, `components/camera/__tests__/useMealTextEstimate.dom.test.tsx`, `utils/__tests__/coachAIVoiceMeal.test.ts`, `components/navigation/__tests__/{VoiceListeningOverlay,VoiceTranscriptModal}.dom.test.tsx`, `utils/__tests__/{unusedPermissions,iosPrivacyConfig}.test.ts`, `utils/voiceCommand/__tests__/`, `services/__tests__/voiceCommand.test.ts`, `utils/__tests__/coachAINativeToolCall.test.ts`, `services/localModels/__tests__/{localToolMessages,llamaRuntime}.test.ts`, `components/modals/__tests__/{VoiceCommandModal,VoiceCommandProvider,CopyDayFromHistoryModal}.dom.test.tsx`, `hooks/__tests__/{useDiaryVoiceActions,useFoodScreenModals}.test.ts`, `utils/__tests__/{aiReadiness,browserVoice}.test.ts`.
- **Mood tracking**: daily mood prompt (`SHOW_DAILY_MOOD_PROMPT_SETTING_TYPE`). Mood is correlated with calories, workout volume and macros in progress charts.
- **Home Screen Widgets**: Android/iOS widgets use `expo-widgets` in `widgets/`. `WidgetSync` owns readiness, subscriptions, foreground/day refresh, localization and palette snapshots; renderers never read the database or app context. Android must keep `enableAndroid: true` (the SDK 58 config plugin defaults to disabled). `withWidgetDeepLinks` runs after Expo generates providers (listed before `expo-widgets` because dangerous mods execute in reverse) and handles shortcut taps natively after process death. Root buttons must remain the first interaction target. An Android `CircularProgressIndicator` is a 32dp ProgressBar that ignores `strokeWidth`, so the ring's size/look is `plugins/withWidgetProgressRing.js` overriding peek-glance's `peek_determinate_circular_progress` layout and drawable by name; keep committed copies identical to the plugin output (`plugins/__tests__/withWidgetProgressRing.test.ts`). iOS uses SwiftUI links and an App Group; initial-layout modules need relative imports (Expo's isolated registry stubs project aliases). Clear persistent snapshots and invalidate in-flight reads before data reset/restore; Android placements must be re-added after the migration. **Widget snapshots must never contain `null`** (`expo-widgets` writes App Group `UserDefaults`, which rejects `NSNull` and throws from `createWidget`, crashing iOS at boot — `docs/FIXES.md`); use `''`, `0` or a boolean such as `hasGoal`. A widget layout function is serialized, so it may use only its imports and locals (which is why each Android layout repeats the same `Button` root); `environment.widgetFamily` picks the small/medium iOS layout. **Layouts branch on a snapshot's `state` discriminant** (`ready`/`stale` Nutrition; `day`/`private`/`stale` Cycle), never an empty string; a snapshot carries no field no layout reads. `publishDaily` in `WidgetSync` is the one place the iOS midnight-expiry timeline is built.
  - **The Cycle widget is read-only and shows only the day count.** `widgets/cycleWidgetSnapshot.ts` (pure) decides what it may say, `widgets/cycleWidgetData.ts` reads it. It shows the cycle day under the `Musclog` brand line, nothing else (no estimate, never the word "cycle"), and does **not** follow `cycleNotificationStyle` (someone who adds the widget asked to see it). A home screen is readable without unlocking, so **a cycle or whole-app lock hides the day** (`cycleWidgetMayShowDay`; not `scopeRequiresLock`, which answers "does this screen ask") and the widget becomes the `private` "Musclog / Tap to open" shortcut. The loader reads the lock first and the encrypted records **only when the day may be shown**, building the timeline with predictions off. A failed read publishes the private snapshot, never previous values; the iOS timeline expires the day count at midnight. It refreshes on writes to `menstrual_cycles`/`cycle_periods`/`cycle_contexts` (`withChangesForTables`) and when the lock changes. Tapping opens `/app/cycle` via `open-cycle`. **Don't add logging buttons** (a home-screen tap would write encrypted cycle data past the lock). Needs `npm run prebuild`. Pinned by `widgets/__tests__/cycleWidget{Snapshot,Data}.test.ts`.
  - **The Activity Heatmap widget is the Home heatmaps' `compact` card at its smallest** (Android 2×1; iOS `systemSmall`, the shortest home-screen family, plus `systemMedium`): two labelled rows of the last 30 days (workouts, nutrition) and no title row on Android; iOS small wraps each source into three rows of ten (`environment.widgetFamily`), no setting to choose between them, independent of `home_heatmaps`. `widgets/activityWidgetSnapshot.ts` (pure) turns days into levels with the SAME `buildActivityStrip`, `WORKOUT_HEATMAP_MAX_VALUE`/`NUTRITION_HEATMAP_MAX_VALUE` and `ACTIVITY_LEVEL_OPACITY` (now in `utils/activityHeatmap.ts`) so a day is the same shade in both; `widgets/activityWidgetData.ts` reads through `ActivityHeatmapRepository.fetchWorkoutDays`/`fetchMealDays` (plaintext columns only) and refreshes on writes to `workout_logs`/`nutrition_logs`. The snapshot carries levels (0–4) per day plus `levelColors` composed with `widgetColorWithAlpha` (neither runtime has an opacity modifier). After midnight iOS shows `expiredActivityWidgetSnapshot` (each row shifts one day with an empty new day — true at 00:00, so no refresh-prompt fallback). Like Nutrition and unlike Cycle, it ignores app locks (days with activity, never a figure from encrypted records). A square is a `RoundedRectangle` on iOS; on Android a fixed `size(4, 7)` `Box` with `background` and a 2dp `Spacer` between squares, none trailing (no weights/gap arrangement, so the block is centred in a wider cell; Glance containers take at most 10 children, so each source is two rows of 15, each three groups of five (30 squares in one row would need a 3-cell widget); `widgets/__tests__/androidWidgetLayout.test.ts` checks the 110dp minimum width and one cell of height). Tapping opens Home via `open-home` (`/app`). Needs `npm run prebuild` (Android half committed; iOS target regenerates on a Mac). Pinned by `widgets/__tests__/activityWidget{Snapshot,Data}.test.ts`.
- **Home summary card choice**: the top of Home shows either `DailySummaryCard` or `WeeklyStreakCard` per `HOME_SUMMARY_CARD_SETTING_TYPE` (`'daily_summary' | 'weekly_streak'`). The streak card's current streak counts consecutive calendar days with a logged food ending **yesterday**, timezone-aware via `NutritionService.getMacroLoggingStreak()`. The best streak is the longest current streak ever observed, persisted in `MACRO_STREAK_STATE` and only ever grown. `utils/macroStreak.ts` (`getMacroStreak`, via `useMacroStreak`) recomputes at most once per local day. `WeeklyStreakCard` takes workout progress from `useWeeklyWorkoutProgress` (completed workouts by `completed_at` over the rolling 7 local days ending today; goal dots only with an active consistency goal).
  - **The stat strip under it (`components/home/DailyHomeFooter.tsx`) is gated by `SHOW_HOME_STEPS_SETTING_TYPE`, and its content is data: `buildDailyStatCells`** in `components/home/dailyHomeStats.ts`. Either half can be missing independently (steps need the platform health sync and permission; energy cells need a calorie goal), so it renders whatever remains. Copy is discrete `home.dailyStats.*` labels. **`DailyStatCell.labelKey` is a whole key typed `` `home.dailyStats.${string}` ``**, never a suffix the renderer completes (invisible to `check-translations`). **Only the balance cell is tinted** (`tone`: `good` for deficit, `caution` for surplus); every energy cell states its `kcal` unit, steps doesn't; labels wrap to two lines. `EnergyBalance` is owned by `utils/energyBalance.ts`. **Which cell is tappable is the component's business**: steps is the only one that goes anywhere (don't reintroduce an `interactive` flag on `DailyStatCell`; give a cell a real `onPress` if a second becomes tappable). Pinned by `components/home/__tests__/dailyHomeStats.test.ts`.
  - **`DailyHomeFooter` derives everything it shows** — `useDailySteps`, `useCircadianBurn`, `computeEnergyBalance` and the metric-history modal the step count opens. The route hands it only the day, the user's TDEE and `consumedKcal` (`null` withholds the energy half — intuitive-eating mode or no goal).
  - **Today's nutrition has ONE owner on Home: `app/app/(tabs)/index.tsx` calls `useDailyNutritionSummary` once** and passes `DailyNutritionSummary` into `DailyHomeSummary` (two live queries with per-row decryption; the strip and the card both need it). Read the goal off the summary rather than calling `useCurrentNutritionGoal` again. The `visible` gate is `homeSummaryCard !== 'weekly_streak' || showHomeSteps`. `components/home/__tests__/homeNutritionOwnership.test.ts` fails if either child re-subscribes.
  - **The strip's layout is `components/StatStrip.tsx`, shared with the daily summary card's weekly-average macros** (figures divided by hairlines, number first, label under). It takes already-formatted values and a `StatStripPalette` and reads no theme, so one layout serves a plain card (`background.card` + `border.light`) and the hero gradient (`colorfulCard.panel` + `colorfulCard.ink30`). Don't re-inline the divider or container (`components/__tests__/statStrip.test.ts`). **`colorfulCard.panel` washes away from the card's own ink** (towards the scrim under light ink, towards white under dark ink), so it can only raise contrast; the direction is derived from the ink, not `colorfulCardUsesSurfaceInk` (Volt and Varia set it `false` yet have near-black ink). Pinned by `utils/__tests__/dailySummaryCardTheme.test.ts`. `DailyStatCell` stays home-specific; `DailyHomeFooter` maps it to `StatStripItem`s.
  - **Tapping steps opens `UserMetricDataModal` with `metricType="daily_steps"` — a structural filter, not a search seed** (seeding the raw type name never matched the translated label and opened empty in every locale). `metricType` is pushed into the query (`UserMetricService.getMetricsHistory` takes a type); `searchQuery` is what the user types. Metric rows need a `bodyMetrics.metrics.<type>` label in every locale.
  - **The foreground steps refresh is throttled in `services/dailyStepsRefresh.ts`**: `refreshDailyStepsIfStale()` owns the `STEPS_REFRESH_INTERVAL_MS` clock, shares an in-flight sync between callers, and records its timestamp on **success only** (stamping first made one declined permission suppress retries for 15 minutes). Covered by `services/__tests__/dailyStepsRefresh.test.ts`.
  - **"Burned" is energy spent SO FAR today, never the whole day's TDEE, enforced by the argument name**: `computeEnergyBalance` takes `burnedKcal`, not `tdee`, so a full-day figure can't be passed (at 8am it once read `Burned 2781 · Eaten 0 · Deficit 2781`). The elapsed figure comes from the circadian model, rounded at the call site in `app/app/(tabs)/index.tsx`.
- **The circadian expenditure model has one implementation: `utils/circadianBurn.ts`, ticked by `hooks/useCircadianBurn.ts`.** `BLOCK_FRACTIONS` (`constants/circadian.ts`) splits TDEE across six 240-minute phases; `CIRCADIAN_SEGMENTS` lays them out in minutes-since-midnight order, with `earlySlеep` appearing **twice** (its block straddles midnight). `circadianBurnedKcal(tdee, minutesSinceMidnight)` is a strict partition (0 at midnight, exactly `tdee` at end of day), stays fractional, and the caller rounds (strip, card) or floors (live counter). Three surfaces read it (the stat strip, `CaloriesBurnedCard`, `CircadianScienceModal`) — don't add a fourth copy of the segment table, accumulator or phase lookup. `useCircadianBurn` is the only clock; `enabled`/`tickMs` cover the differences (the modal ticks per second only while visible; everything else per minute). The hook uses `useSyncExternalStore`, not `useState` + effect (the reading derives from `Date.now()` each render; a synchronous `setState` in an effect is an ESLint error); the snapshot is **quantised to `tickMs`** (an unquantised clock re-renders forever). The Cyrillic `е` in `earlySlеep` is a typo frozen into `BLOCK_FRACTIONS` and the `progress.circadianPhase.*` / `progress.circadianModal.*` keys in every locale; renaming it in one place silently loses the rate and label.
- **Today's step count reads through `hooks/useDailySteps.ts`, which must use `observeWithColumns(['value'])`.** The platform sync upserts ONE `daily_steps` row per day, so later readings are in-place updates (`observe()` emits only on membership change — same trap as `useNotes`). The query filters `deleted_at`, and because `getDecrypted()` is async the hook gates each publish on an emission token so a slow earlier decryption can't land after a newer one.
- **Food search source**: configurable via `FOOD_SEARCH_SOURCE_SETTING_TYPE` (Open Food Facts, USDA, Musclog barcode DB, local-only).
- **OCR before AI**: when enabled (`USE_OCR_BEFORE_AI_SETTING_TYPE`), images are OCR'd locally before being sent to the LLM, reducing data sent externally.

## QA (locale-aware numbers)

After changing number formatting, manually verify with app language **de** or **pt-BR**: food/daily summary, profile stats, nutrition check-in, workout history/volume, progress charts, and the Android nutrition widget show locale-appropriate decimal and grouping separators.

## Progression sessions and weekly target periods

- **Schema**: additive migration v31 (exercise rule snapshots); v32 (prescription/effort metadata, target modes, dated nutrition target periods, weekly review records); v33 (`workout_log_exercises.superset_slot_id` — split replacements inherit the original slot ID, and superset ordering groups by that slot, falling back to the log block ID, never the movement catalogue ID); v35 (`exercise_user_settings.rep_ceiling`); v37 (`nutrition_health_ownership`, permanent ownership and ignored-original facts). `database/weeklyReviewSchema.ts` owns the new table definitions shared by migration and schema. Preserve every table and optional column in backups/restores.
- **`WorkoutProgressionService.projectTemplate`** is the shared read-only recommendation projection for preview, Next time and Weekly Review. Start targets are established once. Explicit rule history is scoped to movement + stable template-exercise identity; saving unchanged exercises reuses their rows. Active-session rule copies never follow template edits. Warm-ups are excluded and incomplete prescribed sets can't imply full success.
- **Every deload snaps DOWN to a load that is really lighter, through `loadBelow` (`utils/setProgression.ts`).** Rounding a 10% cut to the nearest loadable weight sends a 10 kg dumbbell's 9 kg back to 10 kg, so the "deload" changed nothing. `loadBelow(current, target, step)` walks down one step at a time until the load is at or under the target AND strictly below current, returning current when nothing lighter exists (an empty bar, an unloaded exercise) — the caller then holds and must not claim a deload. `deloadedLoad` (automatic, rep-range rules, a lighter week), the linear rule's `deloadPercent` and a lowered percentage training max all use it. Never call `step.roundLoad` directly on a deload target.
- **Unloaded bodyweight work only ever adds reps, up to a per-exercise ceiling.** `computeStartTarget` takes `repCeiling` for a bodyweight exercise whose last session had no external load; it climbs one rep a session past the plan's range and, at the ceiling, holds with a `{ kind: 'ceiling' }` explanation (add a vest or belt, a harder variation, or slow the tempo) — it never invents external kilograms. Once external load is logged, it progresses like any other. The ceiling is `exercise_user_settings.rep_ceiling`, picked from `BODYWEIGHT_REP_CEILING_OPTIONS` (10–50, never typed). With no pick the default depends on the movement (`defaultRepCeilingFor(loadMultiplier)`): 15 for anything moving at least 85% of body mass (pull-ups, chin-ups, dips, muscle-ups, glute-ham raises, jumps), else 25 — no name lists. An explicit pick is stored verbatim even when it equals today's default (`storedRepCeiling`). Non-React code reads it via `ExerciseUserSettingsService.getResolvers(gym).repCeilingFor(exercise)`; the hook's `get(id).repCeiling` is the pick only (`undefined` when none) and the caller supplies the default.
- **The percentage rule's training max goes down as well as up.** After `failuresBeforeReset` (default 2) sessions in a row missing reps at the same training max, it is cut by `resetPercent` (default 10%) — the 5/3/1 reset — and the misses that caused it can't trigger another (the streak counts only sessions at the current max). Both fields are optional in stored JSON: `FIELDS_ADDED_LATER` fills defaults for older rules, while an out-of-range value makes the whole rule untrusted. Never re-estimate the training max from recent sets (submaximal sets ratchet it down).
- **A session that doesn't advance a rule hands its state on only while it still belongs to that rule** (`carriedSessionState`: same percentage method, starting training max and reset marker). Otherwise a changed starting max would be hidden from `resolveTrainingMax`, which detects the change by comparing the rule with the one the last session ran under.
- **Progression rule data and rule planning are two modules**: `utils/progressionRuleSchema.ts` owns the stored shape (types, bounds, defaults, `parse*`/`serialize*`); `utils/progressionRules.ts` owns the planning arithmetic (`planRuleChanges`, `resolveTrainingMax`, `counterpartAt`). UI and persistence import from the schema module; only the progression service and `trainingWeekOverride` need the planner. The weekly-progression integration tests are split the same way (`weeklyReview`, `weeklyProgressionSession`, `weeklyReviewDecision`) over `weeklyProgressionHarness.ts`/`weeklyProgressionMocks.ts` — import the mocks module first in any new suite (it registers module mocks before services load).
- **`applyStartProgression` writes ONE plan shape, whoever decided it.** `planExercise` returns a `WeekOverridePlan` plus rule state for both a Weekly Review override and the rule/automatic projection, and a single loop writes it (prescription, adjustment, soft-deleted sets). Don't split it again. "The same set last time" is `counterpartAt` everywhere.
- **A set's rated effort is written through `writeSetEffort` (`utils/setEffort.ts`)**, never by setting `repsInReserve`/`rirRecorded` by hand: a number rates the set, `null` makes it unrated (0 placeholder, `rir_recorded = false`). `WorkoutSetUpdate.repsInReserve` is `number | null` so the past-workout editor can clear a rating; `undefined` means "leave it".
- **A projection that changed nothing is `holding`, not `suggested`.** `GuidanceState` (one definition, `utils/progressionProjection.ts`) adds `holding` ("Same as last time"); `WorkoutRecommendationList` says "the targets are last time's" once for the whole list (`progression.holdHintList`).
- **Both workout views write through `WorkoutSessionService` / `useWorkoutSessionController`; presentation must not implement progression math.** The live screen is `WorkoutTrainingView` over `components/workout/session/`: `sessionModel.ts` (pure: groups sets into blocks, numbers working sets **per exercise** — workout-wide `set_order` once labelled an exercise's first set "Set 4" — and resolves the Your rule / Suggested / Same as last time / Learning / Needs attention badge), `setDraft.ts` (pure: locale-aware draft ⇄ metric values and stepper arithmetic), `InlineExerciseCard`/`InlineSetRow`, `GuidedExerciseView` (photo hero with editable steppers), `RestTimerBar`, `ProgressionWhySheet`, `SessionTopBar`. Translation keys there are whole-key tables (`GUIDANCE_STATE_KEYS`, `PROGRESSION_METHOD_KEYS`, …), never ``t(`progression.states.${x}`)``. The rest panel's expanded state derives from the controller's `restId` (bumped on each `startRest`), not an effect; the rest bar's haptics use `hapticBeat` and don't buzz on a skipped rest. **`WorkoutService.getWorkoutWithDetails` must pass each log exercise's `progressionJson` into the enriched sets** — the session's `refresh` reloads through it, and without it every set after the first completion read as rule-free (`docs/FIXES.md`).
- **The Weekly Review screen is `app/app/(tabs)/weekly-review.tsx`, at `WEEKLY_REVIEW_ROUTE` (`constants/weeklyReviewRoute.ts`)** — every link goes through the constant. It reviews training AND nutrition, so it doesn't live under `/nutrition` (which lit up the Food tab when opened from Workouts). `app/app/nutrition/checkin-list.tsx` is a `@deprecated` `<Redirect>` kept for saved/shared links (its doc block lists the files to delete with it); queued notifications store `{ type, reviewId | reviewDay }` and `handleNotificationNavigation` builds the URL at tap time. The screen's sheets are one `ReviewPanel` union (not a flag per sheet) and its writes go through one `run()` (busy flag, error banner, reload).
- **Calorie warnings in the review never depend on whether the recommendation can be applied yet.** A change of `LARGE_CHANGE_SHARE` (15%) or more always shows a "not recommended" warning with the percentage; with the minimum-calorie floor off in Advanced settings, a recommendation below `getMinCalories` also shows a floor warning (`isBelowCalorieFloor`; the snapshot records `minimumCalories`/`minimumCaloriesDisabled`, optional for older snapshots). Until the week has enough evidence the column is labelled "Estimate" and dimmed — shown, never offered.
- **Weekly Review entry points**: `WeeklyReviewCard` (Home compact while a review is due and a one-line entry otherwise via `compact quietUntilReady`; Workouts compact; Food only while a review is due, empty or fasting — otherwise Food's way in is the summary card menu's "Weekly Review" item (`DailySummaryBottomMenu`'s `weeklyReview`, test id `weekly-review-entry`), its supporting line built by `targetModeSummary` in `components/weeklyReview/targetModeLabel.ts`). `useWeeklyReviewStatus` calls a week ready only when `weekHadActivity` finds a completed workout, food log or weigh-in in it — never from account age (restores and imports rewrite it). `WorkoutTodayRepository.upcoming` marks a scheduled workout already finished today as `completedToday`, and the review's "Next up" skips it. Fallback order: schedule → rotating plan → history; with neither, `pickRotationSuggestion` (`utils/workoutRotationSuggestion.ts`, pure) picks the workout trained in the last six weeks that has rested longest, and nothing once a workout was finished today — so the Today card and "Next up" are never a dead end.
  - "Start Workout" on a library card navigates straight into the session; only the card's heading opens the preview — **never make the whole `WorkoutCard` pressable** (on web a tap on the nested Start button also fires the card and opens the preview over the session). Guided's Complete set is `GuidedCompleteButton`, pinned between the scroll view and `RestTimerBar` by `WorkoutTrainingView` (not in the scroll, where it fell below the fold). Context cards (`WorkoutSessionInsights`) render in Inline only. The action chip row scrolls sideways with an edge fade (`hidesContentToTheRight`, color from `useSurfaceColor`); `InlineExerciseCard` publishes its surface for it. The Workouts screen has one primary action, the Today card: library `WorkoutCard`s are all `standard`.
  - Empty states and the one-time welcome are decided by `utils/weeklyReviewEmptyState.ts` (pure) from `overview().activity`, built by `WeeklyReviewService.weekActivity` — the same counts `weekHadActivity` reads, so badge and page can't disagree. A half is `data`, `weekOff` (logged before) or `first` (never logged); the welcome (`WeeklyReviewWelcome`, dismissal persisted as `weekly_review_welcome_seen`) waits for a week with activity and shows only on the latest review. A fully empty week offers no acknowledge/keep action; a half that's empty while the other has data still offers one (or the week could never be marked reviewed). `useWeeklyReviewOverview` keys its result by selection and has no `clear()` (`docs/FIXES.md`). Weekly records go through `summarizeWeeklyRecords` (`utils/weeklyRecords.ts`, one row per exercise); the nutrition sentence through `nutritionReason` (`components/weeklyReview/nutritionReason.ts`), reading the `expenditure`/`trendChangeKg` the recommendation snapshot records (optional for older snapshots).
  - Color tints use Tailwind's opacity modifier (`bg-accent-primary/10`), never the JS token name (`bg-accent-primary10` compiles to nothing; `docs/FIXES.md`).
  - Prescription JSON separates plan/recommendation from actual values and records plan/manual overrides. Missing new RIR is `rir_recorded=false`, never intentional zero; null legacy flags retain compatibility. Undo touches only unfinished sets and removes their pending weight/rep drafts while retaining effort fields. Session actions go through `useSessionExerciseActions`, whose `runSaving` reports whether the write went through — callers clear typed values or close a sheet only on `true`, and a failure in a swap's "remember this" follow-up is reported on its own. Session selections, notes, swap, warm-up actions and overview entries carry `logExerciseId`, never a catalogue ID; `exerciseId` is reserved for catalogue data/settings/units. A split swap preserves `workingSetCount` in the original prescription so a partial block can't imply full success. Pending strings and wall-clock timers survive restarts. Sensor recordings capture an immutable set/movement/device owner at start, persist the owner set ID, and attach only to that set with the actual logged reps.
- **Nutrition targets resolve through `NutritionTargetService` on every consumer.** `NutritionGoalService.updateGoal` owns all edits and mode transitions: it closes/replaces periods and updates the goal in one `commitPrepared` batch (including legacy `isDynamic` callers). Don't reintroduce a separate mode writer. Weekly approvals create dated periods effective tomorrow; daily legacy goals keep their calculator. Weekly recommendations include available empirical inputs even with a weight/date trajectory. `WeeklyReviewService.decide` batches decision and periods, deduplicates submissions and restores cached prepared models on failure through `commitPrepared`. Never acknowledge a review when opening, editing, canceling or failing a save.
- **`WeeklyReviewService.decide` takes a `WeeklyReviewDecisionInput` union**: training carries its `training` choice; a nutrition `keep` names its goal; a nutrition `apply` also carries the recommendation and accepted targets. Callers narrow before calling (the screen returns early without a goal or recommendation) rather than the service re-checking optional fields with `!`. A lifter grinding at RIR ≤ `GRINDING_AVERAGE_RIR` over at least `MIN_RATED_SETS` rated sets is offered a lighter week when a lift has stalled — a 1RM goal stalling/declining, OR any exercise that had to deload on its own that week. The "Edit recommendation" sheet is `components/weeklyReview/TargetsEditorSheet.tsx` over the pure `targetsDraft.ts`: every field parses strictly and Apply waits for all; calories stay editable (the recommendation doesn't add up exactly — fiber is set on top of the split), but the sheet shows what the macros add up to (`goalCaloriesFromMacros`, `utils/carbsConvention.ts`) and offers "Use N kcal" when they differ by more than `MACRO_CALORIE_MISMATCH_SHARE` (5%).
- **The training half of a Weekly Review is a decision, not an acknowledgement.** `WeeklyReviewService.decide({ section: 'training', training })` takes `progress | repeat | lighter` plus the evidence shown (`recommended`, `reasons`, `goals`) and stores `training_decision` and a frozen `training_json` snapshot (`utils/trainingWeekDecision.ts`; one that doesn't parse is _no decision_, never a best-effort repair). `training_reviewed_at` still means "the training half is done" (badge, cards and `ready` unchanged). The window a decision shapes runs from the moment it's made to `period.next` (a late review gets a shorter one and never overlaps the next); `decide` refuses once that has passed (for nutrition only an `apply`; keeping targets on a past review stays possible, and the screen offers only that, via `periodEnded`), and only the latest review can still decide (`overview().training.canDecide`). A user who has never finished a workout isn't asked — their training half counts as done in `overview().ready`, `useWeeklyReviewStatus` and the screen. Rules live in one pure file, `utils/weeklyTrainingRecommendation.ts` (`recommendTrainingWeek`, `goalVerdicts`, `consistencySuggestion`, `lighterSetCount`) — change thresholds there. Fitness goals feed it through `ExerciseGoalService.load1rmGoalInputs` / `project1rmGoal` / `evaluate1rmGoal`, the same code `useExerciseGoalProgress` uses; the review passes `excludeWindows` so deliberately light weeks never read as "declining". The consistency goal is only ever _offered_ a change (two weeks in a row). **Don't add the `weekly_reviews` training columns to `weeklyReviewTables`**: v32's `createTable` is built from that array, so a later column there is created by v32 and then fails v34's `addColumns` on upgrade — they live in `weeklyReviewTrainingColumns` and `weeklyReviewSchemaTables` is the current shape.
- **A chosen repeat or lighter week is applied by `WorkoutProgressionService.applyStartProgression`, and is the one thing that ignores `workout_autonomy = manual`** (manual means no changes _on the app's own_; this is the user's explicit choice). `TrainingWeekRepository.activeTrainingWeek()` (a leaf module, since the review service already imports the progression service) answers which decision is live; `planWeekOverride` (`utils/trainingWeekOverride.ts`, pure) does the arithmetic. _Repeat_ starts each set from the same-position set of the last session. _Lighter_ applies `deloadedLoad` to last session's load, keeps the plan's reps, keeps `lighterSetCount` of the working sets (trailing ones soft-deleted in the SAME batch with `prepareSoftDelete`; warm-ups untouched) and sets planned RIR to `LIGHTER_WEEK_TARGET_RIR` so between-sets re-targeting doesn't push the load back up. Both record a `StartSetAdjustment` with `source: 'weekly_review'` (the explanation credits the review; undo restores the plan) and don't advance the rule's remembered state (`carriedSessionState`). Training done inside a lighter-week window is excluded from `WorkoutProgressionService.history` and goal projections (else the week after a deload "progresses" from light loads). The next-up target list is hidden once a repeat/lighter week is chosen.
- Weekly periods use seven completed local calendar days before the selected weekday, with latest-due behavior. Retain reviewed history across goal changes and old check-in route/notification links. Notification reminders are opt-in, deduplicated and don't immediately repeat. Training remains available without a nutrition goal and with intuitive eating.
- **Seeded interactions**: `playwright.progression.config.ts` targets the development-only `/app/test/progression-flow` fixture and real database/services. Run `npx playwright test --config playwright.progression.config.ts` against the local Expo server (`MUSCLOG_TEST_URL` overrides the default URL). Production test routes stay guarded.
- **A Weekly Review can be applied once the reviewed week has food on `MIN_WEEKLY_FOOD_DAYS` (5) of its 7 days** (flagged fasts count, via `getNutritionDayCoverage`) **and `MIN_WEEKLY_WEIGHT_ENTRIES` (3) weigh-ins** — stricter than the 30-day history's `MIN_WEIGHT_ENTRIES` (2) because two noisy readings can fake a trend that swings calories; the `weeklyReview.collectData` sentence interpolates both constants. The week has already closed, so logging TODAY can't unlock it: `overview().gaps` names the earliest day missing food and the latest missing a weigh-in, and the review opens the food diary (`/app/nutrition/food?date=yyyy-MM-dd`, parsed by `localCalendarDayFromParam`) or `AddUserMetricEntryModal` (`initialDate`) ON that day. Don't point those buttons at today's screens.
- **Discarding a live workout goes through `useWorkoutSessionController().discard`**, which deletes the log and then clears the stored active workout, the rest end time and its scheduled reminder, and persisted drafts (deleting only the log left Home offering to resume it and a "rest over" alert could fire). Finishing navigates once, from the `completedAt` effect. The controller's one-second clock runs only while a rest is counting down.
- **The TDEE the app shows and the daily dynamic targets come from `getLatestHistoricalNutritionWindow`** (`utils/historicalNutritionParams.ts`), used by `useEmpiricalTDEE` and `resolveDailyMacros`/`resolveDynamicGoalPreview`. It always leaves unlogged days out of the intake average (`excludeUnloggedDays`: a forgotten day reads as an average one), while flagged fasts stay in as 0 kcal via `getRangeNutrients`' `effectiveDayCount`. When the 30 days ending today have too little data, it measures the 30 days ending the day after the last food log (or at the last weigh-in, when weighing stopped first) and marks the result `fromPastWindow`; every surface that shows that TDEE renders `PastTdeeWindowNote` with its dates (`CaloriesBurnedCard`, `NutritionGoalsBody` via `ResolvedMacros.pastEmpiricalWindow`). Never let the window span the gap itself (the trend interpolates across it and spreads tissue change over months without food). The Weekly Review recommendation (`resolveWeeklyRecommendation`) never falls back — it judges the reviewed week. Comeback nutrition uses the formula for its first weeks. Pinned by `utils/__tests__/{historicalNutritionParams,dynamicNutritionTarget}.test.ts`.
- Empirical TDEE separates intake coverage (`historicalTotalDays` / `totalDays`) from elapsed weight-anchor days (`historicalWeightChangeDays` / `weightChangeDays`). Food belongs to the half-open weight-anchor interval; a fasting or missing-log denominator must never divide the entire tissue-energy change. `useWeeklyReviewOverview` refreshes evidence on focus and discards obsolete requests (review-status changes alone can't detect new food or weights).
